Algebraic Relations among Special Gamma Values and the Chowla–Selberg Phenomenon over Function Fields Thanks: The author is supported by MOST grant 109-2115-M-007-017-MY5 and the National Center for Theoretical Sciences.
Fu-Tsun Wei

Abstract
. The aim of this paper is to determine all algebraic relations among various special gamma values over function fields, and prove a Chowla–Selberg-type formula for quasi-periods of CM abelian t t -modules. Our results are based on the intrinsic relations between gamma values in question and periods of CM dual t t -motives, which are interpreted in terms of their “distributions”. This also enables us to derive an analogue of the Deligne–Gross period conjecture for CM Hodge–Pink structures.



2000 Mathematics Subject Classification
Primary 11J93; Secondary 11G09

1.
Introduction

The main goal of this paper is to determine the algebraic relations among special gamma values over function fields, and derive an analogue of the Chowla–Selberg formula for quasi-periods of CM abelian
t
-modules.
In the classical case, the gamma function was first introduced by Euler in order to solve the interpolation problem of factorials.
This transcendental function shows up in many areas and plays an essential role since then.
The celebrated Chowla–Selberg formula (see
[
CS67
]
) expresses the periods of CM elliptic curves, up to algebraic multiples, in terms of particular twisted products of gamma values at fractions.
This led to extensive studies on the connection between special gamma values in question and periods of CM abelian varieties
(see
[
Gr78
]
,
[
And82
]
,
[
Co93
]
,
[
MR04
]
,
[
Yang10
]
,
[
BM16
]
, and
[
Fr17
]
, etc.).
We refer the reader to
[
Gr21
]
for a comprehensive historical review.
On the other hand, gamma functions on the function field side similarly arose from
Carlitz factorials

[
Car35
]
, and then grew into three “interpolations” in the work of Goss
[
Go88
]
and Thakur
[
T91
]
.
After establishing natural functional equations of these gamma functions, Thakur proposed a recipe/conjecture on the
Chowla–Selberg phenomenon
(see
[
T91
, 7.12]
) for the periods of CM Drinfeld modules and examined several cases.
In this paper, we shall prove Thakur’s conjecture, and clarify the intrinsic relations between special values of various gamma functions and “abelian CM periods” in positive characteristic.

1.1.
Special gamma values and the algebraic independence

Let
p
be a prime number and
q
be a power of
p
.
Let
A\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[\theta]
, the polynomial ring with one variable
\theta
over a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with
q
elements, and
k\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\theta)
, the fraction field of
A
.
Denote by
k_{\infty}
the completion of
k
with respect to the absolute value
|\cdot|_{\infty}
associated to the infinite place of
k
normalized so that
|\theta|_{\infty}=q
, and
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
the completion of a chosen algebraic closure of
k_{\infty}
.
Let
\bar{k}
be the algebraic closure of
k
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Put
A_{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{a\in A\mid a\text{ is monic}\}
.

There are three analogues of the gamma function in the function field case:

•

Arithmetic gamma function
(see Goss
[
Go80
, App.]
): Let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the ring of
p
-adic integers. For
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Pi_{\operatorname{ari}}(y-1),\quad\text{ where }\quad\Pi_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{j}}}{\theta^{q^{i}}})\right)^{y_{i}}.

Here
y_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
are the unique integers with
0\leq y_{i}<q
such that

y=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}y_{i}q^{i}
.

•

Geometric gamma function
(see Thakur
[
T91
]
): For
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
,

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{x}\cdot\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\quad\text{ where }\quad\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{a\in A_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right)^{-1}.

•

Two-variable gamma function
(see Goss
[
Go88
]
): For
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

\Gamma(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{x}\cdot\frac{\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y-1)}{\Pi_{\operatorname{ari}}(y-1)}\quad\text{ where }\quad\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in A_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}\\
\deg a=i\end{subarray}}\Big(1+\frac{x}{a}\Big)^{-y_{i}}.

Here
y_{i}
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
are chosen as in the arithmetic case.

The two-variable gamma function defined above actually follows Thakur’s modification in
[
T91
, the
\Gamma_{2}
in Definition 8.1.1]
.
We refer the reader to
Remark
5.4.1
for its comparison with Goss’ original definition.
The two-variable gamma function can be seen as an extension of both the arithmetic and geometric ones.
In particular, one has that

\Gamma(x,1-\frac{1}{q-1})\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q-1})=\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}).

The natural functional equations of these gamma functions, e.g. the corresponding reflection and multiplication formulas, are well-established in
[
Go88
]
and
[
T91
]
.
We recall in Proposition
5.4.2
the needed monomial relations, up to
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiples, among the gamma values at
x\in k\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{Q}\cap\mathbb{Z}_{p}
induced by the functional equations.
The first theorem of this paper is the following analogue of the Lang–Rohrlich conjecture:

Theorem 1.1.1

(Theorem
5.4.7
and Corollary
5.4.8
)
.

Given
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, we have that

\displaystyle\ \trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\Gamma(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ 1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(A/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}=1
if
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
and
0
otherwise.
Consequently, all algebraic relations among gamma values
\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
,
\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
,
\Gamma(x,y)
for
x\in k\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
are explained by the monomial relations listed in Proposition
5.4.2
.

Remark 1.1.2
.

(1) For
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
, we denote by
c_{1}\sim c_{2}
if
c_{1}/c_{2}\in\bar{k}^{\times}
.
From the relation in
[
T91
, Theorem 1.4]
:

\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{a}{q-1})\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{a}{q-1}},\quad\forall a\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ with }0\leq a<q-1,

where
\tilde{\pi}
is the
Carlitz fundamental period
(see Remark
3.1.8
),
the monomial relations in Proposition
5.4.2
imply that Theorem
1.1.1
coincides with the geometric case in
[
ABP04
, Corollary 1.2.2]
(resp. the arithmetic case in
[
CPTY10
, Corollary 3.3.3]
) when taking
\ell=1
(resp.
\mathfrak{n}=1
), see Remark
5.4.9
(1) and (2).

(2) The transcendence theory of arithmetic and geometric gamma values has been fully developed separately
(see
[
T91
]
,
[
T96
]
,
[
All96
]
,
[
MY97
]
, and
[
CPTY10
]
for arithmetic gamma values, and see
[
Si97
]
,
[
BP02
]
,
[
ABP04
]
for the geometric ones).
In contrast, there seems to have been no further progress on two-variable gamma values after Goss and Thakur.
Theorem
1.1.1
not only fills in this missing part, but also clarifies the algebraic independence of these three different types of gamma values.
In particular, the algebraic relations among arithmetic and geometric gamma values come solely from their relations with
\tilde{\pi}
, see Remark
5.4.9
(3).

The proof of Theorem
1.1.1
is based upon the “period interpretation” of the gamma values in question, which is illustrated via the “period distribution” introduced in the next subsection.

1.2.
The period distribution and gamma distributions

Let
\matheur{A}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
be the polynomial ring over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with another variable
t
(transcendental over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
), and
\matheur{k}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
the fraction field of
\matheur{A}
.
Let
v_{\theta}:\matheur{k}\cong k
be the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra isomorphism sending
t
to
\theta
.
We regard
\matheur{k}
as the function field of the projective
t
-line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and
v_{\theta}
corresponds to the evaluation map at the point
\theta\in\mathbb{P}^{1}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
.
For
f\in\matheur{k}
, we denote
f(\theta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=v_{\theta}(f)\in k
.

Let
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
be a separable closure of
\matheur{k}
, and put
\matheur{G}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}/\matheur{k})
.
Fix an embedding
\nu:\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
extending from
v_{\theta}
.
With respect to
\nu
, the Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(k^{\mathrm{sep}}_{\infty}/k_{\infty})
, where
k^{\mathrm{sep}}_{\infty}
is the separable closure of
k_{\infty}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, corresponds to a subgroup of
\matheur{G}
denoted by
\matheur{G}_{\infty}
.
The space
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
of
Stickelberger functions
consists of all locally constant
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions
\varphi
on
\matheur{G}
satisfying (see Definition
3.2.1
):

(1)

For
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}\in\matheur{G}
and
\varrho_{\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}
, we have

\varphi\big(\varrho_{1}\cdot(\varrho_{2}\varrho_{\infty}\varrho_{2}^{-1}\varrho_{\infty}^{-1})\big)=\varphi(\varrho_{1});

(2)

Let
d\varrho_{\infty}
be the Haar measure on
\matheur{G}_{\infty}
normalized so that
\text{vol}(\matheur{G}_{\infty},d\varrho_{\infty})=1
.
Then

\int_{\matheur{G}_{\infty}}\varphi(\varrho_{1}\varrho_{\infty})d\varrho_{\infty}=\int_{\matheur{G}_{\infty}}\varphi(\varrho_{2}\varrho_{\infty})d\varrho_{\infty}\quad\forall\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}\in\matheur{G}.

The connections between particular Stickelberger functions and Dirichlet
L
-values are discussed in Lemma
4.2.6
.
In particular,
let
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
be a
CM field over
\matheur{k}
(recalled in Section
2.3
) which is fixed by the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
and
\Xi
a
generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
(see Definition
2.3.1
).
We define the Stickelberger function
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}
associated to
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
in (
3.2.8
), and show that
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
is spanned by
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}
for all CM fields
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
fixed by
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
and generalized CM types
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
(see Lemma
3.2.9
).

In
[
BCPW22
, Sec. 5]
, an analogue of Shimura’s period symbols was introduced arising from the “CM periods” through the theory of Anderson’s dual
t
-motives.
The fundamental properties of these symbols, which were established in
[
BCPW22
, Thm. 5.3.2]
,
enable us to compare CM periods associated to different CM fields.
The needed results are recalled in Section
3.1
.
We then introduce an analogue of Anderson’s
period distribution
in the following.

Theorem 1.2.1

(Theorem
3.3.1
)
.

Fix an embedding
\nu:\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
extending from the evaluation map
v_{\theta}
.
There exists a unique
\mathbb{Q}
-linear map
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}:\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
satisfying that for every CM field
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
fixed by
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
and every generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
,

\mathscr{P}_{\nu}(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi})=\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{\nu},\Xi),

where
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{\nu},\Xi)
is the period symbol given in Definition
3.1.7
, and
\xi_{\nu}
corresponds to the embedding
\nu\big|_{\matheur{K}}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
under the bijection in Remark
2.3.3
.

On the other hand, the gamma distributions are defined by taking values at “fractions”: for
x\in k
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, put
\{x\}\in k
(resp.
\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
) with
|\{x\}|_{\infty}<1
(resp.
0\leq\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}<1
) and
x-\{x\}\in A
(resp.
y-\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}\in\mathbb{Z}
). Then
\{\cdot\}
and
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}
can be viewed as functions on
k/A
and
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
, respectively.
For
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
, we set

\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\Gamma(\{x\},1-\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}),&\text{ if $0\neq x\in k/A$,}\\
\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}})^{-1},&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Then
\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
, and the
two-variable gamma distribution

\hat{\Gamma}:k/A\times\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is defined by

\hat{\Gamma}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},\quad\forall(x,y)\in k/A\times\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}.

Moreover, the
geometric (resp. arithmetic) gamma distribution

\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}:k/A\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
(resp.
\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
) is

\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\hat{\Gamma}(x,\frac{1}{1-q}),\quad\forall x\in k/A\quad(\text{resp.}\quad\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\hat{\Gamma}(0,y)^{-1},\quad\forall y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}).

The main bridge between the above gamma distributions and the period distribution in Theorem
1.2.1
is built from the so-called
Stickelberger distributions
(in the sense of
[
KL81
, Chap. 1, §3]
) associated to the corresponding “diamond brackets” introduced by Thakur
[
T91
]
.
Intuitively,
we identify
k/A
(resp.
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
) with the “Carlitz torsions” (resp. roots of unity) in
\bar{k}
, respectively, and equip with a
\matheur{G}
-action
\star
via the Artin map (see (
3.4.6
) and (
4.2.1
)).
For each
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
, we define
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\operatorname{St}(x,y):\matheur{G}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
respectively by (see Definition
4.2.2
):

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\varrho\star x\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}}-\frac{1}{q-1},\quad\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle-\varrho\star y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}

\operatorname{St}(x,y)(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\varrho\star x,-\varrho\star y\rangle-\langle-\varrho\star y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}},\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G},

where
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}}
,
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}
, and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
are the
geometric, arithmetic, and two-variable diamond brackets
, respectively (recalled in Section
4.1
).
It is seen that
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
, and
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
all belong to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
,
where
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
is the Galois group of the compositum
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
of all
Carlitz cyclotomic function fields
and constant field extensions over
\matheur{k}
(see Section
3.4
), and
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
consists of all the functions in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
factoring through
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
.
Moreover, the diamond bracket relations (recalled in Section
4.1
) assure the “distribution” properties of
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}:k/A\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
, and
\operatorname{St}:(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
(see Remark
4.2.3
).
Generalizing the construction of the “soliton dual
t
-motives” in
[
ABP04
, 6.4.2]
, we derive the following analogue of Deligne’s theorem (stated in
[
And82
, Theorem 4.7]
):

Theorem 1.2.2

(Theorem
5.4.5
)
.

Let
\nu_{1}:\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be the specific embedding defined in (
5.1.1
), and
\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
be the restriction of the period distribution to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
with respect to
\nu_{1}
.
Then

\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}=\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}},\quad\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}=\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{\Gamma}=\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}.

The strategy of the proof of Theorem
1.1.1
is briefly outlined as follows:
Given
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
be the
(\mathfrak{n},\ell)
-th cyclotomic function field
(given above (
3.4.5
)) and
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
the Galois group of
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
over
\matheur{k}
.
Denote by
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
the space consisting of the functions in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
factoring through
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
Let
\bar{k}\big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}))\big)
be the field generated by arbitrary representatives of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi)\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
.
The main theorem of
[
BCPW22
]
on Shimura’s conjecture shows that (see Theorem
3.3.5
)

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}))\big)=1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}.

On the other hand, we prove below that the Stickelberger distributions
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}
, and
\operatorname{St}
are actually
universal
(see Corollary
4.3.3
,
4.3.7
, and
4.3.13
).
Combining with Theorem
1.2.2
, we obtain that the field
\bar{k}\big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}))\big)
is algebraic over

\displaystyle\ \bar{k}\Big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}(x,y)\big)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A,\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bar{k}\Big(\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A,\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\Gamma(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big).

The final assertion of Theorem
1.1.1
thereby will follow from the compatibility between the monomial relations in Proposition
5.4.2
and the diamond bracket relations in Lemma
4.1.2
,
4.1.4
, Proposition
4.1.7
, and Remark
4.1.8
.

Theorem
1.2.2
shows on one hand that all types of gamma values at fractions come from periods of CM dual
t
-motives, and, on the other hand, enables us to write “abelian CM periods” in terms of products of the gamma values in question.
Indeed, the universality of the Stickelberger distributions indicates that every Stickelberger function
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
can be written as a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear combination of
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
for
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
.
In particular, let
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
be an
imaginary
field over
\matheur{k}
(i.e. the infinite place of
\matheur{k}
does not split in
\matheur{K}
) and
\varphi{K}\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
be the characteristic function of the Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{K})
.
Writing

(1.2.3)

\varphi{K}=\sum_{x\in k/A}\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}}m_{x,y}\operatorname{St}(x,y),\quad\text{ where }m_{x,y}\in\mathbb{Q}\text{ with }m_{x,y}=0\text{ for almost all }x,y,

we prove the following recipe/conjecture of Thakur from Theorem
1.2.2
:

Theorem 1.2.4

(Theorem
6.2.3
)
.

Keep the notation from above.
For every nonzero period
\lambda

of a Drinfield
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
over
\bar{k}
with CM by
O{K}
, where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
, we have

\lambda\sim\prod_{x\in k/A}\prod_{y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)^{m_{x,y}}.

Remark 1.2.5
.

The above recipe is actually generalized to quasi-periods of CM abelian
t
-modules with arbitrary CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
, where
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
, see Theorem
6.1.4
and
6.2.2
.

Our next task is to carry out the decomposition (
1.2.3
) in an explicit form.
This is achieved in Proposition
6.3.5
from the arithmetic property of the Stickelberger functions
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
for
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
regarded as “evaluators” of Dirichlet
L
-values in Lemma
4.2.6
.
Therefore Theorem
1.2.2
provides a definite expression for every “abelian CM period” in terms of a monomial product of special gamma values,
which results in an analogue of the Chowla–Selberg formula presented in the next subsection.

1.3.
The Chowla–Selberg formula for abelian
t
-modules

In the theory of function field arithmetic,

abelian
t
-modules
, introduced by Anderson
[
And86
]
, play the analogous rule of commutative algebraic groups in the classical settings.
The
quasi-periods
of abelian
t
-modules (introduced by Gekeler
[
Ge89
]
and Yu
[
Yu90
]
for Drinfeld modules, and generalized to arbitrary abelian
t
-modules by Brownawell-Papanikolas
[
BP02
]
) often show up as “special values” in various circumstances (see
[
Car35
]
,
[
Yu91
]
,
[
CY07
]
,
[
NP21
]
,
[
GN21
]
,
[
Ge88
]
,
[
C12a
]
,
[
C12b
]
,
[
CG22
]
, etc.).
From the comparisons between the “de Rham pairings” established in
[
BCPW22
, Prop. 8.3.4]
(recalled in (
6.1.2
)), we may naturally transform the study of quasi-periods of a given abelian
t
-module
E_{\rho}
into the analysis of periods of the associated
Hartl–Juschka dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}(\rho)
(see (
6.1.1
)).
In particular, when
E_{\rho}
is a

CM abelian
t
-module
over
\bar{k}
(see after (
6.1.2
)), its quasi-periods can be expressed in terms of our period symbols (cf.
[
BCPW22
, Thm. 8.4.1 and 8.5.2]
).

Now, let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
Suppose further that
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
is the
(\mathfrak{n},\ell)
-th cyclotomic function field.
Let
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
be the Stickelberger function associated to
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
given in (
3.2.8
).
We may view
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}
as a
\mathbb{Z}
-valued function on
\matheur{G}{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})
.
For each
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, put
(\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi})(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}(\varrho\varrho_{0})
for every
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
.
Then the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is written as (see (
6.1.3
))

\sum_{\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}}\bar{k}\cdot\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}),

where
\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
is an arbitrary representative of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
.
For each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
, put
m_{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}(\varrho)
.
From the explicit expression of
\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}
in Proposition
6.3.5
for every
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, we derive the following Chowla–Selberg formula for quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
:

Theorem 1.3.1

(Theorem
6.3.7
(1))
.

Keep the notation as above.
The space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by

\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)}\right)^{\frac{m_{\varrho}}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}},\quad\text{ for }\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K},

where
\wt(\Xi)
is the weight of
\Xi
given in (
2.3.2
),
\matheur{K}^{+}
is the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
, and
n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)
is a particular rational number defined in (
6.3.4
).

In particular, let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
. When
\wt(\Xi)=1
, i.e.
\Xi
is a CM type of
\matheur{K}
,
there exist exactly
\varrho_{1},...,\varrho_{d}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
, such that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}(\varrho)=1
if
\varrho=\varrho_{i}
for some
i
with
1\leq i\leq d
and
0
otherwise.
We then obtain the Chowla–Selberg formula for
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
, where
\Lambda_{\rho}
is the
period lattice of
E_{\rho}
(see Section
6.2
):

Theorem 1.3.2

(Theorem
6.3.7
(2))
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
, where
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
, and put
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
.
Take
\varrho_{1},...,\varrho_{d}\in\matheur{G}{K}
as above, and let
\nu_{\varrho_{j}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{1}\circ\varrho_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq d
.
There exist an ideal
\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
of
O{K}
(the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
) and a suitable
\bar{k}
-linear isomorphism
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
so that the
image of every period vector
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
has the form
(\nu_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)\lambda_{1},...,\nu_{\varrho_{d}}(\alpha)\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
,
where
\alpha\in\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
and for
1\leq j\leq d
,

\lambda_{j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{d}\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\frac{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho_{j^{\prime}}\varrho_{j}^{-1},a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}.

Furthermore, applying Theorem
1.3.1
and
1.3.2
to the case of Drinfeld modules, we get:

Theorem 1.3.3

(Theorem
6.3.7
(3))
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
r
over
\bar{k}
which has CM by
O{K}
, where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in an imaginary field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
with
[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]=r
.
Suppose
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Then

\lambda^{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sim\tilde{\pi}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\text{\rm id}_{\matheur{K}},a,i)}\quad\text{ for every nonzero period }\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}.

Moreover,

\varpi{K}^{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\frac{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}},\quad\text{ for }\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K},

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
and
form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
.

Remark 1.3.4
.

(1) When the imaginary field
\matheur{K}
in Theorem
1.3.3
is either a constant field extension of
\matheur{k}
or the
t
-th Carlitz cyclotomic function field, we verify in Example
6.3.9
and
6.3.10
that the first statement of Theorem
1.3.3
agrees with Thakur’s formula in
[
T91
, Theorem 1.6]
and
[
T04
, Theorem 4.11.2]
, respectively,
and the second statement matches with
[
CPTY10
, Theorem 3.3.2]
and
[
T04
, Theorem 6.4.7 (2)]
, respectively.

(2) Suppose the imaginary field
\matheur{K}
in Theorem
1.3.3
is quadratic over
\matheur{k}
.
We may take
\ell=2
and
\mathfrak{n}=\mathfrak{d}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
to be the generator of the discriminant ideal of
O{K}/\matheur{A}
.
Let

\boldsymbol{\chi}:\matheur{G}{K}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
be the quadratic character associated to
\matheur{K}/\matheur{k}
.
Regarding
\boldsymbol{\chi}
as a quadratic character on
(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})
via the Artin map (
3.4.5
), we calculate in Example
6.3.11
that

n_{\mathfrak{d}}(\varrho,a,i)=\frac{w{K}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{d})}{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}\cdot\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $\varrho=\text{id}{K}$,}\\
-1,&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})
is the ideal class group of
O{K}
and
w{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\#(O{K})^{\times}/\#(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times})
.
Thus, if we put

\omega_{\matheur{K}}^{\pm}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{\tilde{\pi}}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{d}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\pm\frac{w{K}\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{d})}{2\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}},

then Theorem
1.3.3
says that

\lambda\sim\varpi{K}^{+},\quad\text{ for every nonzero period $\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}$.}

Moreover,
\{\varpi{K}^{+},\varpi{K}^{-}\}
are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
and form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of the quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
.

(3) After suitable “normalization” of the gamma values in (
6.4.4
), we derive a Lerch-type formula for the derivative of the Dirichlet
L
-function at
s=0
(see Theorem
6.4.5
).
Also, applying the Kronecker limit formula established in
[
Wei20
]
, we then obtain an “analytic version” of the Chowla–Selberg formula in (
6.4.7
).

1.4.
The Deligne–Gross conjecture for CM Hodge–Pink structures

Motivated by his geometric proof of the Chowla–Selberg formula, Gross
[
Gr78
, p. 205]
proposed a conjecture (with an explicit formulation suggested by Deligne) which asserts that the periods of a “geometric” Hodge structure with multiplication by an abelian number field can be expressed, up to
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{\times}
-multiples, as products of gamma values at fractions, with exponents determined by the Hodge decomposition.
This conjecture still remains wide open, except for certain verified cases (cf.
[
Gr78
, p. 205–207]
,
[
MR04
]
, and
[
Fr17
]
).
Having sufficient tools at hands on the function field side, we are now able to prove an analogue of the Deligne–Gross period conjecture in the context of dual
t
-motives.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
, and
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})=(H{M},W_{\bullet}H{M},\mathfrak{q}{M})
the
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure of
\matheur{M}
(see Example
7.1.7
).
Given a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
, suppose it has
full-CM
by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
(see (
7.2.1
)).
Assume further that
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
be the
Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
(see Definition
7.2.2
).
We verify in Lemma
7.2.3
that
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
In particular, the associated Stickelberger function
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}
lies in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
.
As mentioned in the end of Section
1.2
, the universality of the Stickelberger distribution ensures that there exists a function
\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}:(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A)\times(\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
such that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}=\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A}\ \sum_{y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}}\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)\cdot\operatorname{St}(x,y).

On the other hand, for each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
, let
\nu_{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{1}\circ\varrho:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
As
\matheur{H}
has full-CM by
\matheur{K}
, there exists a unique one dimensional eigen-subspace of
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H^{\prime}
whose eigenvalue for each
\alpha\in\matheur{K}
is
\nu_{\varrho}(\alpha)
.
Identifying
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
with a subspace of the de Rham module
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
via the
period isomorphism
(see (
3.1.2
)), take a nonzero differential
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
which lies in the eigen-subspace of
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
with respect to
\nu_{\varrho}
.
Our analogue of the Deligne–Gross period conjecture is presented in the following.

Theorem 1.4.1

(Theorem
7.3.1
)
.

Keep the notation from above.
If
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is algebraic (i.e.
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
,
then for every
\gamma
in
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
so that the “period integral”
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is nonzero, we have that

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\sim\prod_{x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A}\ \prod_{y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}}\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)^{\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(\varrho^{-1}\star x,\varrho^{-1}\star y)}.

The main step in the proof of Theorem
1.4.1
is the appeal to the function field analogue of the Hodge conjecture established by Pink, Hartl, and Juschka in
[
Pink97
]
,
[
HP04
]
,
[
Jus10
]
, and
[
HJ20
]
, to obtain a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
together with an
essentially surjective
morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
(i.e.
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is isogenous to
f(\matheur{M})
) so that
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
is isomorphic to
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
via
f
(see Theorem
7.1.8
).
As
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
has full-CM by
\matheur{K}
, we may take
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
to be a CM dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})
without loss of generality (see Proposition
7.2.6
and Remark
7.2.7
), and the morphism
f
becomes defined over
\bar{k}
by Lemma
2.2.6
.
This enables us to see that

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}^{\varrho^{-1}}}\big),

and so the result follows from Theorem
1.2.2
.

Remark 1.4.2
.

(1) The proof of Theorem
1.4.1
ensures in particular that a nonzero algebraic differential
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
exists for each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
(see Remark
7.3.2
).

(2) Although the choice of the function
\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
may not be unique, we can follow the approach of our Chowla–Selberg formula to take an explicit one (see Remark
7.3.3
).
As a result, analogues of the Gross formulas
[
Gr78
, (4), (5) in p. 194]
are shown in Example
7.3.4
.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Basic settings

Given a finite field
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with
q
elements,
let
A\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[\theta]
, the polynomial ring with one variable
\theta
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
, and
k\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(\theta)
, the field of fractions of
A
.
Let
|\cdot|_{\infty}
be the absolute value on
k
with respect to the infinite place
\infty
of
k
which is normalized so that
|\theta|_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=q
.
We may identify the completion of
k
with respect to
|\cdot|_{\infty}
with
k_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}={\mathbb{F}_{q}(\!(1/\theta)\!)}
.
Let
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be the completion of a fixed algebraic closure
\bar{k}_{\infty}
of
k_{\infty}
.
We still denote by
|\cdot|_{\infty}
the extended absolute value on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Let
\bar{k}
be the algebraic closure of
k
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,
and let

k^{\text{sep}}
(resp.
k_{\infty}^{\text{sep}}
) be the separable closure of
k
(resp.
k_{\infty}
) in
\bar{k}
(resp.
\bar{k}_{\infty}
).
In this paper, elements in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
are regarded as scalars.

Let
\matheur{A}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
, the polynomial ring with another variable
t
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
(where
t
is transcendental over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
), and
\matheur{k}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)
, the field of fractions of
\matheur{A}
.
Here we regard
\matheur{k}
(resp.
\matheur{A}
) as the function field (resp. affine coordinate ring) of the projective (resp. affine)
t
-line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
(resp.
\mathbb{A}^{1}
) over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
In particular, the evaluation map at the point
t=\theta
on the base change of
\mathbb{P}^{1}
from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
k
induces an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra isomorphism
v_{\theta}:\matheur{k}\cong k
sending
t
to
\theta
.
For each
f\in\matheur{k}
, we put
f(\theta)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=v_{\theta}(f)\in k
.
The absolute value on
\matheur{k}
induced by the one on
k
via
v_{\theta}
is denoted by
|\cdot|_{\infty}
as well if no confusion arises.
Finally, let
\matheur{A}_{+}
be the set of monic polynomials in
\matheur{A}
.
By abuse of notation, the ideal generated by
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
is also denoted by
\mathfrak{n}
.

2.2.
Dual
t
-motives and Betti modules

Let
\mathbb{K}
be an algebraically closed subfield of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
containing
\bar{k}
.
Let
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
be the non-commutative polynomial ring over
\mathbb{K}
subject to the following relations:

tc=ct,\quad t\sigma=\sigma t,\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma c=c^{1/q}\sigma,\quad\forall c\in\mathbb{K}.

We may view the (resp. twisted) polynomial ring
\mathbb{K}[t]
(resp.
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
) as a subring of
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
.

Definition 2.2.1
.

A
dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
is a left
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module
\matheur{M}
satisfying that:

(1)

\matheur{M}
is a free
\mathbb{K}[t]
-module of finite rank;

(2)

\matheur{M}
is a free left
\mathbb{K}[\sigma]
-module of finite rank;

(3)

(t-\theta)^{N}\cdot\matheur{M}\subset\sigma\matheur{M}
for a sufficiently large integer
N
.

Given two dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
, a
morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
is a
\mathbb{K}[t,\sigma]
-module homomorphism from
\matheur{M}
to
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
.

Remark 2.2.2
.

A morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
is called an
isogeny
if
f
is injective with finite dimensional cokernel over
\mathbb{K}
. In this case, we call
\matheur{M}

isogenous
to
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
.
Moreover, it is known that there exists
a\in\matheur{A}
such that
a\matheur{M}^{\prime}\subset f(\matheur{M})
(see
[
ABP04
, Theorem 4.4.7]
), whence there exists another isogeny
g:\matheur{M}^{\prime}\rightarrow\matheur{M}
satisfying that

f\circ g(m^{\prime})=a\cdot m^{\prime},\quad\forall m^{\prime}\in\matheur{M}^{\prime},\quad\text{ and }\quad g\circ f(m)=a\cdot m,\quad\forall m\in\matheur{M}.

Thus
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is isogenous to
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}
as well.

2.2.3
Uniformizability
.

Let
\mathbb{T}
be the following Tate algebra:

\mathbb{T}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\left\{\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}c_{i}t^{i}\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t]\!]\ \Bigg|\ \lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}|c_{i}|_{\infty}=0\right\}.

Let
n\in\mathbb{Z}
.
For each
f=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}c_{i}t^{i}\in\mathbb{T}
,
the
q^{n}
-power Frobenius twist of
f
is

f^{(n)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}c_{i}^{q^{n}}t^{i}.

In particular, we may associate a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]
-module structure on
\mathbb{T}
extending
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]
-multiplication so that

\sigma\cdot f=f^{(-1)},\quad\forall f\in\mathbb{T}.

Let
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
be the twisted polynomial ring over
\mathbb{T}
subject to the relation

\sigma f=f^{(-1)}\sigma
for every
f\in\mathbb{T}
.
Set
\mathbb{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
,
which is equipped with a module structure over
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
so that
\sigma
acts diagonally on both
\mathbb{T}
and
\matheur{M}
.
The
Betti module of
\matheur{M}
is

(2.2.4)

H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{M}^{\sigma}=\{m\in\mathbb{M}\mid\sigma\cdot m=m\}.

It is known that
H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
is a free
\matheur{A}
-module of rank less than or equal to
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
,
and the natural map

\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\matheur{A}}H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow\mathbb{M}

is injective
(cf.
[
BCPW22
, (2.4.1)]
).
We call
\matheur{M}

uniformizable
if the map

\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\matheur{A}}H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow\mathbb{M}

is also surjective, whence becomes a
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
-module isomorphism.
In this case, one has that
\operatorname{rank}_{\matheur{A}}H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\matheur{M})
,
and
[
HJ20
, Proposition 2.3.30]
says that

H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})\subset\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\mathbb{M}^{\dagger},

where
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
is the subring of
\mathbb{T}
consisting of all rigid analytic functions on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus\{\theta^{q^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
.

2.2.5
Push-forward
.

Let
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be two uniformizable dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{K}
.
Given a morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
,
we may extend
f
to a
\mathbb{T}[\sigma]
-module homomorphism from
\mathbb{M}
to
\mathbb{M}^{\prime}
which induces an
\matheur{A}
-module homomorphism
f_{*}:H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
, called the
push-forward of
f
.
In particular, when
f
is an isogeny, by Remark
2.2.2
one knows that

f_{*}
must be injective with

\dim_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}\left(\frac{H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})}{f_{*}\big(H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\big)}\right)=\dim_{\mathbb{K}}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}{f(\matheur{M})}\right)<\infty.

Thus
the
\matheur{k}
-linear map

\text{id}{k}\otimes f_{*}:\matheur{k}\otimes_{\matheur{A}}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow\matheur{k}\otimes_{\matheur{A}}H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
is an isomorphism.

A dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
is said to be
defined over
\mathbb{K}
if there exists a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}_{0}
over
\mathbb{K}
such that

\matheur{M}\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\matheur{M}_{0}
.
We have the following.

Lemma 2.2.6
.

Let
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be two uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
.
We put
\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{K}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}
, which become uniformizable dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Then every morphism
\tilde{f}:\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
is actually defined over
\mathbb{K}
, i.e.
\tilde{f}(\matheur{M})\subseteq\matheur{M}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
n=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
and
n^{\prime}=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
.
Take
\mathbf{m}\in\Mat_{1,n}(\matheur{M})
(resp.
\mathbf{m^{\prime}}\in\Mat_{1,n^{\prime}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
) whose entries form a
\mathbb{K}[t]
-base of
\matheur{M}
(resp.
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
).
By
[
ABP04
, Proposition 3.1.3 and Lemma 4.4.12]
there exist
\Psi\in\GL_{n}(\mathbb{T}\cap\mathbb{K}[\![t]\!])

and
\Psi^{\prime}\in\GL_{n^{\prime}}(\mathbb{T}\cap\mathbb{K}[\![t]\!])
so that
the entries of
\Psi^{-1}\mathbf{m}
(resp.
(\Psi^{\prime})^{-1}\mathbf{m^{\prime}}
) form an
\matheur{A}
-base of
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
(resp.
H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
).
Then we can find
U\in\Mat_{n,n^{\prime}}(\matheur{A})
so that

\tilde{f}_{*}\big(\Psi^{-1}\mathbf{m}\big)=U\cdot(\Psi^{\prime})^{-1}\mathbf{m^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, the push-forward
\tilde{f}_{*}
of
\tilde{f}
satisfies that

\Psi^{-1}\cdot\tilde{f}(\mathbf{m})=\tilde{f}_{*}\big(\Psi^{-1}\mathbf{m}\big).

Combining these two equalities, we obtain that

\tilde{f}(\mathbf{m})=\big(\Psi\cdot U\cdot(\Psi^{\prime})^{-1}\big)\mathbf{m^{\prime}},

whence
\Psi\cdot U\cdot(\Psi^{\prime})^{-1}\in\Mat_{n,n^{\prime}}(\mathbb{K}[\![t]\!]\cap\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t])=\Mat_{n,n^{\prime}}(\mathbb{K}[t])
.
Therefore
\tilde{f}(\matheur{M})\subseteq\matheur{M}^{\prime}
.
∎

2.2.7
Purity
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
.
We may extend the
\sigma
-action on
\matheur{M}
to
\matheur{M}(\!(1/t)\!)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\otimes_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}
by:

\sigma\cdot(f\otimes m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f^{(-1)}\otimes(\sigma m),\quad\forall f\in\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)\quad\text{ and }\quad m\in\matheur{M},

where for each Laurent series
f=\sum_{i=m}^{\infty}c_{i}t^{-i}\in\mathbb{K}(\!(1/t)\!)
,

f^{(-1)}
is the
q^{-1}
-power Frobenius twist of
f
:

f^{(-1)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=m}^{\infty}c_{i}^{q^{-1}}t^{-i}.

We say that
\matheur{M}
is
pure
if there exist an
\mathbb{K}[\![1/t]\!]
-lattice of
L
of
\matheur{M}(\!(1/t)\!)
and
u,v\in\mathbb{N}
such that

t^{u}\cdot L=\sigma^{v}\cdot L.

In this case, it is known that (see
[
HJ20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (e)]
)

-\frac{v}{u}=-\frac{\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}}{\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}\matheur{M}}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}w(\matheur{M}),

called the
weight of
\matheur{M}
.
Moreover, any subquotient of a pure
t
-motive is still pure of the same weight (see
[
HJ20
, Proposition 2.4.10 (c)]
).

2.3.
CM fields and CM types

A finite extension
\matheur{F}
over
\matheur{k}
is called
totally real
if the infinite place
\infty
of
\matheur{k}
splits completely in
\matheur{F}
. We note that
\matheur{F}
is then necessarily separable over
\matheur{k}
, and every finite extension
\matheur{K}/\matheur{k}
contains a maximal totally real intermediate field.
A
CM field over
\matheur{k}
is a finite separable extension
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}

such that every place
\infty^{+}
of its maximal real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
lying over the infinite place
\infty
of
\matheur{k}
is non-split in
\matheur{K}
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\matheur{k}
.
As the classical case, one may consider CM types as special collections of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embeddings from
\matheur{K}
into
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
sending
t
to
\theta
.
For our purpose, we shall follow
[
BCPW22
, Definition 3.3.2]
to define generalized CM types using the geometric model of the CM function field
\matheur{K}
.
In concrete terms, suppose the constant field of
\matheur{K}
is
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
with
\ell\geq 1
.
Then
\matheur{K}
is geometric over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)
.
Let
X
be the smooth, projective, geometrically connected algebraic curve defined over
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
with function field
\matheur{K}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
(which is always geometric over
\matheur{k}
), and let
X^{+}
be the curve over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
with function field
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
The embeddings

\matheur{k}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}^{+}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}
induce the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphisms (viewing
X
as an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-scheme)
\pi_{X/X^{+}}:X\rightarrow X^{+}
,
\pi_{X^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:X^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}
, and
\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}:X\rightarrow\mathbb{P}^{1}

so that

\pi_{X/\mathbb{P}^{1}}=\pi_{X^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}\circ\pi_{X/X^{+}}.

Let
\mathbf{X}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X
and
\mathbf{X}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X^{+}
be the base changes of
X
and
X^{+}
from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\bar{k}
, respectively,
and denote by
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
,
\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{+}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}
, and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}
the base changes of respective morphisms to
\bar{k}
.
Set

J{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{-1}(\theta)=\{\xi\in\mathbf{X}(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mid\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta\},

the set of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-valued points of
\mathbf{X}
lying above
\theta\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
(which is actually a subset of
\mathbf{X}(\bar{k})
).
The generalized CM types of a given CM field are defined as follows:

Definition 2.3.1
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\matheur{k}
with the maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
Let
I{K}
be the free abelian group generated by elements in
J{K}
.
Let
I{K}^{0}
be the subgroup of
I{K}
consisting of elements

\Phi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi,\quad m_{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z},

satisfying

\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{1}^{+})}m_{\xi}=\sum_{\xi^{\prime}\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi_{2}^{+})}m_{\xi^{\prime}},\quad\forall\xi_{1}^{+},\xi_{2}^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

We put

(2.3.2)

\wt(\Phi)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}^{-1}(\xi^{+})}m_{\xi}\quad\text{ for a }\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

A
generalized CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a nonzero effective divisor in
I{K}^{0}
. A
CM type
of
\matheur{K}
is a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
for which
\text{wt}(\Xi)=1
.

Remark 2.3.3
.

Let
\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
be the set of embeddings
\nu:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
satisfying that
\nu\big|_{\matheur{k}}=\nu_{\theta}
.
We recall the bijection between
J{K}
and

\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})

given in
[
BCPW22
, after Prop. 3.3.4]
as follows:
Take
\xi\in J{K}
, corresponding to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow X.

As
\xi
is not
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
-valued (where
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
is the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
),
this morphism must factor through the generic point

\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X
.
Thus we obtain an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism
\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\rightarrow\Spec(\matheur{K})
, which corresponds to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra embedding
\nu_{\xi}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
sending
t
to
\theta
(as
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi)=\theta
).
Conversely, for
\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
, the induced
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism

\Spec(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\Spec(\matheur{K})\hookrightarrow X

gives a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-valued point
\xi_{\nu}
of
\mathbf{X}
so that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\xi_{\nu})=\theta
(as
\nu(t)=\theta
).
It is clear that

\xi_{\nu_{\xi}}=\xi,\quad\forall\xi\in J{K},\quad\text{ and }\quad\nu_{\xi_{\nu}}=\nu,\quad\forall\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

In particular, let
\Xi=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
.
one has

J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}=\{\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{i})\mid i=1,...,d\}

whence
\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})=\{\nu_{\xi_{i}}\big|_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\mid i=1,...,d\}
.
This explains the analogy between our CM types and the classical ones.

2.3.4
.

Inflation and restriction.

Let
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
be two CM fields over
\matheur{k}
with
\matheur{K}_{1}\subset\matheur{K}_{2}
. Let
X_{1}
(resp.
X_{2}
) be the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-scheme associated to
\matheur{K}_{1}
(resp.
\matheur{K}_{2}
), and let
\pi:X_{2}\rightarrow X_{1}
be the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism corresponding to the embedding
\matheur{K}_{1}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}_{2}
.
The inflation
\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}:I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}\rightarrow I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}
and restriction
\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}:I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}\rightarrow I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}
are defined via the pull-back and push-forward, respectively:

\displaystyle\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Phi_{1})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi^{*}(\Phi_{1}),\quad\forall\Phi_{1}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{1}},

\displaystyle\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Phi_{2})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\pi_{*}(\Phi_{2}),\quad\forall\Phi_{2}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}.

One checks that

\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}\bigl(I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}^{0}\bigr)\subset I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}^{0}\quad\textup{and}\quad\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}\bigl(I_{\matheur{K}_{2}}^{0}\bigr)\subset I_{\matheur{K}_{1}}^{0},

which in particular implies that for each generalized CM type
\Xi_{1}
of
\matheur{K}_{1}
(resp.
\Xi_{2}
of
\matheur{K}_{2}
), the divisor
\Inf_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Xi_{1})
(resp.
\Res_{\matheur{K}_{2}/\matheur{K}_{1}}(\Xi_{2})
) is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}_{2}
(resp.
\matheur{K}_{1}
).

2.4.
CM dual
t
-motives

We retain the notations of the last subsection with a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
whose constant field is
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
, and fix an algebraically closed subfield
\mathbb{K}
of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
containing
\bar{k}
.
Let
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\matheur{A}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
in
\matheur{K}
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
, the corresponding embedding
\nu_{\xi}
induces a ring homomorphism

\bar{\nu}_{\xi}:O_{\mathbf{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}\twoheadrightarrow\mathbb{K}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}.

Set
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\ker\bar{\nu}_{\xi}
, which is a maximal ideal of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
so that
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}\cap\mathbb{K}[t]=(t-\theta)\mathbb{K}[t]
.
The map
(\xi\mapsto\mathfrak{P}_{\xi})
gives a one-to-one correspondence between
J{K}
and the set of maximal ideals of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
lying over
(t-\theta)\mathbb{K}[t]
, and

(t-\theta)\cdot O_{\mathbf{K}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}.

Definition 2.4.1
.

A
CM dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{K}
is a dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}
satisfying:

(1)

There exists a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
with
[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{K}[t]}(\matheur{M})
and an
\matheur{A}
-algebra homomorphism
O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{K}[t,\tau]}(\matheur{M})
, which induces an
O_{\mathbf{K}}
-module structure on
\matheur{M}
so that
\matheur{M}
is projective of rank one over
O_{\mathbf{K}}
(see
[
BCPW22
, Lemma C.1.1]
).

(2)

There exists a generalized CM type
\Xi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi
of
\matheur{K}

such that

\sigma\matheur{M}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\cdot\matheur{M},\quad\text{ where }\mathfrak{I}_{\Xi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{m_{\xi}}.

(3)

\matheur{M}
is pure.

In this case, we say that
\matheur{M}
has generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.

Remark 2.4.2
.

In
[
BCPW22
]
, the definition of CM dual
t
-motives comes from geometric constructions following
[
Si97
]
,
[
BP02
]
and
[
ABP04
]
.
It is shown in
[
BCPW22
, Proposition C.1.3]
that these two definitions are equivalent.

Recall the following essential properties of CM dual
t
-motives (see
[
BCPW22
, Theorem 1.2.5 and Remark 1.2.6]
).

Theorem 2.4.3
.

(1)

Given a CM field
\matheur{K}
and a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
, there exists a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.

(2)

Every CM dual
t
-motive is uniformizable (and pure).

(3)

Two CM dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{K}
with the same generalized CM type must be isogenous over
\mathbb{K}
.

3.
Period symbols and the period distribution

3.1.
Period symbols

Let
\matheur{M}
be a dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
.
The
de Rham module of
\matheur{M}
over
\bar{k}
is (see
[
HJ20
, Section 4.5]
):

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\bar{k}}(\matheur{M}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M},\bar{k}).

In general, for an algebraically closed subfield
\mathbb{K}
of
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
containing
\bar{k}
,
we set

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{K})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\bar{k}}H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\cong\Hom_{\mathbb{K}}(\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}},\mathbb{K}),\quad\text{where}\quad\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{K}\otimes_{\bar{k}}\matheur{M}.

We also identify
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{K})
with the space of
\mathbb{K}
-linear functionals on
\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}}
which factor through
\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}}/(t-\theta)\matheur{M}_{\mathbb{K}}
.
Elements of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{K})
are called
differentials of
\matheur{M}
over
\mathbb{K}
.

Suppose
\matheur{M}
is uniformizable.
Given
\omega\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
,
we naturally extend
\omega
to a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear functional on
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}=\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}
by setting

\omega(f\otimes m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=f(\theta)\cdot\omega(m),\quad\forall f\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\text{ and }m\in\matheur{M},

and this induces
a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear functional
\omega:\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}/(t-\theta)\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Suppose
\matheur{M}
is uniformizable.
The
de Rham pairing
of
\matheur{M}
is defined as follows:

(3.1.1)

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\times H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty},\quad(\omega,\gamma)\longmapsto\int_{\gamma}\omega\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\omega(\gamma),

This pairing is non-degenerate
(see
[
BCPW22
, Lemma 5.1.2]
).
Every period integral
\int_{\gamma}\omega
is called a
period of
\matheur{M}
.
In particular, let

H{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\matheur{A}}(H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\matheur{k})\quad\text{ and }\quad H{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{v_{\theta},\matheur{k}}{\otimes}H{M}\cong\Hom_{\matheur{A}}(H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\mathbb{C}_{\infty}),

where the
\matheur{A}
-module structure on
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
comes from the evaluation map
v_{\theta}:\matheur{k}\cong k\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
The de Rham pairing induces the so-called
period isomorphism

(3.1.2)

H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cong H{M}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}.

Given two uniformizable dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{K}
,
let
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be a morphism over
\mathbb{K}
. The induced
\mathbb{K}
-linear homomorphism
f^{*}:H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\mathbb{K})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{K})
defined by

f^{*}\omega^{\prime}(m)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\omega^{\prime}(f(m)),\quad\forall m\in\matheur{M}\text{ and }\omega^{\prime}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\mathbb{K}),

is called the
pull-back of
f
.
Recall that
f_{*}:H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
is the push-forward of
f
(see Section
2.2.5
).
The following equality holds:

(3.1.3)

\int_{f_{*}\gamma}\omega^{\prime}=\int_{\gamma}f^{*}\omega^{\prime},\quad\forall\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\ \omega^{\prime}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\mathbb{K}).

Consequently, let
f_{*}^{\vee}:H^{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rightarrow H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
be the
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
-linear map induced by
f_{*}
.
The following diagram commutes:

(3.1.4)

\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{f^{*}}
\textstyle{H^{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{*}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}},}

Remark 3.1.5
.

Let
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be two dual
t
-motives over
\mathbb{K}
.
A morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
(over
\mathbb{K}
) is
essentially surjective
if
f(\matheur{M})
is isogenous to
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
via the inclusion.
In this case, we get that
f_{*}^{\vee}:H^{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\rightarrow H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
is injective, whence so is
f^{*}:H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\mathbb{K})\rightarrow H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{K})
by the above commutative diagram.

Now, let
\matheur{M}
be a CM dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
, there exists a
nonzero differential
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
, unique up to a
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiple, satisfying (see
[
BCPW22
, Proposition 5.2.1]
)

(3.1.6)

\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}(\alpha\cdot m)=\nu_{\xi}(\alpha)\cdot\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}(m),\quad\text{$\forall\,m\in\matheur{M}$ and $\alpha\in O{K}$.}

We call
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
the
differential of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi
.
Since
H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
is projective of rank one over
O{K}
and any other CM dual
t
-motive with the same CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
must be isogenous to
\matheur{M}
, the period
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}
for a nonzero cycle
\gamma\in H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
is, up to a
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiple, uniquely determined by
\matheur{K}
,
\Xi
, and
\xi
alone.

Definition 3.1.7

(See
[
BCPW22
, Def. 5.2.2]
)
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\matheur{k}
and
\Xi
be a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Given
\xi\in J{K}
, the
period symbol

\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
is the coset in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
represented by the period

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi}

for any CM dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}
with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
and a nonzero cycle
\gamma\in H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
.

We also use the notation
p{K}(\xi,\Xi)\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
for an arbitrary representative of
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\Xi)
, and call it a period symbol as well if there is no risk of confusion.

Remark 3.1.8
.

We denote by
x\sim y
for
x,y\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
that
x/y\in\bar{k}^{\times}
.
When
\matheur{K}=\matheur{k}
and
\Xi=n\xi_{\theta}
, where
\xi_{\theta}
is the point
t=\theta\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{/\bar{k}}
,
we have that (see
[
BCPW22
, Proposition 5.2.3]
)

p_{\matheur{k}}(\xi_{\theta},n\xi_{\theta})\sim\tilde{\pi}^{n},

where
\tilde{\pi}
is a fundamental period of the Carlitz
\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]
-module:

\tilde{\pi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(-\theta)^{\frac{q}{q-1}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(1-\frac{\theta}{\theta^{q^{i}}}\right)^{-1}.

The needed properties of period symbols are stated in the following (see
[
BCPW22
, Theorem 1.3.1]
):

Theorem 3.1.9
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field over
\matheur{k}
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
,
we may extend
\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi,\cdot)
to a unique group homomorphism
from
I{K}^{0}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
satisfying the following:

(1)

Let
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
be a CM field containing
\matheur{K}
. For
\xi^{\prime}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
,

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\big({\Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\xi^{\prime}),\Phi^{0}\big)=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\big(\xi^{\prime},{\Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})\big),

where
{\Res}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
and
{\Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}
are restriction and inflation, respectively (see Section
2.3.4
).

(2)

For each
\varrho\in\Aut(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})
,

\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}}\big(\xi^{\varrho},\Phi^{0}\big)=\mathcal{P}{K}\big(\xi,(\Phi^{0})^{\varrho^{-1}}\big),\quad\forall\xi\in J{K},\ \Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0},

where
\xi^{\varrho}\in J{K}
corresponds to the embedding
\nu_{\xi}\circ\varrho
and
(\Phi^{0})^{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi}m_{\xi}\xi^{\varrho}
.

(3)

Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
. Given
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
and
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, we have the Legendre relation

\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}p{K}(\xi,\Phi^{0})\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\text{\rm wt}(\Phi^{0})}.

Next, we shall use these properties to introduce an analogue of Anderson’s period distribution on the space of “Stickelberger functions”.

3.2.
Stickelberger functions

Let
\matheur{G}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}/\matheur{k})
where
\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}
is a separable closure of
\matheur{k}
.
Fix an embedding
\nu:\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
which extends the evaluation map
v_{\theta}:\matheur{k}\cong k
sending
t
to
\theta
.
The Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(k^{\rm sep}_{\infty}/k_{\infty})
is identified with a subgroup
\matheur{G}_{\infty}
of
\matheur{G}
via
\nu
, which is the decomposition group of a place of
\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}
lying over
\infty
.

Definition 3.2.1
.

Let
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
be the space consisting of all locally constant
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions
\varphi
on
\matheur{G}
satisfying the following two conditions:

(1)

For
\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}\in\matheur{G}
and
\varrho_{\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}
, we have

\varphi\big(\varrho_{1}\cdot(\varrho_{2}\varrho_{\infty}\varrho_{2}^{-1}\varrho_{\infty}^{-1})\big)=\varphi(\varrho_{1});

(2)

Let
d\varrho_{\infty}
be the Haar measure on
\matheur{G}_{\infty}
normalized so that
\text{vol}(\matheur{G}_{\infty},d\varrho_{\infty})=1
.
Then

\int_{\matheur{G}_{\infty}}\varphi(\varrho_{1}\varrho_{\infty})d\varrho_{\infty}=\int_{\matheur{G}_{\infty}}\varphi(\varrho_{2}\varrho_{\infty})d\varrho_{\infty}\quad\forall\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}\in\matheur{G}.

Remark 3.2.2
.

(1)

The space
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
is endowed with a left action of
\matheur{G}
under right translations:

(\varrho_{0}\cdot\varphi)(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\varphi(\varrho\varrho_{0}),\quad\forall\varrho_{0},\varrho\in\matheur{G}\text{ and }\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}).

Given
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
, let
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
be the stabilizer of
\varphi
in
\matheur{G}
, which is of finite index in
\matheur{G}
.
The condition (1) of Definition
3.2.1
says that the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
is contained in
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
,
which implies in particular that
\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
becomes a subgroup of
\matheur{G}
with finite index.

(2)

Given a subgroup
\matheur{H}
of
\matheur{G}
with
\matheur{H}\supset[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]\ (\supset[\matheur{G}_{\infty},\matheur{G}_{\infty}])
,
one observes that

(a)

\varrho\matheur{G}_{\infty}\varrho^{-1}\subset\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}
for every
\varrho\in\matheur{G}
;

(b)

\matheur{H}
is normal in
\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}
and

\displaystyle\frac{\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}}{\matheur{H}}\cong\frac{\matheur{G}_{\infty}}{\matheur{G}_{\infty}\cap\matheur{H}}
is an abelian group.

When
\matheur{H}=\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
for a function
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
,
let

\matheur{K}_{\varphi}
and
\matheur{K}^{+}_{\varphi}
be the fixed field of
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
and
\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
in
\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}
, respectively.
Then (a) implies that
\matheur{K}_{\varphi}^{+}
is a totally real field over
\matheur{k}
, and (b) shows that

\matheur{K}_{\varphi}
is a CM field over
\matheur{k}
with maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}_{\varphi}^{+}
satisfying that the extension
\matheur{K}_{\varphi}/\matheur{K}_{\varphi}^{+}
is abelian with
\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}_{\varphi}/\matheur{K}_{\varphi}^{+})\cong\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\varphi}/\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
.

(3)

Given two CM fields
\matheur{K}_{1}
and
\matheur{K}_{2}
, the compositum
\matheur{K}_{1}\cdot\matheur{K}_{2}
may not be a CM field in general (cf.
[
BCPW22
, Remark 3.1.2 (4)]
).
However, for
\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2}\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
,
the condition (1) of Definition
3.2.1
ensures that

[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]\subset\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{1}}\cap\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{2}}.

This implies that the compositum
\matheur{K}=\matheur{K}_{\varphi_{1}}\cdot\matheur{K}_{\varphi_{2}}
is still a CM field with

\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}/\matheur{K})=\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{1}}\cap\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{2}},

and its maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}^{+}
is the fixed field of
\matheur{G}_{\infty}(\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{1}}\cap\matheur{H}_{\varphi_{2}})
.

(4)

The condition (1) of Definition
3.2.1
says in particular that the space
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
is independent of the chosen embedding
\nu
.
Indeed, let
\nu^{\prime}:\matheur{k}^{\rm sep}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
be another embedding and
\matheur{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}
be the corresponding decomposition group.
There exists
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}
so that
\nu^{\prime}=\nu\circ\varrho_{0}
, whence

\matheur{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}=\varrho_{0}^{-1}\matheur{G}_{\infty}\varrho_{0}
.
Notice that

[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]=[\matheur{G},\varrho_{0}^{-1}\matheur{G}_{\infty}\varrho_{0}]=[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}].

Thus for a locally constant function
\varphi
on
\matheur{G}
, the condition that
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
is contained in
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
is equivalent to saying that
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}^{\prime}]
is contained in
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
, and for every
\varrho\in\matheur{G}
and
\varrho_{\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}
,

\varphi(\varrho\varrho_{\infty})=\varphi\big((\varrho\varrho_{\infty})(\varrho_{\infty}^{-1}\varrho_{0}^{-1}\varrho_{\infty}\varrho_{0})\big)=\varphi(\varrho(\varrho_{0}^{-1}\varrho_{\infty}\varrho_{0})).

Therefore the assertion holds.

(5)

Given an open subgroup
\matheur{H}
of
\matheur{G}
with
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]\subset\matheur{H}
,
set

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})\mid\varrho\cdot\varphi=\varphi,\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{H}\}.

Every
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H})
can be written in the following unique form:

\varphi=\sum_{\varrho\matheur{H}\in\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}}m_{\varrho\matheur{H}}(\varphi)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\varrho\matheur{H}},

where
m_{\varrho\matheur{H}}(\varphi)=\varphi(\varrho)
for each coset
\varrho\matheur{H}\in\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\varrho\matheur{H}}
is the characteristic function of
\varrho\matheur{H}
.
Moreover, put
\matheur{H}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}
and
\matheur{H}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{G}_{\infty}\cap\matheur{H}
.
For each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H})
, the isomorphism
\matheur{G}_{\infty}/\matheur{H}_{\infty}\cong\matheur{H}^{+}/\matheur{H}
enables us to translate the condition (2) in Definition
3.2.1
into

\sum_{\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}/\matheur{H}_{\infty}}m_{\varrho_{1}\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}}(\varphi)=\sum_{\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}/\matheur{H}_{\infty}}m_{\varrho_{2}\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}}(\varphi),\quad\forall\varrho_{1},\varrho_{2}\in\matheur{G}.

Let
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}
be a CM field satisfying that

\matheur{H}{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gal(\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}/\matheur{K})\supset[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
.
For each
\xi\in J{K}
,
take
\varrho_{\xi}\in\matheur{G}
so that the embedding
\nu_{\xi}\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
satisfies

\nu_{\xi}=(\nu\circ\varrho_{\xi})\big|{K}.

The coset
\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}
in
\matheur{G}
is uniquely determined by
\xi
.
Conversely, given a coset
\varrho\matheur{H}{K}
in
\matheur{G}
, there exists a unique point
\xi_{\varrho}\in J{K}
so that

\nu_{\xi_{\varrho}}=(\nu\circ\varrho)\big|{K}\quad\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

This gives us a bijection (with respect to the fixed
\nu:\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}\cong k^{\mathrm{sep}}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
):

(3.2.3)

\displaystyle J{K}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longleftrightarrow}\limits}}

\displaystyle\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K}

\displaystyle\xi

\displaystyle\longmapsto

\displaystyle\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}

\displaystyle\xi_{\varrho}

\longmapsto

\displaystyle\varrho\matheur{H}{K}.

Remark 3.2.4
.

Given another embedding
\nu^{\prime}:\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,
there exists
\varrho^{\prime}\in\matheur{G}
so that
\nu^{\prime}=\nu\circ\varrho^{\prime}
.
Hence the coset corresponding to a given
\xi\in J{K}
under the above bijection with respect to
\nu^{\prime}
becomes
(\varrho^{\prime})^{-1}\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}
.

Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
, and
\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}/\matheur{K}^{+})=\matheur{G}_{\infty}\matheur{H}{K}
.
Put
\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K},\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{G}_{\infty}\cap\matheur{H}{K}
.
Then (
3.2.3
) induces the following one-to-one correspondence for each
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
:

(3.2.5)

\{\xi\in J{K}\mid\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\}\mathrel{{\mathop{\longleftrightarrow}\limits}}\varrho_{\xi^{+}}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}/\matheur{H}{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big\{\varrho_{\xi^{+}}\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}{K}\mid\varrho_{\infty}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K},\infty}\in\matheur{G}_{\infty}/\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K},\infty}\big\}.

From Remark
3.2.2
(5),
we may write each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
in the following unique form:

(3.2.6)

\varphi=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}(\varphi)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}},\quad\text{where $m_{\xi}(\varphi)=\varphi(\varrho_{\xi})$ for every $\xi\in J{K}$,}

and the condition (2) in Definition
3.2.1
becomes:

(3.2.7)

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi_{1}\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{1})=\xi_{1}^{+}\end{subarray}}m_{\xi_{1}}(\varphi)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi_{2}\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi_{2})=\xi_{2}^{+}\end{subarray}}m_{\xi_{2}}(\varphi),\quad\forall\xi_{1}^{+},\xi_{2}^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}.

Given
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
,
write

\Phi^{0}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\xi,\quad\text{ with\quad$m_{\xi}\in\mathbb{Z}$.}

We set

(3.2.8)

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}},

Lemma 3.2.9
.

Let
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
be a CM field satisfying that
\matheur{H}{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}/\matheur{K})
contains the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
.

(1)

For every
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, we have that
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
.
Moreover, the homomorphism

\varphi{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\Phi^{0}\mapsto\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}):I{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
is injective.

(2)

The space
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
consists of all the rational multiples of
