\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}
for
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, for which
\varphi{K}
induces a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism
\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}\cong\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
.

Proof.

Comparing the condition of
\Phi^{0}
in Definition
2.3.1
with (
3.2.7
), we get that
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}
lies in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
.
Suppose
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}=0
.
As distinct cosets of
\matheur{H}{K}
in
\matheur{G}
are disjoint, the expression of
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}
in (
3.2.8
) tells us that
m_{\xi}=0
for every
\xi\in J{K}
, whence
\Phi^{0}=0
.
This implies that
\varphi{K}
is injective and completes the proof of (1).

To prove
(2)
, take
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
and suppose
\varphi
is
\mathbb{Z}
-valued without loss of generality.
Expressing
\varphi
as in (
3.2.6
),
we get
m_{\xi}(\varphi)\in\mathbb{Z}
, and (
3.2.7
) ensures that

\Phi^{0}_{\varphi}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}(\varphi)\xi\quad\in I{K}^{0}.

It is clear that
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}_{\varphi}}=\varphi
and the result holds.
∎

Remark 3.2.10
.

For each CM field
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
satisfying that
\matheur{H}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}/\matheur{K})
contains the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
, the isomorphism in Lemma
3.2.9
(2) says that

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{Z}}I{K}^{0}=1+(1-\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]})[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}].

The following lemma shows that the embeddings
\varphi{K}:I{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})\subset\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
factor through the inflation maps:

Lemma 3.2.11
.

Let
\matheur{K},\matheur{K}^{\prime}\subset\matheur{k}^{\text{\rm sep}}
be two CM fields over
\matheur{k}
fixed by the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
and
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}^{\prime}
.
For each
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, we have

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}=\varphi_{\matheur{K}^{\prime},\text{\rm Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})}\quad\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}).

Proof.

Let
X
and
X^{\prime}
be the curves corresponding to
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{K}^{\prime}
, respectively.
Let

\pi_{X^{\prime}/X}:X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be the
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-morphism corresponding to the inclusion
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}^{\prime}
, and
\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}/\mathbf{X}}
its base change from
\mathbb{F}_{q}
to
\bar{k}
.
Note that for
\xi^{\prime}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}
, one has that

\pi_{\mathbf{X}^{\prime}/\mathbf{X}}(\xi^{\prime})=\xi
if and only if
\nu_{\xi^{\prime}}\big|{K}=\nu_{\xi}
.
Let
\varrho_{\xi^{\prime}}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}
and
\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}
be the cosets in
\matheur{G}
corresponding to
\xi^{\prime}
and
\xi
, respectively.
Then

\nu_{\xi^{\prime}}\big|{K}=\nu_{\xi}\quad\text{ if and only if }\quad\varrho_{\xi^{\prime}}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}\subset\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}.

Given
\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}
, write

\Phi^{0}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\xi\quad\text{ and so }\quad\text{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\xi' \in J_{\eK'}}{\pi_{\bX'/\bX}(\xi') = \xi}}\xi^{\prime}.

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\varrho_{\xi}\matheur{H}{K}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\sum_{\varrho^{\prime}\in\matheur{H}{K}/\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}}\mathbf{1}_{\varrho_{\xi}\varrho^{\prime}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}m_{\xi}\cdot\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\xi' \in J_{\eK'}}{\pi_{X'/X}(\xi') = \xi}}\mathbf{1}_{\varrho_{\xi^{\prime}}\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varphi_{\matheur{K}^{\prime},\text{Inf}_{\matheur{K}^{\prime}/\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})}.

∎

Remark 3.2.12
.

For each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
, recall that
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
is the stabilizer of
\varphi
in
\matheur{G}
and
\matheur{K}_{\varphi}
is the fixed field of
\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
.
By Remark
3.2.2
(2), we have that
\matheur{K}_{\varphi}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
is a CM field, and

\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}_{\varphi}}=\matheur{H}_{\varphi}
.
Hence
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}_{\matheur{K}_{\varphi}})
.
In conclusion,
Lemma
3.2.9
and
3.2.11
induces a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\varinjlim_{\matheur{K}}I{K}^{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}),

\displaystyle I_{\matheur{K}}^{0}\ \ni\ \Phi^{0}\quad

\displaystyle\longmapsto\ \varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}},

where
\matheur{K}
runs through all CM fields in
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
fixed by
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
, and the direct limit is with respect to the inflation maps.

3.3.
The period distribution

We introduce an analogue of Anderson’s period distribution (see
[
And82
, Proposition 1.5]
) by proving the following theorem:

Theorem 3.3.1
.

Fix an embedding
\nu:\matheur{k}^{\text{\rm sep}}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
.
There exists a unique
\mathbb{Q}
-linear distribution
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}:\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
(with respect to
\nu
) satisfying

(3.3.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\nu}(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi^{0}})=\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{\nu},\Phi^{0}),\quad\forall\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0},

where
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\text{\rm sep}}
is a CM field fixed by the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
,
and
\xi_{\nu}\in J{K}
is the unique point satisfying that
\nu_{\xi_{\nu}}=\nu\big|{K}
.

Proof.

For each CM field
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\text{\rm sep}}
fixed by the commutator subgroup
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
,
define
\tilde{\mathscr{P}}_{\nu,\matheur{K}}:I{K}^{0}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
by

\tilde{\mathscr{P}}_{\nu,\matheur{K}}(\Phi^{0})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{P}{K}(\xi_{\nu},\Phi^{0}),\quad\forall\Phi^{0}\in I{K}^{0}.

By Lemma
3.2.11
, we obtain a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear homomorphism

\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\nu}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\text{id}_{\mathbb{Q}}\otimes\left(\varinjlim_{\matheur{K}}\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\nu,\matheur{K}}\right):\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\Big(\varinjlim_{\matheur{K}}I{K}^{0}\Big)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},

and
\mathcal{P}_{\nu}:\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is nothing but the composition of
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{\nu}
with the inverse of the isomorphism in Remark
3.2.12
.
∎

Remark 3.3.3
.

Notice that
\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}}=\varphi_{\matheur{k},\xi_{\theta}}
, where
\xi_{\theta}\in J_{\matheur{k}}
is the unique point
t=\theta\in\mathbb{P}^{1}_{/\bar{k}}
.
By Remark
3.1.8
we have that

\mathscr{P}_{\nu}(\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}})=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{k}}(\xi_{\theta},\xi_{\theta})=\tilde{\pi}\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field in
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
fixed by
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
.
Suppose further that
\matheur{K}
is Galois over
\matheur{k}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
By
[
BCPW22
, Corollary 1.3.6 (b)]
, we know that

(3.3.4)

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\big(p{K}(\xi_{v},\Phi^{0})\mid\Phi^{0}\in I{k}^{0}\big)=1+(1-\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]})\cdot[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}].

Put
\matheur{G}{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})\cong\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K}
.
Then
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}/\matheur{H}{K})
can be identified with a subspace
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})
of
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions on
\matheur{G}{K}
.
Let
\bar{k}\big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu}\big(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})\big)\big)
be the field generated by arbitrary representatives of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}(\varphi)\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
for all
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})
.
By Theorem
3.3.1
and (
3.3.4
), we obtain:

Theorem 3.3.5
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field in
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
which is Galois over
\matheur{k}
and fixed by
[\matheur{G},\matheur{G}_{\infty}]
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.
Then

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\big(\mathscr{P}_{\nu}\big(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})\big)\big)=1+(1-\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]})\cdot[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}].

Consequently,
the period distribution
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}:\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
is injective.

3.4.
The restriction to cyclotomic function fields

Given
a\in\matheur{A}
, recall the
a
-th division polynomial

C_{a}(t,z)\in\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]
defined recursively as follows: write
a=bt+\epsilon
where
b\in A
and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}
, then

C_{a}(t,z)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if $a=0$;}\\
C_{b}(t,tz+z^{q})+\epsilon z,&\text{ if $a\neq 0$.}\end{cases}

For each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
,
the
\mathfrak{n}
-th Carlitz cyclotomic polynomial
C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z)
is the unique (irreducible) factor of
C_{\mathfrak{n}}(t,z)
which is monic in
z
and satisfies (cf.
[
ABP04
, Section 6.3.2]
)

C_{\mathfrak{n}}(t,z)=\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\mfk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\mfk\mid\nfk}}C^{*}_{\mathfrak{m}}(t,z),\quad\forall\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}.

Let
O_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]/(C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z))
, which is a Dedekind domain, and let
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the field of fractions of
O_{\mathfrak{n}}
, called the
\mathfrak{n}
-th Carlitz cyclotomic function field
.
It is known that
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\matheur{k}
is a finite Galois extension with

(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\cong\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\matheur{k})=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n}}
,
where the isomorphism comes from the Artin map:
for
\alpha=a\bmod\mathfrak{n}\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}
,
the corresponding
\varrho_{\alpha}\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is uniquely determined by the condition that (see
[
Hayes74
, Theorem 2.3]
or
[
Rosen02
, Theorem 12.8]
)

(3.4.1)

\varrho_{\alpha}(z)\bmod C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=C_{a}(t,z)\bmod C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z)\in O_{\mathfrak{n}}.

Moreover,
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is a geometric CM field over
\matheur{k}
, where every place
\infty^{+}
of its maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{+}
lying over
\infty
is totally ramified in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
with ramification index
q-1

(see
[
Hayes74
, Theorem 3.2]
or
[
Rosen02
, Theorem 12.14]
).
In fact, under the Artin map we have that

(3.4.2)

\Gal(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{+})\cong\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\hookrightarrow(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}.

For
\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{m}|\mathfrak{n}
, define the inclusion map
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
by

O_{\mathfrak{m}}=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]}{(C_{\mathfrak{m}}^{*}(t,z))}\hookrightarrow O_{\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]}{(C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z))},\quad z\bmod C_{\mathfrak{m}}^{*}(t,z)\longmapsto C_{\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{m}}(t,z)\bmod C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z).

Let
\matheur{K}^{\text{geo}}
be the compositum of all the Carlitz cyclotomic function fields.
The Galois group
\matheur{G}^{\text{geo}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{geo}}/\matheur{k})
is isomorphic (via (
3.4.1
)) to

\underset{\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}{\varprojlim}(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\overset{\sim}{\longleftrightarrow}\matheur{G}^{\text{geo}}.

We may suppose that our chosen separable closure
\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}
contains
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{geo}}
.

Let
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
be the algebraic closure of
\mathbb{F}_{q}
in
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
, and put
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{ari}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}\cdot\matheur{k}
, the compositum of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
and
\matheur{k}
in
\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
.
Identify

\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{ari}}/\matheur{k})\cong\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}/\mathbb{F}_{q})=\varprojlim_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}/\mathbb{F}_{q})\quad\text{ with }\quad\varprojlim_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})

by Frobenius twistings: for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
, the corresponding
\varrho_{c}\in\matheur{G}_{\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}/\mathbb{F}_{q})
is the
q^{c}
-power Frobenius map, i.e.

(3.4.3)

\varrho_{c}(\varepsilon)=\varepsilon^{q^{c}},\quad\forall\varepsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}.

Let

\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
be the compositum of
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{geo}}
and
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{ari}}
.
The Galois group
\matheur{G}^{\text{cyc}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\text{cyc}}/\matheur{k})
is isomorphic to

\underset{\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}{\varprojlim}(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times\varprojlim_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})\overset{\sim}{\longleftrightarrow}\matheur{G}^{\text{geo}}\times\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}}\overset{\sim}{\longleftrightarrow}\matheur{G}^{\text{cyc}}.

Put
\matheur{H}^{\text{cyc}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{k}^{\text{sep}}/\matheur{K}^{\text{cyc}})
and let
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\infty}
be the image of
\matheur{G}_{\infty}
in
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
.
We have the following diagram:

(3.4.4)

\displaystyle\frac{\matheur{G}_{\infty}\cdot\matheur{H}^{\text{cyc}}}{\matheur{H}^{\text{cyc}}}

\cong

\matheur{G}_{\infty}^{\text{cyc}}

\cong

\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\hskip 7.11317pt\times\ \underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\varprojlim}\ (\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})

\cap

\cap

\cap

\displaystyle\frac{\matheur{G}}{\matheur{H}^{\text{cyc}}}

\cong

\matheur{G}^{\text{cyc}}

\cong

\underset{\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}{\varprojlim}(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times\ \underset{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}{\varprojlim}\ (\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
.

Given
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}\cdot\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the compositum of
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}
and
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
in
\matheur{K}^{\text{cyc}}
, called the
(\mathfrak{n},\ell)
-th cyclotomic function field
.
Via the identification

(3.4.5)

(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})

\cong

\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n}}\times\matheur{G}_{\ell}

\cong

\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}/\matheur{k})

(\alpha\ \ ,\ \ c)

\longmapsto

(\varrho_{\alpha},\varrho_{c})

\longmapsto

\varrho_{\alpha,c}
,

the maximal totally real subfield
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
of
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
is the fixed field of the subgroup corresponding to

\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
.
In particular, one has that
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1}=\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\matheur{G}_{\ell}=\matheur{G}_{1,\ell}
.
Moreover, for each irreducible
\mathfrak{p}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\mathfrak{n}
, let
\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathfrak{p}}\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
be the Frobenius automorphism corresponding to
\mathfrak{p}
.
We have that (see
[
Rosen02
, Theorem 12.10]
)

(3.4.6)

\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathfrak{p}}=\varrho_{\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}},c_{\mathfrak{p}}},\quad\text{ where $\alpha_{\mathfrak{p}}=\mathfrak{p}\bmod\mathfrak{n}\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}$ and $c_{\mathfrak{p}}=\deg\mathfrak{p}\bmod\ell$.}

For each quotient group
\overline{\matheur{G}}
of
\matheur{G}
,
we let
\mathscr{S}(\overline{\matheur{G}})
be the space consisting of the functions in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G})
which factor through
\overline{\matheur{G}}
.
We may identify
\mathscr{S}(\overline{\matheur{G}})
as a subspace of the
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions on
\overline{\matheur{G}}
.
Then
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\text{cyc}})
is the union of the spaces

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
for all
(\mathfrak{n},\ell)\in\matheur{A}_{+}\times\mathbb{N}
, and by Remark
3.2.2
(5) we have that

(3.4.7)

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\left\{\varphi:\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}\ \Bigg|\ \sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\varphi(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon,c})\text{ is independent of $\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}$}\right\}.

Let
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}^{\rm cyc}
be the restriction of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}
to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\text{cyc}})
.
We shall connect the image of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu}^{\rm cyc}
with the special gamma values
in Section
5.4
.
To do so, we first discuss three types of “diamond brackets” and the corresponding “Stickelberger distributions” in the next section.

4.
Diamond brackets and Stickelberger distributions

4.1.
Diamond brackets

I. The arithmetic case

Given
y\in\mathbb{R}
, let
\langle y\rangle_{\text{ari}}
be the
fractional part of
y
, i.e. the unique real number with
0\leq\langle y\rangle_{\text{ari}}<1
so that

y-\langle y\rangle_{\text{ari}}\in\mathbb{Z}.

We may view
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\text{ari}}

as a function on the quotient group
\mathbb{R}/\mathbb{Z}
, called the
arithmetic diamond bracket
.
Recall the classical reflection and multiplication formulas in the following: for
y\in\mathbb{R}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\langle y\rangle_{\text{ari}}+\langle-y\rangle_{\text{ari}}=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if $y\not\in\mathbb{Z}$;}\\
0&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{i=0}^{N-1}\langle y+\frac{i}{N}\rangle_{\text{ari}}=\langle Ny\rangle_{\text{ari}}+\frac{N-1}{2}.

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
be the localization of
\mathbb{Z}
at the prime number
p
, i.e.

\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{a/b\in\mathbb{Q}\mid a,b\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ with }b\not\equiv 0\bmod p\}.

For each
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, there exist
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and integers
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
so that

\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\cdot\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}.

Set

(4.1.1)

\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{\ell}\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i},

which depends only on
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
.
Restricting
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\text{ari}}
to
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, we have the following relations:

Lemma 4.1.2
.

Given
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, take
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
so that
(q^{\ell}-1)y\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(1)

There exist unique integers
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
satisfying

\langle y\rangle_{\text{\rm ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\langle\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\text{\rm ari}}.

(2)

The following equality holds:

\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\langle q^{i}y\rangle_{\text{\rm ari}}=\frac{\ell\cdot\text{\rm wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)}{q-1}.

II. The geometric case

For
N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, define
\langle\cdot\rangle_{N}:k_{\infty}\rightarrow\{0,1\}
by (see
[
ABP04
, 5.5.1
Definitions
]
,
[
Si97-2
]
, and
[
T91
, Definition 7.6.1]
):

\langle\sum_{i}\epsilon_{i}t^{-i}\rangle_{N}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $\epsilon_{i}=0$ for $0<i<N+1$ and $\epsilon_{N+1}=1$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Since
\langle x+a\rangle_{N}=\langle x\rangle_{N}
for every
x\in k_{\infty}
and
a\in A
,
we may view
\langle\cdot\rangle_{N}
as a function on the quotient group
k_{\infty}/A
.
The
geometric diamond bracket
is:

\langle\cdot\rangle_{\text{geo}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\langle\cdot\rangle_{N}:k_{\infty}/A\rightarrow\{0,1\}.

Observe that for
a\in A
and
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
with
\deg a<\deg\mathfrak{n}
, one has that

\langle\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}}\rangle_{N}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $a\in A_{+}$ with $\deg\mathfrak{n}-\deg a=N+1$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

whence

\langle\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}}\rangle_{\text{geo}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $a\in A_{+}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

For each
x\in k/A
, set

(4.1.3)

\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $0\neq x\in k/A$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Lemma 4.1.4
.

(Cf.
[
ABP04
, 5.5.5]
)
Given
x\in k/A
and
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
, the following relations hold:

(1)

(Reflection formula)

\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\langle\epsilon x\rangle_{\text{\rm geo}}=\text{\rm wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x).

(2)

(Multiplication formula)

\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\langle x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}}\rangle_{\text{\rm geo}}=\langle\mathfrak{n}\cdot x\rangle_{\text{\rm geo}}+\frac{|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}-1}{q-1}.

III. The two-variable case

We start with the following:

Proposition 4.1.5
.

There exists a unique map
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:k\times\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
satisfying that

(1)

for
x\in k
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
,
a\in A
, and
N\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

\langle x+a,y+N\rangle=\langle x,y\rangle
;

(2)

given
x\in k
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
,
we have

\langle x,y\rangle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\langle x,\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle

when writing
\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)
where
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
;

(3)

given
x\in k
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq i<\ell
, we have

\langle x,\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N \equiv-1-i\bmod\ell}}\langle x\rangle_{N}.

We call
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
the
two-variable diamond bracket
.

Proof.

We may use the properties (1)–(3) to define a unique map
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
on
k\times\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, as long as we show that the equality in (2) is independent of the expression of
y
for a given
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
with
0\leq y<1
.
To verify this, take
\ell,\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1},y_{0}^{\prime},...,y_{\ell^{\prime}-1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
with

0\leq y_{i},y^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}<q
for
0\leq i<\ell
and
0\leq i^{\prime}<\ell^{\prime}
so that

\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\cdot\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}=y=\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}y_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cdot\frac{q^{i^{\prime}}}{q^{\ell^{\prime}}-1}.

For
0\leq i^{\prime\prime}<\ell\ell^{\prime}
, put

y_{i^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=y_{i}\quad\text{ for a unique $i$ with $0\leq i<\ell$ and $i^{\prime\prime}\equiv i\bmod\ell$}.

Then

\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}y_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\cdot\frac{q^{i^{\prime}}}{q^{\ell^{\prime}}-1}=y=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\cdot\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\cdot\sum_{j=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}\frac{q^{i+\ell j}}{q^{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-1}=\sum_{i^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\ell\ell^{\prime}-1}y_{i^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime\prime}\cdot\frac{q^{i^{\prime\prime}}}{q^{\ell\ell^{\prime}}-1},

whence

y_{i^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime\prime}=y_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}\quad\text{ for a unique $i^{\prime}$ with $0\leq i^{\prime}<\ell^{\prime}$ and $i^{\prime\prime}\equiv i^{\prime}\bmod\ell^{\prime}$}.

On the other hand, for
x\in k
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N\equiv-1-i \bmod\ell}}\langle x\rangle_{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\sum_{j=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N\equiv-1-i-\ell j \bmod\ell\ell'}}\langle x\rangle_{N}=\sum_{i^{\prime\prime}=0}^{\ell\ell^{\prime}-1}y_{i^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime\prime}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N\equiv-1-i'' \bmod\ell\ell'}}\langle x\rangle_{N}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}y^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N\equiv-1-i'-\ell' j' \bmod\ell\ell'}}\langle x\rangle_{N}=\sum_{i^{\prime}=0}^{\ell^{\prime}-1}y^{\prime}_{i^{\prime}}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N \in\, \scaleto{\ZZ}{4.6pt}_{\scaleto{\geq 0}{4pt}}}{N\equiv-1-i' \bmod\ell'}}\langle x\rangle_{N}.

Therefore the assertion holds.
∎

Remark 4.1.6
.

(1)

A two-variable diamond bracket was first introduced by Thakur in
[
T91
, Definition 8.3.1]
.
The version provided here is normalized slightly differently from Thakur’s in order to simplify the statements of our results.

(2)

For
x\in k
,
a\in A
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, and
N\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have
\langle a,y\rangle=0=\langle x,N\rangle
.

(3)

For every
x\in k
, we have

\langle x,\frac{1}{q-1}\rangle=\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\langle x\rangle_{N}=\langle x\rangle_{\text{geo}}.

(4)

We may view
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
as a function on
(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})
.

The following
diamond bracket relations
are straightforward:

Proposition 4.1.7
.

Take
x\in k
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
.

(1)

For
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq i<\ell
,

\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\langle\epsilon x,\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $x\notin A$ and $\ord_{\infty}(x)\equiv-i\bmod\ell$,}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

(2)

\langle x,y\rangle+\langle x,-y\rangle=\begin{cases}(q-1)\langle x\rangle_{\text{\rm geo}},&\text{ if $y\notin\mathbb{Z}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

(3)

Given
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
,

\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in\eA}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\left(\langle\frac{x+a}{\mathfrak{n}},y\rangle-\langle\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}},y\rangle\right)=\langle x,|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}y\rangle.

Remark 4.1.8
.

(1)

(Reflection formula.) Given
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
,
set

(4.1.9)

\text{wt}_{0}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\cdot\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y).

By Proposition
4.1.7
(1), we have that for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
so that
(q^{\ell}-1)y\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\langle\epsilon x,q^{i}y\rangle=\ell\cdot\text{wt}_{0}(x,y).

(2)

(Multiplication formula.)
Given
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
, Proposition
4.1.7
(3) is equivalent to

\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in\eA}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\langle x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}},y\rangle=\langle\mathfrak{n}x,|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}y\rangle-\langle|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}y\rangle_{\text{ari}}+|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}\cdot\langle y\rangle_{\text{ari}},\quad\forall x\in k\text{ and }y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}.

To see this, we may assume that
y=q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq i<\ell
without loss of generality.
Then the result follows from:

\displaystyle\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\langle\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}},\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\#\{a\in A_{+}\mid\deg a<\deg\mathfrak{n},\ \deg a\equiv\deg\mathfrak{n}+i\bmod\ell\}

\displaystyle=-\langle\frac{q^{\deg\mathfrak{n}+i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}+\frac{q^{\deg\mathfrak{n}+i}}{q^{\ell}-1}.

4.2.
Stickelberger distributions

Define actions of
\matheur{G}
both on
k/A
and on
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
as follows:
Given
\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
, write
\varrho=\varrho_{\alpha,c}

where
(\alpha,c)=(\alpha_{\mathfrak{n}},c_{\ell})_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\in\varprojlim_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})

corresponds to
\varrho
via the Artin map (see (
3.4.5
)).
For each
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
, set

(4.2.1)

\varrho\star x\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\alpha_{\mathfrak{n}}(\theta)\cdot x\ \bmod A\quad\text{ and }\quad\varrho\star y\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=q^{c_{\ell}}\cdot y\ \bmod\mathbb{Z}.

We also let
\matheur{G}
act diagonally on
(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})
, i.e.

\varrho\star(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\varrho\star x,\varrho\star y),\quad\forall(x,y)\in(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}).

Recall that
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{ari}}/\matheur{k})
,
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{geo}}/\matheur{k})
, and
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{k})
.
Given
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
, define

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(y):\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{0}(x):\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
, and

\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,y):\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
respectively in the following:

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(y)(\varrho)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\varrho\star y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}},\quad\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{0}(x)(\varrho)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\varrho\star x\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}},\quad\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}}

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,y)(\varrho)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle\varrho\star x,\varrho\star y\rangle,\quad\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}.

In particular, one has

\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,\frac{1}{q-1})=\operatorname{St}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\quad\forall x\in k/A.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Note that from (
3.4.7
),
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
is actually the whole space of
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions on
\matheur{G}_{1,\ell}
.
Moreover, for
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
the reflection formulas in Lemma
4.1.4
(1) and Remark
4.1.8
(1) imply

\displaystyle\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{0}(x)(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon})

\displaystyle=\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1},

\displaystyle\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,y)(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon,c})

\displaystyle=\ell\cdot\text{wt}_{0}(x,y),\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}.

From
(
3.4.7
)
we have that

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(y)\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell}),\quad\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{0}(x)\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1}),\quad\text{ and }\quad\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,y)\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}).

Definition 4.2.2
.

For
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
, we set

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(-y),

\displaystyle\text{wt}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\text{wt}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(-y),

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{0}(x)-\frac{1}{q-1}\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}}},

\displaystyle\text{wt}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\text{wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)-1,

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,-y)-\operatorname{St}_{0}^{\operatorname{ari}}(-y),

\displaystyle\text{wt}(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\text{wt}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\cdot\text{wt}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y).

We call
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
, and
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
the
Stickelberger functions associated to
y
,
x
, and
(x,y)
, respectively.
The maps

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}})
,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}:k/A\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}})
,
and

\operatorname{St}:(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})

are called the
Stickelberger distributions associated to
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}}
,
and
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
, respectively.

Remark 4.2.3
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
suppose
\langle-y\rangle_{\text{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)
where

y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
.
The diamond bracket relations imply the following:

(1)

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})\quad\text{ and }\quad\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)(\varrho\varrho_{c})=\frac{\ell\cdot\text{wt}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)}{q-1},\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{1,\ell}.

(2)

\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a<\deg\nfk'}}\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}})=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}x),\quad\forall\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\in A_{+},

and

\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon})=\text{wt}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1}.

(3)

\operatorname{St}(x,y)=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}\operatorname{St}(x,\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}),\quad\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\operatorname{St}(x,y)(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon,c})=\ell\cdot\text{wt}(x,y),\quad\forall\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},

and

\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk'}}\operatorname{St}(x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}},y)=\operatorname{St}(\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}x,|\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|_{\infty}y),\quad\forall\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\in A_{+}.

In particular, from the definitions of
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\operatorname{St}
, we get that

(4)

For every
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
,

\operatorname{St}(0,y)=-\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\quad\operatorname{St}(x,\frac{1}{1-q})=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),

and

\operatorname{St}(x,y)+\operatorname{St}(x,-y)=\begin{cases}(q-1)\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x),&\text{ if $y\neq 0\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proposition 4.2.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.

(1)

The space

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
is spanned by
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
for all
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
.
Consequently,

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}})
is spanned by

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
for all
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
.

(2)

The space

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})
is spanned by
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
for all
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
.
Consequently,
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}})
is spanned by
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
for all
x\in k/A
.

(3)

The space

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
is spanned by

\operatorname{St}(x,y)

for all

x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
.
Consequently,
the space
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\rm cyc})
is spanned by
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
for all
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
.

This is an analogue of Deligne’s Theorem stated in
[
And82
, Theorem 3.1]
for the classical case.
The key step is to regard the Stickelberger functions
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
for
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
, where
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
are given, as the “evaluators” of the Artin
L
-functions associated to the characters on the Galois group
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
Let
\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
be the Pontryagin dual of
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
Identifying
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
with
(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
via the Artin map in (
3.4.5
),
for each character
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}

we let
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\boldsymbol{\chi}\big|_{(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}:(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, and put
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
where
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}}
is the conductor of
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}
.
The (finite part of the)
Artin
L
-function associated to
\boldsymbol{\chi}
can be written as the following:

L_{\matheur{A}}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{irr.~}\mathfrak{p}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}\\
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\operatorname{Frob}_{\mathfrak{p}})}{|\mathfrak{p}|_{\infty}^{s}}\right)^{-1},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1.

Let
L(s,\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f})
be the
Dirichlet
L
-function associated to the character
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}:(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}

(see
[
Rosen02
, equality (1) in p. 11 and Proposition 4.3]
):

\displaystyle L(s,\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{irr.~}\mathfrak{p}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}\\
\mathfrak{p}\nmid\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}}\end{subarray}}\left(1-\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}(\mathfrak{p})}{|\mathfrak{p}|_{\infty}^{s}}\right)^{-1},\quad\operatorname{Re}(s)>1.

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-q^{1-s}},&\text{ if $\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}$ is trivial;}\\
&\\
\displaystyle\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\afk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}},\ \deg\afk< \deg\cfk_{\bochi_f}}{(\afk, \cfk_{\bochi_f}) = 1}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}(\mathfrak{a})}{|\mathfrak{a}|_{\infty}^{s}},&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Write

\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,1)=q^{-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}\quad\text{ where }\quad s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\ln q}\cdot\frac{d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}{\ell}\quad\text{ for a unique integer $d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}$ with $0\leq d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}<\ell$.}

By (
3.4.6
) we have that

L_{\matheur{A}}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})=L(s+s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}},\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f})=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-q^{1-{(s+s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}}},&\text{ if $\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}$ is trivial;}\\
&\\
\displaystyle\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\afk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}},\ \deg\afk< \deg\cfk_{\bochi}}{(\afk, \cfk_{\bochi}) = 1}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\mathfrak{a},\deg\mathfrak{a})}{|\mathfrak{a}|_{\infty}^{s}},&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

For
\mathfrak{m}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\mid\mathfrak{m}\mid\mathfrak{n}
, put

(4.2.5)

\displaystyle L_{\matheur{A}}^{\mathfrak{m}}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=

\displaystyle L_{\matheur{A}}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\text{irr.~} \pfk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\pfk\mid\mfk,\ \pfk\nmid\cfk_{\bochi}}}(1-\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\mathfrak{p},\deg\mathfrak{p})}{|\mathfrak{p}|_{\infty}^{s}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-q^{1-(s+s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}}\cdot\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\text{irr.~} \pfk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\pfk\mid\mfk}}(1-\frac{1}{|\mathfrak{p}|^{s+s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}),&\text{ if $\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}$ is trivial;}\\
&\\
\displaystyle\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\afk\in\eA_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}},\ \deg\afk< \deg\mfk}{(\afk, \mfk) = 1}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\mathfrak{a},\deg\mathfrak{a})}{|\mathfrak{a}|_{\infty}^{s}},&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

We derive the following:

Lemma 4.2.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Take
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and
\mathfrak{m}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{m}\mid\mathfrak{n}
.
For each
a\in\matheur{A}
with
\text{\rm gcd}(a,\mathfrak{m})=1
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq c<\ell
, we have that when
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}
is non-trivial,

(4.2.7)

\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}=\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,c+\deg\mathfrak{m})\cdot\begin{cases}L_{\matheur{A}}^{\mathfrak{m}}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}),&\text{ if $\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\mid\mathfrak{m}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

and when
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}
is trivial,

(4.2.8)

\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(0,\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}=\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,c)\cdot L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}).

Proof.

When

\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\nmid\mathfrak{m}
, there exists
\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}
with
\alpha\equiv 1\bmod\mathfrak{m}
such that
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}(\alpha)\neq 1
.
Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho\varrho_{\alpha,0})\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho\varrho_{\alpha,0})}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}(\alpha)\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)},

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}=0
.

When
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}
is non-trivial with
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\mid\mathfrak{m}
,
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}}\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}}\langle\frac{\alpha(\theta)a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{m}(\theta)},i+c\rangle\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\alpha,i)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,c)\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathfrak{a}\in\matheur{A}_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}},\ \deg\mathfrak{a}<\deg\mathfrak{m},\\
\text{gcd}(\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{m})=1\end{subarray}}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\mathfrak{a},\deg\mathfrak{a}-\deg\mathfrak{m})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{m})^{\times}}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,c+\deg\mathfrak{m})\cdot L_{\matheur{A}}^{\mathfrak{m}}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}),

where the last equality follows from (
4.2.5
).

Finally, when
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}
is trivial, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\operatorname{St}(0,\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\varrho)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}

\displaystyle=\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\left(-\langle\frac{q^{c+i}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}\cdot q^{-is_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\cdot\frac{q^{-cs_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}{1-q^{\ell}}\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}q^{i(1-s_{\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}})}

\displaystyle=\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,c)\cdot L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}).

∎

Remark 4.2.9
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
Put
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}/\matheur{k})
.
Then we may identify
\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
, the Pontryagin dual of
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
, with the subgroup of
\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
consisting of all characters
\boldsymbol{\chi}
satisfying that
\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho_{\epsilon,c})=1
for all
\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
and
c\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
.
By
[
Rosen02
, Proposition 14.12]
, we get that for each
\boldsymbol{\chi}^{+}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
,

\ord_{s=0}L{A}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi}^{+})=1\quad\text{ if and only if \quad$\boldsymbol{\chi}^{+}$ is non-trivial.}

Denote by
O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and
O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
, respectively.
Let
\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(s)
and
\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}}(s)
be the Dedekind-Weil zeta functions of
O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and
O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}
, respectively, i.e.

\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{nonzero ideal}\\
\mathfrak{A}\subset O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\#(O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}/\mathfrak{A})^{s}}\quad\text{and}\quad\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}}(s)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\text{nonzero ideal}\\
\mathfrak{A}^{+}\subset O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\#(O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}/\mathfrak{A}^{+})^{s}},\ \operatorname{Re}(s)>1.

Then it is known that (cf.
[
Rosen02
, Proposition 14.11]
)

\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(s)=\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}}(s)\cdot\prod_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}L{A}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})\quad\text{ and }\quad\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}}(s)=\prod_{\boldsymbol{\chi}^{+}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}^{+}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}L{A}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi}).

As
\ord_{s=0}\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(s)=[\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}:\matheur{k}]-1=\ord_{s=0}\zeta_{O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}^{+}}(s)
(see
[
Rosen02
, Theorem 14.4]
), we obtain that:

Corollary 4.2.10
.

For
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
,
L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=0
if and only if

\text{$\boldsymbol{\chi}$ is non-trivial \hskip 10.22217ptand }\quad\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho_{\epsilon,c})=1,\quad\forall\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\text{ and }c\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}.

Proof of Proposition
4.2.4
.

By Remark
4.2.3
(4), it suffices to prove (3).
Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
be the space of
\mathbb{C}
-valued functions on
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
,
which is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra under the convolution product:

f_{1}*f_{2}(\varrho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\varrho_1,\varrho_2 \in\eG_{\nfk,\ell}}{\varrho_1\varrho_2 = \varrho}}f_{1}(\varrho_{1})f_{2}(\varrho_{2}).

For each character
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, the element
\#(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})^{-1}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}
is an idempotent in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
, and

\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\bigoplus_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\mathbb{C}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

In particular, for
\phi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
and
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, one has

\phi*\boldsymbol{\chi}=(\phi\mid\boldsymbol{\chi})_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi},\quad\text{ where }\quad(\phi\mid\boldsymbol{\chi})_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\phi(\varrho)\bar{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\varrho),

and so

(4.2.11)

\phi=\sum_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\phi*\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}}{\#(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})}=\frac{1}{\#\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\cdot\sum_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(\phi\mid\boldsymbol{\chi})_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

Let
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
be the subspace spanned over
\mathbb{C}
by
\operatorname{St}(x,y)
in
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
for all
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
.
As
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
is invariant under the action of
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
,
Lemma
4.2.6
implies that

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\bigoplus_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\bochi\in\widehat{\eG}_{\nfk,\ell}}{ L_{\eA}(0,\overline{\bochi}) \neq 0}}\mathbb{C}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}=\Bigl\{\varphi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})\ \Big|\ (\varphi\mid\boldsymbol{\chi})_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}=0,\ \forall\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\text{ with }L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=0\Bigr\}.

For
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, by Corollary
4.2.10
we have that the condition
L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=0
is equivalent to saying that
\boldsymbol{\chi}
is non-trivial and
\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho_{\epsilon,c})=1
for every
(\epsilon,c)\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
.
Therefore

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\left\{\varphi\in\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})\ \Bigg|\ \sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\varphi(\varrho\varrho_{\epsilon,c})\text{ is independent of $\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}$}\right\}.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
be the space of
\mathbb{Q}
-valued functions on
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
The above description of
\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
and (
3.4.7
) shows the desired identity:

\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})\cap\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{Q}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\sum_{x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A}\ \sum_{y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{Q}\cdot\operatorname{St}(x,y).

∎

Remark 4.2.12
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
For each CM field
\matheur{K}
contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
,
Let
\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}{K})
be the space of
\mathbb{C}
-valued functions on
\matheur{G}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})
.
The above proof also shows that

\mathbb{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})\cong\mathcal{F}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}{K})\cap\mathcal{H}_{\mathbb{C}}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}\\
L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}})\neq 0\end{subarray}}\mathbb{C}\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

In particular, let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{+}/\matheur{k})
.
For each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}{K})
, by Corollary
4.2.10
and (
4.2.11
) we have the following expression:

\varphi=\frac{1}{\#\matheur{G}{K}}\cdot\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\varphi(\varrho)\right)+\frac{1}{\#\matheur{G}{K}}\cdot\sum_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\varphi(\varrho)\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

This will be used in Section
6.3
.

4.3.
Universal distributions associated to diamond bracket relations

I. The arithmetic case

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}
be the free abelian group generated by all elements in
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
.
We identify
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}
with a subgroup of
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}
.
Every element in
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}

(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) can be written uniquely as a formal sum

\boldsymbol{y}=\sum_{y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}}n_{y}[y],\quad\text{where $n_{y}\in\mathbb{Z}$ (resp.\ $\mathbb{Q}$) and $n_{y}=0$ for almost all $y$.}

Given
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
)
be the subgroup of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}
(resp. subspace of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) generated by
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
.
Also, we let
\mathcal{R}^{\text{ari}}_{\ell}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{A}^{\text{ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
generated by

[y]-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}[\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}],\quad\forall y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z},

where
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
with

0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
so that

\langle-y\rangle_{\text{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)
.
Put
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}
and

\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{ari}}
, where

\mathcal{R}^{\text{ari}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\cup_{\ell}\mathcal{R}^{\text{ari}}_{\ell}
.
We call
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}
the
universal distribution associated to the arithmetic diamond bracket relations
.

Lemma 4.3.1
.

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}\leq\ell
for each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.

Proof.

As
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}
is generated by the images of
[\displaystyle\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}]
for
0\leq i<\ell
, the result follows.
∎

On the other hand,
extending
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}
to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear homomorphism from
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
(resp.
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}})
),
by Proposition
4.2.4
(1) we have

Lemma 4.3.2
.

For each
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathcal{A}^{\text{\rm ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
is surjective.
Consequently,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathcal{A}^{\text{\rm ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\text{\rm ari}})

is surjective.

From Remark
4.2.3
(1), one has that
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{ari}})=0
, which says that the surjection
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
factors through
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}
.
Since
\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})=\ell
by Remark
3.2.10
, combining Lemma
4.3.1
and
4.3.2
we obtain

Corollary 4.3.3
.

The map from
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}
to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})
induced by
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism.
Consequently,

(4.3.4)

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\ell}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{1,\ell})=\ell,

and we have a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism

\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}\cong\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{ari}})
.

II. The geometric case

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}
be the free abelian group generated by all elements in
k/A
.
We may identify
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}
with a subgroup of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}
.
Every element in
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
)
can be written uniquely as a formal sum

\boldsymbol{x}=\sum_{x\in k/A}n_{x}[x],\quad\text{where $n_{x}\in\mathbb{Z}$ (resp.\ $\mathbb{Q}$) and $n_{x}=0$ for almost all $x$.}

Given
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
, let
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n},\mathbb{Q}}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
spanned by elements in

\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
.
Also, we let
\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the subspace generated by

[\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\cdot x]-\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk'}}[x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}}],\quad\forall x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\in A_{+}\text{ with }\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mid\mathfrak{n}(\theta),

and

\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}[\epsilon x],\quad\forall x\in(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A)\setminus\{0\}.

Put
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n},\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n}}
and

\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{geo}}
, where

\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{geo}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\cup_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathcal{R}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\operatorname{geo}}
.
We call
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}
the
universal distribution associated to the geometric diamond bracket relations.

Lemma 4.3.5
.

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathfrak{n}}\leq 1+\displaystyle(1-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(A/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}
for each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
, where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=1
if
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

This is similar to Lemma
4.3.11
when taking
\ell=1
.
We postpone the proof to there.
∎

On the other hand,
extending
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}
to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear homomorphism from
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})
(resp.
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}})
),
by Proposition
4.2.4
(2) we have

Lemma 4.3.6
.

For each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}:\mathcal{A}^{\text{\rm geo}}_{\mathfrak{n},\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})
is surjective.
Consequently,

\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}:\mathcal{A}^{\text{\rm geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\text{\rm geo}})

is surjective.

Notice that
\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})=\displaystyle 1+(1-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}
by Remark
3.2.10
.
From a similar approach as in the arithmetic case, we obtain

Corollary 4.3.7
.

The map from
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n}}
to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})
induced by
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism.
Consequently,

(4.3.8)

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathfrak{n}}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},1})=\displaystyle 1+(1-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

and we have a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism

\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{geo}}\cong\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{geo}})
.

III. The two-variable case

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
be the free abelian group generated by elements in
(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})
,
and regard
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
as a subgroup of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{Q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
.
Every element in
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) can be written uniquely as

\mathbf{a}=\sum_{x,y}m_{x,y}\cdot[x,y],\quad m_{x,y}\in\mathbb{Z}\ \text{ (resp.\ $\mathbb{Q}$)}

and

m_{x,y}=0
for almost all
(x,y)\in(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})
.
Given
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
generated by
[x,y]
for all
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
.
Also, we let
\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
generated by

(4.3.9)

[x,y]-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}[x,\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}]\quad\text{ and }\quad[\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}x,|\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}|_{\infty}y]-\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk'}}[x+\frac{a}{\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}},y],

for all
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
,

\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\in A_{+}
with
\mathfrak{n}^{\prime}\mid\mathfrak{n}(\theta)
, and

y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\text{ with }\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)

where
0\leq y_{1},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
,
and

(4.3.10)

\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}[\epsilon x,q^{i}y],\quad\forall x\in(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A)\setminus\{0\}\text{ and }y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}.

Put
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}/\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
, where

\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\cup_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathcal{R}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
We call
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
the
universal distribution associated to the two-variable diamond bracket relations
.

Lemma 4.3.11
.

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\leq 1+\displaystyle(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}

for each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}
is defined in Lemma
4.3.5
.

Proof.

When
\deg\mathfrak{n}=0
, i.e.
\mathfrak{n}=1
, observe that
\mathcal{U}_{1,\ell}^{\operatorname{cyc}}=\mathcal{U}_{\ell}^{\operatorname{ari}}
, and the result follows from Lemma
4.3.1
.
Suppose
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
.
Write
\mathfrak{n}(\theta)=\mathfrak{p}_{1}^{e_{1}}\cdots\mathfrak{p}_{s}^{e_{s}}
, where
\mathfrak{p}_{1},...,\mathfrak{p}_{s}\in A_{+}
are distinct monic irreducible polynomials.
Adapting the argument in
[
La90
, Theorem 9.1 and 9.2]
(see also
[
Ku79
]
),
the multiplication relation in (
4.3.9
) shows that
the space
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
is generated by the images of
[\displaystyle x,\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}]
in
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for all integers
i
with
0\leq i<\ell
and
x
in the following set:

\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{s}\frac{a_{j}}{\mathfrak{p}_{j}^{e_{j}}}\ \Bigg|\ 1\neq a_{j}\in A,\ \deg a_{j}<\deg\mathfrak{p}_{j}^{e_{j}},\text{ and either }\ \mathfrak{p}_{j}\nmid a_{j}\ \text{ or }\ a_{j}=0\right\}.

Moreover, the reflection relation in (
4.3.10
)
allows us to drop
\displaystyle\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{q-1}-1
elements in the above generating set of
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\ell\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}-\left(\frac{\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}{q-1}-1\right)

\displaystyle=1+(\ell-\frac{1}{q-1})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}

as desired.
∎

On the other hand,
extending
\operatorname{St}
to a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear homomorphism from
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
(resp.
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
) to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
(resp.
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
),
by Proposition
4.2.4
(c) we have

Lemma 4.3.12
.

For each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,

\operatorname{St}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
is surjective.
Consequently,

\operatorname{St}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})

is surjective.

Notice that

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=1+\displaystyle(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}
by Remark
3.2.10
.
We then obtain

Corollary 4.3.13
.

The map from
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
to
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
induced by
\operatorname{St}
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism.
Consequently,

(4.3.14)

\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})=\displaystyle 1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

and we have a
\mathbb{Q}
-linear isomorphism

\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\cong\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
.

Remark 4.3.15
.

(1)

Corollary
4.3.3
,
4.3.7
, and
4.3.13
say that the Stickelberger distributions
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\operatorname{St}
are actually “universal” with respect to the corresponding diamond bracket relations.

(2)

Extending the action of
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
to
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,

\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and

\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, respectively,
induces isomorphisms in Corollary
4.3.3
,
4.3.7
, and
4.3.13
, which are in fact
\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
-equivariant.

In the next section, we shall
compare the compositions
\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
,
\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
,
and
\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\operatorname{St}:\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}

for a particular embedding
\nu_{1}:\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}

with the corresponding “gamma distributions”.

5.
Gamma distributions

5.1.
Generalized CM types corresponding to Stickelberger distributions

Given
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
,
recall that
C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z)
is the
\mathfrak{n}
-th Carlitz cyclotomic polynomial given in Section
3.4
.
Let
U_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the affine smooth curve in the
(t,z)
-plane over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
defined by
C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z)=0
, (i.e.
O_{\mathfrak{n}}
is the affine coordinate ring of
U_{\mathfrak{n}}
), and
X_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the projective model of
U_{\mathfrak{n}}
over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
The embedding
\matheur{k}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
induces a finite morphism
\pi_{\mathfrak{n}}
from
X_{\mathfrak{n}}
to
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, the projective
t
-line over
\mathbb{F}_{q}
.
Let
\infty
be the point at infinity in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
There exists
\#(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}/(q-1)
points in
X_{\mathfrak{n}}
lying above
\infty
, and each point is totally ramified over
\infty
with the inertia group equal to the image of
\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
under the Artin map (see (
3.4.2
)). In particular, all of the points of
X_{\mathfrak{n}}
lying above
\infty
are
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-rational.
Finally, for
\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{m}|\mathfrak{n}
, the inclusion map
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{m}}\hookrightarrow\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
given by

O_{\mathfrak{m}}=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]}{(C_{\mathfrak{m}}^{*}(t,z))}\hookrightarrow O_{\mathfrak{n}}=\frac{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t,z]}{(C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z))},\quad z\bmod C_{\mathfrak{m}}^{*}(t,z)\longmapsto C_{\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{m}}(t,z)\bmod C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(t,z),

corresponds to a morphism
\pi_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}/X_{\mathfrak{m}}}:X_{\mathfrak{n}}\rightarrow X_{\mathfrak{m}}
.

Let
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
be the base changes of
U_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
X_{\mathfrak{n}}
to
\bar{k}
, respectively.
The
Carlitz exponential function

\exp_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
is given by:

\exp_{\mathcal{C}}(z)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=z\cdot\prod_{a\in A-\{0\}}\left(1-\frac{z}{\tilde{\pi}\cdot a}\right),\quad\forall z\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty},

where
\tilde{\pi}
is the Carlitz fundamental period given in Remark
3.1.8
.
For each
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
, take

\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\exp_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}\right)\quad\in\bar{k}.

One has that
C_{\mathfrak{n}}^{*}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})=0
, and
C_{a}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})=C_{b}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})
for
a,b\in\matheur{A}
with
a\equiv b\bmod\mathfrak{n}
.
All points in
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
lying above the point
\theta
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{/\bar{k}}
are precisely

\xi_{\mathfrak{n},\alpha}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\big(\theta,C_{a}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})\big)\ (\in\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}}),\quad\text{ where }\alpha=a\bmod\mathfrak{n}\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}.

Note that the evaluation at the point
\xi_{\mathfrak{n},\alpha}
gives an embedding
\nu_{\mathfrak{n},\alpha}:K_{\mathfrak{n}}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
, and

\nu_{\mathfrak{n},\alpha}=\nu_{\mathfrak{n},1}\circ\varrho_{\alpha},\quad\forall\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

where
\varrho_{\alpha}\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is the automorphism corresponding to
\alpha
via the Artin map in (
3.4.1
).
In particular, for
\mathfrak{m}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{m}|\mathfrak{n}
, one has that
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{m}}}(\xi_{\mathfrak{n},1})=\xi_{\mathfrak{m},1}
.
This induces a unique embedding
\nu_{1}:\matheur{K}^{\rm cyc}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}
so that

(5.1.1)

\nu_{1}\big|_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}=\text{id}_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\nu_{1}\big|_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{n},1},\quad\forall\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}.

Moreover, let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
For
(\alpha,c)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
,
put
\nu_{\alpha,c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{n},1}\circ\varrho_{\alpha,c}
where
\varrho_{\alpha,c}
is given in (
3.4.5
).
Then

\nu_{\alpha,c}\big|_{\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}=\text{Frob}_{q}^{c_{\ell}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\nu_{\alpha,c}\big|_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\nu_{\mathfrak{n},\alpha}.

Set

U_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}U_{\mathfrak{n}}\quad\subset\quad X_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X_{\mathfrak{n}},

and

\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}U_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}=\coprod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}\quad\subset\quad\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}=\coprod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\bar{k}\underset{\text{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\times}\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}})\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}U_{\mathfrak{n}}\hskip-19.91684pt

\displaystyle\subset\quad\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\bar{k}\underset{\text{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\times}\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}})\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X_{\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle\,(\cong\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}U_{\mathfrak{n}}=\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}})

\displaystyle\,(\cong\bar{k}\times_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}X_{\mathfrak{n}}=\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}).

Moreover,
J_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}=\{\xi_{\alpha,c}\mid\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\text{ and }c\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\}
, where
\xi_{\alpha,c}\in\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(\ell-c)}
corresponds to
the embedding of
\nu_{\alpha,c}:\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
In particular, the point
\xi_{\alpha,c}
actually coincides with
\xi_{\alpha}
when identifying
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(\ell-c)}
with
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.

Now, for
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and an integer
c
with
0\leq c<\ell
,
we set

\displaystyle\Xi_{x,c}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\varrho_{\alpha,i}\star x\,,\,\varrho_{\alpha,i}\star\frac{q^{c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x,\frac{q^{i+c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}.

We also put

\displaystyle\Xi_{x}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\varrho_{\alpha,i}\star x\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{\operatorname{geo}}\cdot\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}/\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}}(\xi_{\alpha}),

and

\displaystyle\Xi_{\text{ari},c}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\varrho_{\alpha,i}\star\frac{q^{c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\text{ari}}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}
