\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\frac{q^{i+c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\text{ari}}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{c-1}q^{j}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)_{j-c+\ell}+\sum_{j=c}^{\ell-1}q^{j}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)_{j-c}.

Here for
0\leq i\leq\ell-1
,
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)_{i}\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(\ell-i)})
is the divisor
corresponding to
\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)

under the identification
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(\ell-i)}\cong\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
In particular, by Remark
4.1.6
(3) one has that
\Xi_{x}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{x,c}
.
In general, for each
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
, write

\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}q^{c}/(q^{\ell}-1)
where
y_{0},...y_{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
.
We set

\Xi_{(x,y)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}\Xi_{x,c},\quad\Xi_{y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}\Xi_{\operatorname{ari},c},

and

\Phi_{x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi_{x}-\Xi_{1/(q-1)},\quad\Phi_{(x,y)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Xi_{(x,-y)}-\Xi_{-y}.

From the above construction, the following lemma is straightforward:

Lemma 5.1.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
we have that

\Xi_{x}
,
\Xi_{y}
, and
\Xi_{(x,y)}
are generalized CM types of
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
,
and
\Phi_{x},\ \Phi_{(x,y)}\in I_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}^{0}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)^{-1}\varphi_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Xi_{x}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(-y)=\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(y)

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)^{-1}\varphi_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Xi_{y}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}_{0}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)^{-1}\varphi_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Xi_{(x,y)}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)^{-1}\varphi_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Phi_{x}},

\displaystyle\operatorname{St}(x,y)

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)^{-1}\varphi_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Phi_{(x,y)}}.

5.2.
Shtuka functions

To construct a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Xi)

where
\Xi=\Xi_{x}
,
\Xi_{y}
, or
\Xi_{(x,y)}
,
we shall find the corresponding “shtuka functions” and
apply the geometric approach in
[
BCPW22
, Appendix B]
.
First, recall the following result:

Theorem 5.2.1
.

(
[
ABP04
, Section 6.3.9]
)

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
.
Given nonzero
x
in
\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
, there exists a regular function

g_{x}=1+\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}t^{i}z^{j}\in O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}=\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\mathfrak{n}}
where
c_{ij}\in\bar{k}
with
|c_{ij}|_{\infty}<1
satisfying that

\operatorname{div}(g_{x})=-\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(N)}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}),

where

\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\tilde{\infty} \in X_\nfk}{\scaleto{\pi}{3.3pt}_{\!\scaleto{X_\nfk/\PP^1}{6.3pt}}(\tilde{\infty}) = \infty}}\tilde{\infty}.

Remark 5.2.2
.

Let
n
be an integer.
For each rational function
f
on
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
(i.e.
f
is in
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
’s function field
\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}(t)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}(t)}\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
),
let
f^{(n)}
be the
q^{n}
-power Frobenius twist of
f
, i.e.
f^{(n)}
is obtained by raising every coefficient of
f
to the
q^{n}
-power.
Moreover, for each (
\bar{k}
-)point
P\in\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
,
its
q^{n}
-power Frobenius twist
is the point
P^{(n)}
on
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
obtained by raising every coordinate of
P
to the
q^{n}
-power.
Extending the
q^{n}
-power Frobenius twist additively to the divisors on
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
,
by Theorem
5.2.1
we have

\operatorname{div}(g_{x}^{(n)})=\operatorname{div}(g_{x})^{(n)}=-\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(n+N)}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}).

Given nonzero
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
0\leq c<\ell
,
we may write

\Xi_{x,c}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N\geq 0}{N \equiv-1-i-c \bmod\ell}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,i}=\Xi_{x,c,(0)}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{x,c,(i)},

where

\Xi_{x,c,(0)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N\geq 0}{N \equiv-1-c \bmod\ell}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,0}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}),

and for
1\leq i<\ell
,

\Xi_{x,c,(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N\geq 0}{N \equiv-1-(\ell-i)-c \bmod\ell}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha,\ell-i}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}).

In particular, put

\displaystyle\Xi_{x,c}^{\#}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\left(\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{N\geq 0}{N \equiv-1-i-c \bmod\ell}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\right)\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i)}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}})

\displaystyle(=\Xi_{x,c,(0)}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell-1}\Xi_{x,c,(i)}^{(i-\ell)}\quad\text{when identifying $\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}$ with $\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}$ for $0\leq i<\ell$})

\displaystyle=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-c-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{-1-i-c+\ell}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i)}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\ \sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\left(\sum_{N^{\prime}\geq 1}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{-1-i-c+\ell+\ell N^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i)}\Bigg].

Take

W_{x,c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\left(\sum_{N^{\prime}\geq 1}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{-1-i-c+\ell+\ell N^{\prime}}\sum_{j=0}^{N^{\prime}-1}\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i+\ell j)}\right)\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}})

and
h_{x,c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(g_{x}^{(1+c-\ell)})^{q^{\ell}-1}\in O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}
.
Under the identification between
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}
,
we obtain that

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(h_{x,c})

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{N=0}^{\infty}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(1+c-\ell+N)}

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N\geq 0\\
N\equiv-1-i-c\bmod\ell\end{subarray}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{N}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(1+c-\ell+N)}

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\Bigg[\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-c-1}\sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{-1-i-c+\ell}\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i)}

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\ \sum_{\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}\left(\sum_{N^{\prime}\geq 1}\langle\alpha(\theta)\cdot x\rangle_{-1-i-c+\ell+\ell N^{\prime}}\right)\cdot\xi_{\alpha}^{(-i+\ell N^{\prime})}\Bigg]

(5.2.3)

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}\ +\ \Xi_{x,c}^{\#}\ +\ W_{x,c}^{(\ell)}\ -\ W_{x,c}\hskip 34.14322pt\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}).

For convenience, we also put
h_{0,c}=1
,
\Xi_{0,c}^{\#}=W_{0,c}=0
for
0\leq c<\ell
.

In the arithmetic case,
we put

\Xi_{\text{ari},c}^{\#}=\sum_{i=0}^{c-1}q^{i}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)^{(c-i-\ell)}+\sum_{i=c}^{\ell-1}q^{i}\cdot\pi_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}^{*}(\theta)^{(c-i)}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}).

Take

\displaystyle h_{\text{ari},c}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=0}^{c-1}(1-\frac{t}{\theta^{q^{c-i-\ell}}})^{q^{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=c}^{\ell-1}(1-\frac{t}{\theta^{q^{c-i}}})^{q^{i}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=0}^{c-1}(1-\frac{t^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{q^{c-\ell}}})\cdot\prod_{i=c}^{\ell-1}(1-\frac{t^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{q^{c}}})\quad\in\bar{k}[t]\subset O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}

and

W_{\text{ari},c}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=0\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}})
.
Note that
\pi^{*}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}/\mathbb{P}^{1}}(\infty)=(q-1)\cdot\infty_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}}
and
\ord_{\infty}(h_{\operatorname{ari},c})=-\displaystyle\frac{q^{\ell}-1}{q-1}
.
Under the identification between
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
and
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}
, we have

(5.2.4)

\operatorname{div}(h_{\text{ari},c})=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\Xi_{\text{ari},c}^{\#}+W_{\text{ari},c}^{(\ell)}-W_{\text{ari},c}\quad\in\Div(\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}).

In general, let
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
and write

\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}q^{c}/(q^{\ell}-1)
where
y_{0},...y_{\ell-1}\in\mathbb{Z}
with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
.
We set

\Xi^{\#}_{x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\Xi^{\#}_{x,c},\quad\Xi^{\#}_{y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}\Xi^{\#}_{\operatorname{ari},c},\quad\Xi^{\#}_{(x,y)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}\Xi^{\#}_{x,c},

W_{x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}W_{x,c},\quad W_{y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}W_{\text{ari},c},\quad W_{(x,y)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}y_{c}W_{x,c},

and

h_{x}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}h_{x,c},\quad h_{y}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}h_{\text{ari},c}^{y_{c}},\quad h_{(x,y)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}h_{x,c}^{y_{c}}.

Then:

Proposition 5.2.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
, we have that

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(h_{x})

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\ell\text{\rm wt}_{0}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\Xi_{x}^{\#}+W_{x}^{(\ell)}-W_{x},

\displaystyle\operatorname{div}(h_{y})

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\ell\text{\rm wt}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{0}(y)\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\Xi_{y}^{\#}+W_{y}^{(\ell)}-W_{y},

\displaystyle\text{and }\quad\operatorname{div}(h_{(x,y)})

\displaystyle=-(q^{\ell}-1)\cdot\ell\text{\rm wt}_{0}(x,y)\infty_{X_{\mathfrak{n}}}+\Xi_{(x,y)}^{\#}+W_{(x,y)}^{(\ell)}-W_{(x,y)}.

5.3.
Generalized soliton dual
t
-motives

We now apply the geometric method from
[
BCPW22
, (B.2.1)]
to construct the desired CM dual
t
-motives.
Set

O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\mathfrak{n}}\quad\text{ and }\quad O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\cong\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(i)},

where for
0\leq i<\ell
,

O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bar{k}\underset{\text{Frob}_{q}^{-i},\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}}{\otimes}O_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}\quad(\cong\bar{k}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O_{\mathfrak{n}}=O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}})

is the affine coordinate ring of
\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}
.
The field of fractions of
O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(i)}
is denoted by
\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}
, which is the function field of
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}
.

Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
let
*
be either
x
,
y
, or
(x,y)
, and
set

\matheur{M}(*)=\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*})}=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(i)},

where

\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(0)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\big(\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}}(-W_{*}^{(\ell)})\big)\quad\subset O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(0)},

and

\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma\big(\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}}(-W_{*}^{(\ell-i)}+\sum_{j=1}^{i}\Xi_{*,(j)}^{(j-i)})\big)\quad\subset\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)},\quad 1\leq i<\ell.

The
\sigma
-action on
\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*})}
is given by

\sigma\cdot(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Big(h_{*}\cdot m_{(\ell-1)}^{(-1)},m_{(0)}^{(-1)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-2)}^{(-1)}\Big),\quad\forall(m_{(0)},\ldots,m_{(\ell-1)})\in\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*})}.

By
[
BCPW22
, Theorem B.2.3]
, we have the following:

Lemma 5.3.1
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A

and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
take
*
to be either
x
,
y
, or
(x,y)
.

\matheur{M}(*)
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},\Xi_{*})
over
\bar{k}
.

In particular, Theorem
5.2.1
shows that:

Lemma 5.3.2
.

Keep the notation from Lemma
5.3.1
.
Let
\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}[t]}O_{\mathfrak{n}}
.
The infinite product

\psi_{*}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{j=1}^{\infty}\left(h_{*}^{-1}\right)^{(\ell j)}\quad\text{ lies in }\quad\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\times},

and

\tilde{\psi}_{*}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\psi_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)})\in H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M}(*))\subset\mathbb{M}(*)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(*).

Proof.

The proof is adapted from
[
ABP04
, 6.4.2]
.
We include the details for completeness.

The support of
W^{(\ell)}_{*}
in
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
is contained in the pre-image of
\{t=\theta^{q^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
.
Thus when identifying
O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(0)}
with
O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}
,
there exist
f_{1},...,f_{\ell}\in\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
(the function field of
\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}
) with possible poles lying over
\{t=\theta^{q^{i}}\mid i\in\mathbb{N}\}
and
m_{1},...,m_{\ell}\in\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(0)}
such that

f_{1}m_{1}+\cdots+f_{\ell}m_{\ell}=1\in O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}.

Then the restriction on the possible poles of
f_{1},...,f_{\ell}
forces that
f_{1},...,f_{\ell}\in\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}
, which means that
1\in\mathbb{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(0)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(0)}
and

\mathbb{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(0)}=\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}.

Moreover, by Remark
5.2.2
we are able to show that
h_{*}^{(\ell j)}
lies in
\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\times}
for all
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
and
the condition on the coefficients of
g_{x}
in Theorem
5.2.1
ensures that the infinite product
\psi_{*}
lies in
\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}^{\times}
.

To prove the second statement,
observe that when identifying
\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(i)}
with
\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}
for
1\leq i<\ell
,
one has that

\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(i)}\supset\Gamma\big(\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-W_{*}^{(\ell-i)})\big),

whence

\mathbb{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(i)}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}_{(W_{*},h_{*}),(i)}\ \supset\mathbb{T}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}\Gamma\big(\mathbf{U}_{\mathfrak{n}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{X}_{\mathfrak{n}}}(-W_{*}^{(\ell-i)})\big)=\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n}}\ \ni\psi^{(-i)}.

In particular,

(\psi_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)})\in\mathbb{M}(*)
.
Finally, it is straightforward that

\sigma\cdot(\psi_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)})=(\psi_{*}^{(-\ell)}h_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)})=(\psi_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)}).

Therefore
(\psi_{*},\psi_{*}^{(-1)},...,\psi_{*}^{(1-\ell)})\in H_{\text{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(*))
.
∎

Remark 5.3.3
.

Identifying
O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(0)}
with
O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n}}}
,
the above proof actually shows that

\psi_{*}\in\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}\otimes_{\bar{k}[t]}O_{\mathbf{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(0)}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}^{\dagger}.

This enables us to evaluate
\psi_{*}
at
\xi_{1,0}=(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})\in\mathbf{U}_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell},(0)}
:

Proposition 5.3.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given nonzero
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A

with
|x|_{\infty}<1
and
y=q^{c}/(q^{\ell}-1)

for an integer
c
with
0\leq c<\ell
, we have that

\psi_{(x,y)}(\xi_{1,0})=\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a = c+\ell j}}\Big(1+\frac{x}{a}\Big)^{1-q^{\ell}}\right)

and

\psi_{y}(\xi_{1,0})=\prod_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{i=0}^{c-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{q^{c+\ell j}}})\cdot\prod_{i=c}^{\ell-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{q^{c+\ell+\ell j}}})\right)^{-1}.

Proof.

Given nonzero
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A
with
|x|_{\infty}<1
and a non-negative integer
N
,
from
[
ABP04
, 6.3.7]
one has that

g_{x}^{(N+1)}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})=\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\deg a = N}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right).

Thus for
0\leq c<\ell
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
,
we get

(h_{x,c}^{-1})^{(\ell j)}(\xi_{1,0})=g_{x}^{(c+1-\ell+\ell j)}(\theta,\lambda_{\mathfrak{n}})^{1-q^{\ell}}=\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\deg a = c+\ell(j-1)}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right)^{1-q^{\ell}}.

Moreover, from the definition of
h_{\operatorname{ari},c}
it is straightforward that

(h_{\operatorname{ari},c}^{-1})^{(\ell j)}(\xi_{1,0})=\left(\prod_{i=0}^{c-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{c-\ell+\ell j}})\prod_{i=c}^{\ell-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{i}}}{\theta^{q^{c+\ell j}}})\right)^{-1}.

Therefore the result holds.
∎

Remark 5.3.5
.

Using the notation in Proposition
5.3.4
,
we get in particular that

\psi_{x}(\xi_{1,0})=\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\psi_{(x,\frac{q^{c}}{q^{\ell}-1})}(\xi_{1,0})=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A_{\scaleto{+}{4pt}}}{\deg a = i}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right)^{1-q^{\ell}}=\prod_{a\in A_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right)^{1-q^{\ell}}.

5.4.
Algebraic relations among special gamma values

Let
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
be the ring of
p
-adic integers (i.e. the completion of
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
).
Put
-A_{+}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{-a\mid a\in A_{+}\}
and

A_{+,i}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\{a\in A_{+}\mid\deg a=i\}\quad\text{for each non-negative integer $i$.}

Recall the following
factorial functions
(see
[
Go88
]
and
[
T91
]
): for
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus-A_{+}
and
y=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}y_{i}q^{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
0\leq y_{i}<q
for each
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\displaystyle\Pi_{\text{ari}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{j=0}^{i-1}(1-\frac{\theta^{q^{j}}}{\theta^{q^{i}}})\right)^{y_{i}},

\displaystyle\Pi_{\text{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{a\in A_{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}}}\left(1+\frac{x}{a}\right)^{-1},

\displaystyle\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{i=0}^{\infty}\left(\prod_{a\in A_{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\displaystyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-1.00002pt}{\resizebox{532786}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.83336pt}{$\textstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{559561}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.29166pt}{$\scriptstyle+$}}}}}{\raisebox{-0.57141pt}{\resizebox{643572}{262144}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.20833pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle+$}}}}}},i}}\Big(1+\frac{x}{a}\Big)^{-y_{i}}\right),

\displaystyle\Pi(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y)}{\Pi_{\text{ari}}(y)}.

In particular, for
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus-A_{+}
one has that

\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,\frac{1}{1-q})=\Pi_{\text{geo}}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad\Pi(x,\frac{1}{1-q})=\frac{\Pi_{\text{geo}}(x)}{\Pi_{\text{ari}}(1/(1-q))}.

The corresponding
gamma functions
are defined as follows:

x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}y_{i}q^{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with
0\leq y_{i}<q
for
i\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\text{ari}}(y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Pi_{\text{ari}}(y-1),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\text{geo}}(x)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{x}\cdot\Pi_{\text{geo}}(x),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{x}\cdot\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y-1),

\displaystyle\Gamma(x,y)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{1}{x}\cdot\Pi(x,y-1)=\frac{\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y)}{\Gamma_{\text{ari}}(y)}.

In particular, for
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
one has that

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,1-\frac{1}{q-1})=\Gamma_{\text{geo}}(x)\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(x,1-\frac{1}{q-1})=\Gamma_{\text{geo}}(x)\Big/\Gamma_{\text{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q-1}).

Remark 5.4.1
.

The original
Goss gamma function
is equal to (see
[
Go88
, Theorem 4.2.6]
):

\Gamma_{\text{Goss}}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(-x,-y)\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y).

From the reflection formula (cf.
[
T91
, Theorem 1.4 and Lemma 2.3]
):

\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-y)=\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\biggl(1-\frac{\theta}{\theta^{q^{i}}}\biggr)^{-1}=(-\theta)^{\frac{-q}{q-1}}\cdot\tilde{\pi},

we get that

\Gamma_{\text{Goss}}(x,y)=(-\theta)^{\frac{-q}{q-1}}\cdot\tilde{\pi}\cdot\Pi(-x,-y)=(-\theta)^{\frac{-q}{q-1}}\cdot\tilde{\pi}\cdot(-x)\cdot\Gamma(-x,1-y).

We list the monomial relations among gamma values in the following (see
[
T91
]
for arithmetic gamma values and
[
Go88
]
for two-variable gamma values):

Proposition 5.4.2
.

(1)

For
x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,

\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-y)\sim\tilde{\pi}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma(x,y)\cdot\Gamma(x,1-y)\sim\frac{\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)^{q-1}}{\tilde{\pi}}.

(2)

For
x\in k\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
,
a\in A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(a,y)=\Gamma(a,y)\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(N)\sim\Gamma(x,N)\sim 1.

(3)

Given
x\in k\setminus A
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
,
a\in A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
,

\Gamma(x+a,y+N)\sim\Gamma(x,y)\quad\text{ and }\quad\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y+N)\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y).

(4)

Given
x\in k\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
,
write
\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}y_{i}q^{i}/(q^{\ell}-1)
where
y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}
are integers with
0\leq y_{0},...,y_{\ell-1}<q
.
Then

\Gamma(x,y)\sim\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma(x,1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{y_{i}}\quad\text{ and }\quad\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\sim\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{y_{i}}.

(5)

Given
x\in k\setminus A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
,

\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\prod_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\Gamma(\epsilon x,q^{c}y)\sim 1\quad\text{ and }\quad\prod_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(q^{c}y)\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\ell\text{\rm wt}(y)/(q-1)}.

(6)

For
x\in k\setminus A
,
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, and

\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
,

\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\Gamma(\frac{x+a}{\mathfrak{n}},y)\sim\Gamma(x,|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}y).

Remark 5.4.3
.

Since

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)=\Gamma(x,1-\frac{1}{q-1})\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q-1}),\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),

we have the following relations for geometric gamma values (see also
[
ABP04
]
):

(1)

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(a)\sim 1,\quad\forall a\in A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})\quad\text{ and }\quad\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x+a)\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x);

(2)

Given
x\in k\setminus A
and
\mathfrak{n}\in A_{+}
,

\prod_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\epsilon x)\sim\tilde{\pi}\quad\text{ and }\quad\prod_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{a \in A}{\deg a < \deg\nfk}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\frac{x+a}{\mathfrak{n}})\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\cdot\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{|\mathfrak{n}|_{\infty}-1}{q-1}}.

For each
x\in k_{\infty}
, put
\{x\}\in k_{\infty}
to be the fractional part of
x
, i.e.
|\{x\}|_{\infty}<1
and
x\equiv\{x\}\bmod A
.
We may view
\{\cdot\}
as a function on
k_{\infty}/A
.
Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
The formulas for the evaluations of
\psi_{x},\psi_{\operatorname{ari},c},\psi_{x,c}\in\mathbb{O}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,(0)}^{\dagger}
at
\xi_{1,0}

in Proposition
5.3.4

for every
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
0\leq c<\ell

give us the following key identities:

Proposition 5.4.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,
we have that

\psi_{x}(\xi_{1,0})=\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(\{x\})^{q^{\ell}-1},\quad\psi_{y}(\xi_{1,0})=\Pi_{\text{\rm ari}}(-\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}})^{q^{\ell}-1},

and

\psi_{(x,y)}(\xi_{1,0})=\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(\{x\},-\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}})^{q^{\ell}-1},\quad\left(\frac{\psi_{(x,y)}(\xi_{1,0})}{\psi_{y}(\xi_{1,0})}\right)=\Pi(\{x\},-\langle y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}})^{q^{\ell}-1}.

Finally, define
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}:\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
,

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}:k/A\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
,
and

\tilde{\Gamma}:(k/A)\times(\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
as follows:
for
x\in k/A
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}/\mathbb{Z}
,

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}),\quad\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\Gamma(\{x\},1-\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}),&\text{ if $x\neq 0\in k/A$;}\\
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)^{-1},&\text{ otherwise,}\end{cases}

and

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\Gamma}(\{x\},\frac{1}{1-q})=\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q-1})^{-1}\cdot\begin{cases}\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\{x\}),&\text{ if $x\neq 0\in k/A$;}\\
1,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

We may extend
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\tilde{\Gamma}

to
\mathbb{Q}
-linear homomorphisms from
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{ari}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
,
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{geo}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
, and
\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathbb{Q}}
to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
, respectively, and their compositions with the quotient map
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}
are denoted by
\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\hat{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\hat{\Gamma}
, respectively.
The above proposition
leads us to the following analogue of Deligne’s theorem on gamma distributions (stated in
[
And82
, Theorem 4.7]
):

Theorem 5.4.5
.

\hat{\Gamma}_{\text{\rm ari}}=\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}},\quad\hat{\Gamma}_{\text{\rm geo}}=\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}},\quad\text{ and }\quad\hat{\Gamma}=\mathscr{P}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\circ\text{\rm St}.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lemma
5.1.2
, we have that for
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(y)^{q^{\ell}-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(\xi_{1,0},\Xi_{\operatorname{ari},-y})=\psi_{\operatorname{ari},-y}(\xi_{1,0})\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(x)^{q^{\ell}-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(\xi_{1,0},\Phi_{x})=\left(\frac{\psi_{\operatorname{geo},x}(\xi_{1,0})}{\psi_{\operatorname{ari},1/(q-1)}(\xi_{1,0})}\right)\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times},

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\circ\operatorname{St}(x,y)^{q^{\ell}-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}}(\xi_{1,0},\Phi_{(x,y)})=\left(\frac{\psi_{x,-y}(\xi_{1,0})}{\psi_{\operatorname{ari},-y}(\xi_{1,0})}\right)\cdot\bar{k}^{\times}\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}/\bar{k}^{\times}.

Comparing with the definition of
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\tilde{\Gamma}
,
the result thereby follows from Proposition
5.4.4
.
∎

Remark 5.4.6
.

For each
\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
, we let
\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi)\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
be an arbitrary representative of
\mathscr{P}_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi)
.
Then Theorem
5.4.5
says that for
\boldsymbol{y}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\operatorname{ari}}
,
\boldsymbol{x}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\operatorname{geo}}
, and
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{Q}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
,

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{ari}}(\boldsymbol{y})\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{ari}}(\boldsymbol{y})\big),\quad\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}(\boldsymbol{x})\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}^{\operatorname{geo}}(\boldsymbol{x})\big),\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\Gamma}(\mathbf{a})\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a})\big).

Theorem 5.4.7
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
We have:

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\ \Gamma(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(A/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}.

where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}=1
if
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
and
0
otherwise.

Proof.

Notice that for
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
and
0\neq x\in k/A
,

\tilde{\Gamma}(0,y)=\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\langle-y\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}})^{-1}\quad\text{ and }\quad\tilde{\Gamma}(x,\frac{1}{1-q})=\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\{x\})\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q-1})^{-1}.

Thus by Proposition
5.4.2
(1)–(3) we get

\displaystyle\ \bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y),\Gamma(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \bar{k}\Big(\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A,\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\Big).

From Proposition
4.2.4
(3), Theorem
5.4.5
, and Remark
5.4.6
, we know that the field

\bar{k}\big(\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi)\mid\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})\big)

is algebraic over
\bar{k}\Big(\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A,\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\Big)
.
Hence

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A,\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\big(\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi)\mid\varphi\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(A/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\quad\text{(by Theorem~\ref{thm: trdeg})}.

∎

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
By (
4.3.14
) we have

1+(\ell-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot\#(A/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}=\dim_{\mathbb{Q}}(\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})

where
\mathcal{U}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
is defined in Section
4.3
III.
As
\mathfrak{n}
and
\ell
are chosen arbitrarily, we arrive at:

Corollary 5.4.8
.

All algebraic relations among gamma values
\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)
,
\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
, and

\Gamma(x,y)
for
x\in\matheur{k}\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})
and
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
are explained by the monomial relations listed in
Proposition
5.4.2
.

Remark 5.4.9
.

(1)

Taking
\ell=1
in the above equalities, we re-prove the Lang-Rohrlich conjecture for geometric gamma values in
[
ABP04
, Corollary 1.2.2]
:

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\tilde{\pi},\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\})\Big)=1+(1-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}=1
if
\deg\mathfrak{n}>0
and
0
otherwise.

(2)

Taking
\mathfrak{n}=1
in the above equalities, we re-prove
[
CPTY10
, Corollary 3.3.3]
:

\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\ \Big|\ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big)=\ell.

(3)

Since

\tilde{\Gamma}_{\operatorname{geo}}(x)\sim\tilde{\Gamma}(x,\frac{1}{1-q})=\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\tilde{\Gamma}(x,\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}),

We get that

\displaystyle\ \trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(x),\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\ \Big|\ x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A\setminus(-A_{+}\cup\{0\}),\ \ y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ell+(1-\frac{1}{(q-1)^{\epsilon_{\mathfrak{n}}}})\cdot(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times},

and so all algebraic relations among arithmetic and geometric gamma values come from their relations with the Carlitz period
\tilde{\pi}
.

6.
The Chowla–Selberg phenomenon

Theorem
5.4.5
enables us to connect the “CM periods”, when the CM field is contained in a cyclotomic function field, with special gamma values.
In this section, we shall first recall the relationship between the periods of dual
t
-motives and the periods of “abelian
t
-modules”, and then apply Theorem
5.4.5
to derive an analogue of the Chowla–Selberg formula.

6.1.
Quasi-periods of CM abelian
t
-modules

An
abelian
t
-module of dimension

d>0
over
\bar{k}
is a pair
(E,\rho)=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}E_{\rho}
with the following properties.

•

E
is a
d
-dimensional additive group
{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\bar{k}}
defined over
\bar{k}
.

•

\rho:\matheur{A}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}({\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\bar{k}})
is an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism so that
\partial\rho_{t}-\theta I_{d}
acts nilpotently on
\Lie(\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d})
, where
\partial\rho_{t}
denotes the differential of the homomorphism
\rho_{t}:{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\bar{k}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{G}_{a}^{d}}_{/\bar{k}}
.

•

\mathscr{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(E,\mathbb{G}_{a})
is finitely generated over
\bar{k}[t]
with respect to the
\matheur{A}
-module structure via
\rho
.

It is known that
\mathscr{M}(\rho)
must be free of finite rank over
\bar{k}[t]
, and
\operatorname{rank}_{\bar{k}[t]}\mathscr{M}(\rho)
is called the
rank of
E_{\rho}
.
Set

(6.1.1)

\matheur{M}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\bar{k}[t]}(\tau\mathscr{M}(\rho),\bar{k}[t]dt)

which is constructed by Hartl-Juschka
[
HJ20
]
,
where
\tau
is identified with the
q
-power Frobenius endomorphism on
\mathbb{G}_{a}
.
It is known that
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a dual
t
-motive (and
E_{\rho}
is always “
A
-finite”, see
[
BCPW22
, Theorem 2.3.2]
).
We call
\matheur{M}(\rho)
the
Hartl-Juschka dual
t
-motive associated with
E_{\rho}
.

Suppose that
E_{\rho}
is uniformizable,
in which case so is
\matheur{M}(\rho)
, see
[
BCPW22
, Theorem 2.4.3]
.
Let
\Lambda_{\rho}
be the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
and let
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})
be the de Rham module of
E_{\rho}
(see
[
BP02
, §3.1]
and
[
NP21
, Proposition 4.1.3]
).
Let
[\cdot,\cdot]:H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\times\Lambda_{\rho}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
be the de Rham pairing introduced in
[
NP21
, (4.3.3) and (4.3.4)]
.
We call
[\delta,\lambda]
for
\delta\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})

and
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
a
quasi-period of
E_{\rho}
.
Recall that we have the following natural comparison between the associated de Rham pairings
(cf.
[
BCPW22
, Proposition 8.3.4]
):

(6.1.2)

\textstyle{[\ ,\ ]:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(E_{\rho},\bar{k})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{\Lambda_{\rho}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\sim

\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\int:}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}(\rho),\bar{k})}
\textstyle{\times}
\textstyle{H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}(\rho))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
\textstyle{-z}

We say that
E_{\rho}
is a
CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
if
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
.
In this case, the above comparison of de Rham pairings implies that the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
coincides with (cf.
[
BCPW22
, Thm. 8.4.1]
)

\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}\bar{k}\cdot p{K}(\xi,\Xi).

In particular, suppose
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
be the Stickelberger function associated to the CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
given in (
3.2.8
).
Then Lemma
4.3.12
guarantees that there exists
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
such that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}=\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a})\quad\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}).

In particular, consider
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
whose corresponding embedding
\nu_{\xi_{0}}
is
\nu_{1}\big|_{\matheur{K}}
(where
\nu_{1}:\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}
is chosen in (
5.1.1
)).
As
\matheur{K}
is Galois over
\matheur{k}
, one has

J{K}=\{\xi_{0}^{\varrho}\mid\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{H}{K}\}
.
By Theorem
3.1.9
(2) the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
becomes

(6.1.3)

\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\bar{k}\cdot p{K}(\xi_{0}^{\varrho},\Xi)=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\bar{k}\cdot p{K}(\xi_{0},\Xi^{\varrho^{-1}})=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\bar{k}\cdot\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho^{-1}}}).

Since
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho^{-1}}}=\varrho\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}=\operatorname{St}(\varrho\star\mathbf{a})
for every
\varrho\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
, by Theorem
5.4.5
(and Remark
5.4.6
)
we obtain

Theorem 6.1.4
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
, where
\matheur{K}
is contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{H}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{K})
.
There exists
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
such that the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by

\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho\star\mathbf{a})\ (\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varrho\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi})),\quad\varrho\matheur{H}{K}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{H}{K}.

6.2.
Period vectors of CM Hilbert–Blumenthal
t
-modules

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field with maximal totally real subfield denoted by
\matheur{K}^{+}
, and let

\Xi
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Write
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
, where
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
.
Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
By
[
BCPW22
, Section 8.5]
,
E_{\rho}
is actually a Hilbert-Blumenthal
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
-module with CM by
O_{\matheur{K}}
.
Put
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}(\rho)
.
Let
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{1}},...,\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{d}}
be the differentials of
\matheur{M}
associated with
\xi_{1},...,\xi_{d}
, respectively.
From the natural
\bar{k}
-linear isomorphism
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\matheur{M}/\sigma\matheur{M}
(see
[
BCPW22
, (8.5.1)]
),
we get a
\bar{k}
-linear isomorphism
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
with respect to
\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{1}},...,\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{d}}
.
Moreover, the image of every period vector
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
under this isomorphism is

(\int_{\gamma_{\lambda}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{1}},...,\int_{\gamma_{\lambda}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{1}})\quad\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d},

where
\gamma_{\lambda}\in H_{\rm Betti}(\matheur{M})
is the cycle corresponding to
\lambda
via the natural isomorphism in the comparison diagram (
6.1.2
).

Suppose
\matheur{K}
is contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
By Lemma
4.3.12
, there exists
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
so that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}=\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a})\quad\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\rm cyc}).

Let
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
with
\nu_{\xi_{0}}=\nu_{1}\big|_{\matheur{K}}
.
For
1\leq i\leq d
, take
\varrho_{i}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
so that
\nu_{1}\circ\varrho_{i}\big|{K}=\nu_{\xi_{i}}
.
Write
\Lambda_{\rho}=\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}\cdot\lambda_{0}
where
\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
is an ideal of
O{K}
.
By Theorem
5.4.5
and
6.1.4
we have that

(6.2.1)

\int_{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}}\omega_{\matheur{M},\xi_{i}}\sim p{K}(\xi_{i},\Xi)\sim p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi_{0},\Xi^{\varrho_{i}^{-1}})\sim\wp^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\big(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{i}^{-1}}}\big)\sim\wp^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\big(\varrho_{i}\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}\big)\sim\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho_{i}\star\mathbf{a}).

Therefore:

Theorem 6.2.2
.

Let

\matheur{K}
be a CM field whose maximal totally real subfield is denoted by
\matheur{K}^{+}
.
Suppose
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Put
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
.
Let
\Xi=\xi_{1}+\cdots+\xi_{d}
be a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
For
1\leq i\leq d
, take
\varrho_{i}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
so that
\nu_{1}\circ\varrho_{i}\big|{K}=\nu_{\xi_{i}}
.
Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
.
There exist
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
, an ideal
\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
of
O{K}
, and a suitable
\bar{k}
-linear isomorphism
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
so that
image of the period lattice
\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
is

\left\{\Big(\nu_{\xi_{1}}(a)\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho_{1}\star\mathbf{a}),...,\nu_{\xi_{d}}(a)\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho_{d}\star\mathbf{a})\Big)\ \bigg|\ a\in\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}\right\}.

Now, let
\matheur{K}
be an
imaginary field
over
\matheur{k}
(i.e. the infinite place
\infty
of
\matheur{k}
does not split in
\matheur{K}
).
Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
over
\bar{k}
which has CM by
O{K}
(the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
).
Extending
\rho
to an
\mathbb{F}_{q}
-algebra homomorphism (still denoted by)

\rho:O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}(E_{\rho})
, we have that the differential
\partial\rho:O{K}\rightarrow\End_{\bar{k}}(\Lie(E_{\rho}))
induces an embedding
\nu_{\rho}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Let
\xi_{\rho}\in J{K}
be the point corresponding to the embedding
\nu_{\rho}
.
We may regard
E_{\rho}
as a CM abelian
t
-module with CM type
(\matheur{K},\xi_{\rho})
.

Suppose that
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
(which is equivalent to saying that
\matheur{K}/\matheur{k}
is an abelian extension and the ramification index of
\infty
in
\matheur{K}
is divided by
q-1
from class field theory).
By Theorem
6.2.2
,
We are able to establish the following result which in particular verifies Thakur’s recipe/conjecture on the Chowla–Selberg phenomenon in

[
T91
]
:

Theorem 6.2.3
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module over
\bar{k}
with CM by
O{K}
, where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in an imaginary function field
\matheur{K}
with
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{H}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{K})
.
Take any
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
so that
\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{H}{K}}=\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a})\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}})
.
Then

\lambda\sim\wp^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}(\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{H}{K}})\sim\tilde{\Gamma}(\mathbf{a}),\quad\text{ for every nonzero period }\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}.

Moreover,

\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho\star\mathbf{a})\ (\sim\wp^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}(\varrho\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{H}{K}})),\quad\varrho\matheur{H}{K}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{H}{K}

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
and
form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of quasi-periods of the CM Drinfeld module
E_{\rho}
.

Proof.

As
\matheur{K}
is imaginary,
we have that
\matheur{K}^{+}=\matheur{k}
and the characteristic function
\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{H}{K}}
of
\matheur{H}{K}
is contained in
\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell})
. Hence
Lemma
4.3.12
assures us that there exist
\mathbf{a}\in\mathcal{A}^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}
so that

\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{H}{K}}=\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a}).

Let
\xi_{0}\in J{K}
such that
\nu_{\xi_{0}}=\nu_{1}\big|_{\matheur{K}}
.
Then Theorem
6.2.2
implies that

\lambda\sim p{K}(\xi_{0},\xi_{0})\sim\tilde{\Gamma}(\mathbf{a}),\quad\text{ for every nonzero period }\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}.

Moreover, Theorem
6.1.4
shows that the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by

\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho\star\mathbf{a}),\quad\varrho\matheur{H}{K}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{H}{K},

and

\displaystyle\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}(\tilde{\Gamma}(\varrho\star\mathbf{a})\mid\varrho\matheur{H}{K}\in\matheur{G}^{\operatorname{cyc}}/\matheur{H}{K})

\displaystyle=\trdeg_{\bar{k}}\bar{k}\Big(\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]\quad\text{ (by Proposition~\ref{thm: trdeg}).}

∎

Next, we shall provide explicit constructions for the
\mathbf{a}
’s in Theorem
6.1.4
, Theorem
6.2.2
, and Theorem
6.2.3
,
and present a function field analogue of the Chowla–Selberg formula.

6.3.
The Chowla–Selberg formula

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Identifying
\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
with
(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
via the Artin map as in (
3.4.5
),
every
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
can also be regarded as a character on
(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
.
We put
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}}
, where
\boldsymbol{\chi}_{f}=\boldsymbol{\chi}\big|_{(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}}
.
Recall that for
c\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
and
\alpha\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}
,
by Lemma
4.2.6

we have

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{St}(\frac{\alpha(\theta)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})\ \bigg|\ \boldsymbol{\chi}\right)_{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}},\ell}

\displaystyle=\sum_{(a,i)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})}\operatorname{St}(\frac{\alpha(\theta)}{c_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})(a,i)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i)}

(6.3.1)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\chi}(\alpha,c+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})\cdot L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}).

Therefore:

Lemma 6.3.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
For each character
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, the following equality holds:

\sum_{(a,i)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})=L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

Proof.

For every
(\alpha,c)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{n})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})
, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,i)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})(\alpha,c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{(a,i)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\times(\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z})}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i)}\cdot\left(\langle\frac{a(\theta)\alpha(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i+c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle-\langle\frac{q^{i+c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\rangle_{\operatorname{ari}}\right)\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\operatorname{St}(\frac{\alpha(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})\ \bigg|\ \boldsymbol{\chi}\right)_{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}},\ell}\cdot\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}=L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}})\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}(\alpha,c)

(by (
6.3
))

as desired.
∎

Given a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
which is contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, let
\matheur{G}{K}
be the Galois group
\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})
.
For a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
,
from the bijection (
3.2.3
)
we may write

(6.3.3)

\Xi=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot\xi_{\varrho},

where for each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
,
\xi_{\varrho}\in J{K}
is the point corresponding to the embedding
\nu_{1}\circ\varrho
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
and put
\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}^{+}/\matheur{k})
.
By Remark
4.2.12
, we may express

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}=\frac{1}{\#(\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}})}\cdot\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\right)+\frac{1}{\#(\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}})}\cdot\sum_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\chi}.

Notice that
\sum\limits_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]\cdot\wt(\Xi)
, and for
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
we have (by Remark
4.2.9
and Lemma
6.3.2
):

\boldsymbol{\chi}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}}{L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}).

Therefore

\displaystyle\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}

\displaystyle=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\!\!\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\varrho)\right)\!\!\cdot\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}{L{A}(0,\overline{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\rho}\!\!\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\bochi\in\widehat{\eG}_\eK\setminus\widehat{\eG}_{K^+}}{\cfk_{\bochi} = \cfk}}\!\!\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}).

For each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
, put

(6.3.4)

n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\bochi\in\widehat{\eG}_\eK\setminus\widehat{\eG}_{K^+}}{\cfk_{\bochi} = \cfk}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(\varrho)\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})},\quad\text{ which lies in $\mathbb{Q}$.}

Then

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi}=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}).

In general, we obtain that:

Proposition 6.3.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given a CM field
\matheur{K}
contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
and a generalized CM type
\Xi
of
\matheur{K}
, write
\Xi=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\xi_{\varrho}
as in (
6.3.3
).
For each
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, we have the following expression for the Stickelberger function
\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}
:

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}).

Proof.

Suppose
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
is given.
As

\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\xi_{\varrho}^{\varrho_{0}}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\xi_{\varrho\varrho_{0}}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho\varrho_{0}^{-1}}\xi_{\varrho},

we obtain that

\displaystyle\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho\varrho_{0}^{-1}}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}).

∎

Remark 6.3.6
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be a CM field and
\matheur{K}^{+}
its maximal totally real subfield.
Suppose that
\matheur{K}
is contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for some
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
It is known that (cf.
[
Rosen02
, Theorem 14.4]
)

\prod_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}\setminus\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=\frac{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})\cdot\mathcal{R}(O{K})}{w{K}\cdot\#\operatorname{Pic}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})\cdot\mathcal{R}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})},

where:

•

\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})
(resp.
\operatorname{Pic}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})
) is the ideal class group of
O{K}
(resp.
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
);

•

w{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\#(\mathbb{F}{K}^{\times})/\#(\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times})
, and
\mathbb{F}{K}
is the constant field of
\matheur{K}
;

•

\mathcal{R}(O{K})
(resp.
\mathcal{R}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})
) is the regulator of
O{K}
(resp.
O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
), see
[
Rosen02
, p. 245]
.

Let
h(\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+})
be the following “relative class number”:

h(\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})\cdot\mathcal{R}(O{K})}{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})\cdot\mathcal{R}(O_{\matheur{K}^{+}})}\quad\in\mathbb{N}.

Then for
\mathfrak{c}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}
,
a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}
,
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
, and
\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}
, the expression of
\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)
in (
6.3.4
) tells us in particular that

h(\matheur{K}/\matheur{K}^{+})\cdot\mathfrak{n}_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)\ \in\mathbb{Z}.

Proposition
6.3.5
leads us to the following Chowla–Selberg formula for quasi-periods of CM abelian
t
-modules:

Theorem 6.3.7
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a CM abelian
t
-module with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Xi)
over
\bar{k}
, where
\matheur{K}
is contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for some
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{G}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})
and write

\Xi=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\xi_{\varrho}\ \in I{K}^{0}\quad\text{ as in \eqref{eqn: Xi-varrho}}.

(1) The space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by

\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)}\right)^{\frac{m_{\varrho}}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}},\quad\text{ for }\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K},

where
n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)
is defined in (
6.3.4
).

(2) Suppose
\Xi
is a CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Let
\matheur{K}^{+}
be the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
, and put
d=[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]
.
Write
\Xi=\xi_{\varrho_{1}}+\cdots+\xi_{\varrho_{d}}
, and let
\nu_{\varrho_{j}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(\nu_{1}\big|{K})\circ\varrho_{j}
for
1\leq j\leq d
.
There exist an ideal
\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
of
O{K}
and a suitable
\bar{k}
-linear isomorphism
\Lie(E_{\rho})\cong\bar{k}^{d}
so that the
image of every period
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\Lie(E_{\rho})(\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
has the form
(\nu_{\varrho_{1}}(\alpha)\lambda_{1},...,\nu_{\varrho_{d}}(\alpha)\lambda_{d})\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{d}
,
where
\alpha\in\mathfrak{I}_{\rho}
and for
1\leq j\leq d
,

\lambda_{j}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{d}\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\frac{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho_{j^{\prime}}\varrho_{j}^{-1},a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}.

(3) Suppose further that
E_{\rho}
is a Drinfeld

\matheur{A}
-module over
\bar{k}
with CM by
O{K}
, where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in an imaginary field
\matheur{K}
.
Then

\lambda^{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\sim\tilde{\pi}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)}\quad\text{ for every nonzero period }\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty},

where

n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\text{\rm id}_{\matheur{K}},a,i)=\sum_{\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{\bochi\in\widehat{\eG}_\eK\setminus\widehat{\eG}_{K^+}}{\cfk_{\bochi} = \cfk}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}.

Moreover,

\varpi{K}^{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\frac{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}},\quad\text{ for }\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K},

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
and
form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of quasi-periods of the CM Drinfeld module
E_{\rho}
.

Proof.

For
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, take

\mathbf{a}_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)\right)\cdot[\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}]\quad\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell,\mathbb{Q}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}.

Proposition
6.3.5
implies that

(6.3.8)

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}=\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}{K}}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a}_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}}).

Theorem
6.1.4
implies that the space of quasi-period of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by

\displaystyle\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}(\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}})

\displaystyle\sim\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\left(\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}{K}}+\frac{1}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}\operatorname{St}(\mathbf{a}_{\matheur{K},\Xi^{\varrho_{0}}})\right)

\displaystyle\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{\wt(\Xi)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0},a,i)}\right)^{\frac{m_{\varrho}}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}},\quad\text{ for }\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}.

This completes the proof of (1).

Moreover, from the expression (
6.3.8
),
(2) and (3) follow directly from Theorem
6.2.2
and Theorem
6.2.3
, respectively.
∎

Example 6.3.9
.

(
Chowla–Selberg formula for constant field extensions
.)
Given
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, put
\matheur{K}=\matheur{K}_{1,\ell}=\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}(t)
.
Then

\matheur{G}{K}=\operatorname{Gal}(\matheur{K}/\matheur{k})\cong\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}/\mathbb{F}_{q})\cong\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
as in (
3.4.3
),

and
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=1
for every
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}
.
Put
\zeta_{\ell}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\exp(2\pi\sqrt{-1}/\ell)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
.
Recall that we write
\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,1)=q^{-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}=\zeta_{\ell}^{d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}
for each
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}
.
For
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}
, we calculate

n_{1}(1,i)=\sum_{1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(1,i)}{L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}=\sum_{d=1}^{\ell-1}\zeta_{\ell}^{id}(1-q\zeta_{\ell}^{d})=q-1+\begin{cases}\ell,&\text{ if $i=0$,}\\
-\ell q,&\text{ if $i=\ell-1$,}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
\ell
over
\bar{k}
with CM by
\mathbb{F}_{q^{\ell}}[t]
.
Theorem
6.3.7
(3) implies that
for every nonzero period
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
, we have that

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{\ell}}\cdot\left(\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\tilde{\Gamma}(0,\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{\frac{q-1}{\ell}}\right)\cdot\left(\tilde{\Gamma}(0,\frac{1}{1-q^{\ell}})\Big/\tilde{\Gamma}(0,\frac{q^{\ell-1}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{q}\right)

\displaystyle\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{q^{\ell-1}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{q}\Big/\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(1-\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}),

where the last equivalence comes from the monomial relations among gamma values in Proposition
5.4.2
(2) and (5).
In other words, the first statement of Theorem
6.3.7
(3) implies Thakur’s analogue of Chowla–Selberg formula in
[
T91
, Theorem 1.6]
.
Moreover,
the second statement of Theorem
6.3.7
(3) shows the result in
[
CPTY10
, Theorem 3.3.2]
that

\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}\Big(1-\frac{q^{c}}{q^{\ell}-1}\Big)^{q}\Big/\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}\Big(1-\frac{q^{c+1}}{q^{\ell}-1}\Big),\quad c=0,...,\ell-1,

are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
and
form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
.

Example 6.3.10
.

(
Chowla–Selberg formula for the
t
-th Carlitz cyclotomic function field.
)
Let
\matheur{K}=\matheur{K}_{t,1}=\matheur{k}(\sqrt[q-1]{-t})
, which is a geometric extension over
\matheur{k}
.
Then

\matheur{G}{K}\cong\left(\frac{\matheur{A}}{(t)}\right)^{\times}\cong\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times},\quad\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=t,\text{ and }\quad L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=1,\quad\forall\ 1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}.

Thus for
\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}
, we get

n_{t}(\epsilon,0)=\sum_{1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}}\boldsymbol{\chi}(\epsilon,0)=-1+\begin{cases}q-1,&\text{ if $\epsilon=1$,}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
q-1
over
\bar{k}
with CM by
\mathbb{F}_{q}[\sqrt[q-1]{-t}]
.
Theorem
6.3.7
(3) implies that for every nonzero period
\lambda\in\Lambda_{\rho}
, we have that

\lambda\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\cdot\left(\prod_{\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times}}\Gamma(\frac{\epsilon}{\theta},1-\frac{1}{q-1})^{\frac{-1}{q-1}}\right)\cdot\Gamma(\frac{1}{\theta},1-\frac{1}{q-1})\sim\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\frac{1}{\theta}),

where the last equivalence comes from Proposition
5.4.2
(5).
This matches Thakur’s formula in
[
T04
, Theorem 4.11.2]
.
Moreover, the second statement of Theorem
6.3.7
(3) shows that

\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}(\frac{\epsilon}{\theta}),\quad\epsilon\in\mathbb{F}_{q}^{\times},

form a
\bar{k}
-basis of the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
(coinciding with
[
T04
, Theorem 6.4.7 (2)]
when
q=3
), and that they are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

Example 6.3.11
.

(
Chowla–Selberg formula for imaginary quadratic extensions.
)
Here we apply Theorem
6.3.7
(3) to present an analogue of the Chowla–Selberg formula for CM Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-modules of rank
2
over
\bar{k}
.
Note that by the class field theory, an imaginary quadratic extension
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
is contained in
\matheur{K}^{\operatorname{cyc}}
if and only if the ramification index of the infinite place
\infty
of
\matheur{k}
in
\matheur{K}
divides
q-1
.
In this case, let
\mathfrak{d}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
be the generator of the discriminant ideal of
O{K}/\matheur{A}
(where
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
).
Then
\matheur{K}
is actually contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{d},2}
, and
\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}}
is a group of order
2
.

Let
\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}:\matheur{G}{K}\rightarrow\{\pm 1\}
be the unique non-trivial character in
\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}
.
Then
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}}=\mathfrak{d}
, and for
a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
, we get

n_{\mathfrak{d}}(\varrho,a,i)=\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{d})}{L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}\cdot\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $\varrho=\mathrm{id}_{\matheur{K}}$,}\\
-1,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Since
L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})/w{K}
(see Remark
6.3.6
), the first statement of Theorem
6.3.7
(3) implies that for every nonzero period
\lambda
of a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module of rank
2
over
\bar{k}
with CM by
O{K}
, we have

\lambda^{2\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}\cdot\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\Gamma(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{d}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{2}-1})^{w{K}\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{d})}.

Moreover, put

(6.3.12)

\varpi_{\matheur{K}}^{\pm}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sqrt{\tilde{\pi}}\cdot\prod_{(a,i)\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}\Gamma\Big(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{d}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{2}-1}\Big)^{\pm w{K}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{d})}{2\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}}.

The second statement of Theorem
6.3.7
(3) says that the space of quasi-periods of
E_{\rho}
is spanned over
\bar{k}
by
\{\omega{K}^{+},\omega{K}^{-}\}
, and
\omega{K}^{+},\omega{K}^{-}
are algebraically independent over
\bar{k}
.

Remark 6.3.13
.

Here we use the notation in Example
6.3.11
.
Assume further that
\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}(1,i)=1
for any
i\in\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
.
Put
\chi{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\boldsymbol{\chi}{K}\big|_{(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}}
.
Then
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{d},1}
, which is a Carlitz cyclotomic function field.
In particular, the infinite place
\infty
of
\matheur{k}
is totally ramified in
\matheur{K}
.
These ensure that
q
is odd,
\mathfrak{d}
is square-free with
\deg\mathfrak{d}
odd,
\matheur{K}=\matheur{k}(\sqrt{-\mathfrak{d}})
, and
w{K}=1
.
In this case, we may write

\varpi_{\matheur{K}}^{\pm}=\sqrt{\tilde{\pi}}\cdot\prod_{a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{d})^{\times}}\Gamma_{\operatorname{geo}}\left(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{d}(\theta)}\right)^{\pm\frac{\chi{K}(a)}{2\#\operatorname{Pic}(O{K})}},

and get a precise analogue of the classical Chowla–Selberg formula described by Gross in
[
Gr78
, equation (2) and (3)]
.

6.4.
The normalization of gamma values and a Lerch-type formula

Given a
p
-adic integer
y=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}y_{i}q^{i}\in\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, set (see
[
T91
, 3.6]
)

\partial(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}iy_{i}q^{i}\quad\in p\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

In particular, let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
For each integer
c
with
0\leq c<\ell
, one checks that

\partial(\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})=\frac{cq^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}}+\frac{\ell q^{\ell+c}}{(1-q^{\ell})^{2}}\quad\in\mathbb{Q}.

Moreover, given a character
\boldsymbol{\chi}:\matheur{G}_{1,\ell}\cong\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, we have the following “
L^{\prime}
-evaluator” property:
writing
\boldsymbol{\chi}(1)=q^{-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}
where
s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=-\frac{2\pi\sqrt{-1}}{\ln q}\cdot\frac{d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}{\ell}
for a unique integer
d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}
with
0\leq d_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}<\ell
,

\displaystyle\ \ln q\cdot\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\boldsymbol{\chi}(c)\cdot\partial(\frac{q^{c}}{1-q^{\ell}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ln q\cdot\left[\frac{1}{1-q^{\ell}}\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}cq^{c(1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}+\frac{\ell q^{\ell}}{(1-q^{\ell})^{2}}\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}q^{c(1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ln q\cdot\left[\frac{1}{1-q^{\ell}}\cdot\left(\frac{(1-q^{\ell(1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}\cdot q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}{(1-q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}})^{2}}-\frac{\ell q^{\ell(1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}}{1-q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}\right)+\frac{\ell q^{\ell}}{(1-q^{\ell})^{2}}\cdot\frac{1-q^{\ell(1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}}{1-q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \ln q\cdot\frac{q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}}}{(1-q^{1-s_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}})^{2}}\ =\ L{A}^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi}).

For each
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
, we follow
[
T91
, 3.6]
to normalize
\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)
by setting

(6.4.1)

\Gamma^{*}_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\theta^{-\partial(y-1)}\cdot\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)=\theta^{-\partial(y-1)}\cdot\Pi_{\operatorname{ari}}(y-1)

to obtain

Lemma 6.4.2
.

For
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{1,\ell}
we have that

L{A}^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=-\sum_{c=0}^{\ell-1}\boldsymbol{\chi}(c)\cdot\ln\left|\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}^{*}(1-\frac{q^{c}}{q^{\ell}-1})\right|_{\infty}.

Remark 6.4.3
.

One consequence of the above lemma is that

\displaystyle\ln\left|\tilde{\pi}\cdot\prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}^{*}(1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{-n_{1}(1,i)}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\frac{q\ln q}{q-1}+\ln q\cdot\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}n_{1}(1,i)\cdot\partial(\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta{A}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta{A}(0)}+\sum_{1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{1,\ell}}\frac{L{A}^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{O_{1,\ell}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{O_{1,\ell}}(0)}.

In order to extend the above result to arbitrary cyclotomic function fields, we define

\partial(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\langle x,-y\rangle,\quad\forall x\in k,\ y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}.

Then for each character
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
with
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\neq 1
, from the expression of
L_{A}^{\mathfrak{n}}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})
in (
4.2.5
) the following “
L^{\prime}
-evaluator” property holds:

-\!\!\!\!\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{n}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{n})=1\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{n})\cdot\partial(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})\cdot\ln\left|\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}\right|_{\infty}=(L_{\matheur{A}}^{\mathfrak{n}})^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})+L_{\matheur{A}}^{\mathfrak{n}}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})\cdot\ln|\mathfrak{n}(\theta)|_{\infty}.

We normalize the two-variable gamma values
\Gamma(x,y)
as follows:

(6.4.4)

\Gamma^{*}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\frac{x^{-\partial(x,y-1)}\cdot\Pi_{\operatorname{geo}}(x,y-1)}{\Gamma_{\operatorname{ari}}^{*}(x,y)}=x^{1-\partial(x,y-1)}\cdot\theta^{\partial(y-1)}\cdot\Gamma(x,y).

In particular,
\Gamma^{*}(0,y)=\Gamma^{*}_{\operatorname{ari}}(y)^{-1}
for all
y\in\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}
.
Then we have the following Lerch-type formula:

Theorem 6.4.5
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given a character
\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, the following equality holds:

L{A}^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})=-L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})\ln|\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}|_{\infty}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\\
\text{\rm gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})=1\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})\cdot\ln\left|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\right|_{\infty}.

Consequently, Theorem
6.4.5
leads us to:

Corollary 6.4.6
.

Let
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Given an imaginary field
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, we have that

\ln\left|\tilde{\pi}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
