\text{\rm gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\ \prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)}\right|_{\infty}=\frac{\zeta_{O{K}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{O{K}}(0)}+\ln|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty},

where
\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})\in\matheur{A}_{+}
is the monic generator of the discriminant ideal of
O{K}
over
\matheur{A}
.

Proof.

From the formula of
\tilde{\pi}
in Remark
3.1.8
one gets

\ln|\tilde{\pi}|_{\infty}=\frac{q\ln q}{q-1}=\frac{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}(0)}.

As
\matheur{K}
is imaginary, one has that
\matheur{K}^{+}=\matheur{k}
.
Thus for
\mathfrak{c}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}
,
a\in\matheur{A}
with
\deg a<\deg c
and
\text{gcd}(a,c)=1
, and
0\leq i<\ell
,

n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}\\
\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}=\mathfrak{c}\end{subarray}}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c})}{L_{\matheur{A}}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}.

Therefore we obtain that

\displaystyle\ln\left|\tilde{\pi}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\ \prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)}\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}(0)}+\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)\cdot\ln\left|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}(0)}+\sum_{1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})=1\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\frac{\boldsymbol{\chi}(a,i+\deg\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}\cdot\ln\left|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\right|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{\matheur{A}}(0)}+\sum_{1\neq\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}}\left(\frac{L{A}^{\prime}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}{L{A}(0,\boldsymbol{\chi})}+\ln|\mathfrak{c}_{\boldsymbol{\chi}}|_{\infty}\right)\hskip 56.9055pt\text{(by Theorem~\ref{thm: Lerch-type formula})}

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{O{K}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{O{K}}(0)}+\ln|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty},

where the last equality comes from the fact that
\zeta_{O{K}}(s)=\prod_{\boldsymbol{\chi}\in\widehat{\matheur{G}}{K}}L{A}(s,\boldsymbol{\chi})
and the conductor-discriminant formula.
∎

Remark 6.4.7
.

Let
\matheur{K}
be an imaginary field contained in
\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Take
\nu\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{1}\big|_{\matheur{K}}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\bar{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, and let
K\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu(\matheur{K})
(resp.
O_{K}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu(O{K})
).
Put
r=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
.
Every fractional ideal of
O_{K}
becomes a discrete
A
-lattice of rank
r
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
The Kronecker limit formula established in
[
Wei20
]
implies in particular that (see
[
Wei20
, (5.3)]
)

\frac{\zeta_{O{K}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{O{K}}(0)}=\frac{1}{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O_{K})}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}]\in\operatorname{Pic}(O_{K})}\ln\Big(N(\mathfrak{A})|\Delta(\mathfrak{A})|_{\infty}^{\frac{r}{q^{r}-1}}\Big),

where

•

N(\mathfrak{A})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\#(O_{K}/\mathfrak{A})
for each nonzero ideal
\mathfrak{A}
of
O_{K}
; and

•

\Delta(\cdot)
is the
Drinfeld discriminant function
(on
A
-lattices of rank
r
in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, see
[
Wei20
, p. 880]
).

Therefore Corollary
6.4.6
leads us to the following “analytic” Chowla–Selberg formula:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\#\operatorname{Pic}(O_{K})}\sum_{[\mathfrak{A}]\in\operatorname{Pic}(O_{K})}\ln\Big(N(\mathfrak{A})|\Delta(\mathfrak{A})|_{\infty}^{\frac{r}{q^{r}-1}}\Big)

(6.4.8)

\displaystyle=\ln\left(\frac{|\tilde{\pi}|_{\infty}}{|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty}}\right)+\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)\ln\Big|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\Big|_{\infty}.

Remark 6.4.9
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be a Drinfeld
\matheur{A}
-module over
\bar{k}
of rank
r
which has CM by
O{K}
, where
\matheur{K}
is an imaginary field of degree
r
over
\matheur{k}
.
Suppose that
\matheur{K}
is separable over
\matheur{k}
and tamely ramified at
\infty
.
Then the Colmez-type formula derived in
[
Wei20
, Theorem 1.6]
(together with
[
Wei20
, Remark 1.7 (3)]
) expresses the “stable Taguchi height of
E_{\rho}
” as follows:

h_{\mathrm{Tag}}^{\mathrm{st}}(E_{\rho})=-\frac{1}{2r}\cdot\ln|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty}-\frac{1}{r}\cdot\frac{\zeta_{O{K}}^{\prime}(0)}{\zeta_{O{K}}(0)}.

When
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
(
6.4.7
) implies that

\displaystyle h_{\mathrm{Tag}}^{\mathrm{st}}(E_{\rho})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2r}\cdot\ln|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty}-\frac{1}{r}\ln|\tilde{\pi}|_{\infty}

(6.4.10)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\ \sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\frac{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)}{r}\cdot\ln\Big|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\Big|_{\infty}.

Suppose further that
E_{\rho}
is “sgn-normalized” (see
[
Go98
, Chapter 7]
) and the period lattice of
E_{\rho}
is of the form:

\Lambda_{\rho}=O_{K}\cdot\varpi_{\rho},\quad\text{ where \quad$\varpi_{\rho}\in\mathbb{C}_{\infty}$.}

Then
\varpi_{\rho}
is unique up to
O_{K}^{\times}
-multiples, and we can show that

h_{\mathrm{Tag}}^{\mathrm{st}}(E_{\rho})=-\frac{1}{2r}\cdot\ln|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty}-\ln|\varpi_{\rho}|_{\infty}.

Combining this with (
6.4.9
), we arrive at the following “absolute” Chowla–Selberg formula:

(6.4.11)

|\varpi_{\rho}|_{\infty}^{r}=\frac{|\tilde{\pi}|_{\infty}}{|\mathfrak{d}(O{K}/\matheur{A})|_{\infty}}\cdot\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in\matheur{A},\ \deg a<\deg\mathfrak{c}\\
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1\end{subarray}}\ \prod_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\left|\Gamma^{*}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},1-\frac{q^{i}}{q^{\ell}-1})\right|_{\infty}^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(a,i)}.

7.
The Deligne–Gross period conjecture

In this section, we shall apply the work of Pink and Hartl–Juschka (in
[
Pink97
]
,
[
HP04
]
,
[
Jus10
]
, and
[
HJ20
]
) on the Hodge conjecture over function fields, to derive an analogue of the Deligne–Gross period conjecture.

7.1.
Hodge–Pink structures

Definition 7.1.1
.

Let
H
be a finite dimensional vector space over
\matheur{k}
. An
exhaustive and separated increasing
\mathbb{Q}
-filtration

W_{\bullet}H
is a collection of
\matheur{k}
-subspaces
W_{\mu}H\subset H
for
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
with
W_{\mu^{\prime}}H\subset W_{\mu}H
whenever
\mu^{\prime}<\mu
, such that the associated graded space

\Gr^{W}H\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigoplus_{\mu\in\mathbb{Q}}\Gr^{W}_{\mu}H,\quad\text{ where }\quad\Gr^{W}_{\mu}H\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=W_{\mu}H/(\cup_{\mu^{\prime}<\mu}W_{\mu^{\prime}}H),

has the same dimension over
\matheur{k}
as
H
.

Definition 7.1.2
.

(See
[
HJ20
, Definition 2.2.3]
.) A
(mixed)
\matheur{k}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structure
is a triple
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
, where

•

H
is a finite dimensional vector space over
\matheur{k}
;

•

W_{\bullet}H
is an exhaustive and separated increasing
\mathbb{Q}
-filtration;

•

\mathfrak{q}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H
is a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]
-lattice of full rank.

Here the tensor product is with respect to the following inclusion

\matheur{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(t)=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(t-\theta)\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!).

In particular,
\matheur{H}
is called
pure (of weight
\mu
)
if
H\cong\Gr^{W}_{\mu}H
for some
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Given two (mixed)
\matheur{k}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structures
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
and
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
, a
morphism

f:\matheur{H}\rightarrow\matheur{H}^{\prime}
is a
\matheur{k}
-linear homomorphism
f:H\rightarrow H^{\prime}
such that
f(W_{\mu}H)\subset W_{\mu}H^{\prime}
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
and the induced homomorphism
\mathrm{id}\otimes f:\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H\rightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H^{\prime}
satisfies
(\mathrm{id}\otimes f)(\mathfrak{q})\subseteq\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.1.3
.

In fact,
\matheur{k}
is contained in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]
, as every nonzero
a\in\matheur{A}
is contained in
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]^{\times}
.
In particular,

\frac{1}{t}=\big(\theta+(t-\theta)\big)^{-1}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{n}\cdot(\frac{1}{\theta})^{n+1}\cdot(t-\theta)^{n}.

It is known that (see
[
HJ20
, p. 39 and Lemma 2.1.3]
) the inclusion
\matheur{k}\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]
can be extended to an
\matheur{k}
-algebra embedding
\matheur{k}_{\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]
.
Thus it is natural to extend the above definition to
(mixed)
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structures
and the morphisms between them without difficulty.
Every
\matheur{k}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structure
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
can be extended to a
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structure

\matheur{H}_{\infty}=(H_{\infty},W_{\bullet}H_{\infty},\mathfrak{q})
where
H_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}H
and
W_{\mu}H_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}W_{\mu}H
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Definition 7.1.4
.

A (mixed)
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure is a (mixed)
\matheur{k}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structure
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})

satisfying the
local semistable condition (at
\infty
)
: for every
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-subspace
H_{\infty}^{\prime}
of
H_{\infty}
, put
W_{\mu}H_{\infty}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=H_{\infty}^{\prime}\cap W_{\mu}H_{\infty}
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
,
\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathfrak{q}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}H^{\prime}_{\infty}
, and
\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}H_{\infty}^{\prime}
.
Then

\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\right)-\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}\cap\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\right)\leq\sum_{\mu\in\mathbb{Q}}\mu\cdot\dim_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}\Gr^{W}_{\mu}H^{\prime}_{\infty},

and equality holds when
H^{\prime}_{\infty}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}W_{\mu}H
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
.

Remark 7.1.5
.

The local semistable condition can be also defined on
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structures, and we may say as well that a (mixed)
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-pre-Hodge–Pink structure is a (mixed)
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-Hodge–Pink structure if it satisfies the local semistable condition.
In particular, if
\matheur{H}
is a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure, then
\matheur{H}_{\infty}
is a
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-Hodge–Pink structure.

Given two (mixed)
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structures
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
and
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
,
let
f:\matheur{H}\rightarrow\matheur{H}^{\prime}
be a morphism.
The local semistable condition on
\matheur{H}
and
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
ensures that
f
must be
strict
(see
[
HJ20
, Remark 2.2.11]
), i.e.

f(W_{\mu}H)=f(H)\cap W_{\mu}H^{\prime},\quad\forall\mu\in\mathbb{Q},\quad\text{ and }\quad(\mathrm{id}\otimes f)(\mathfrak{q})=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}f(H)\cap\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}.

We denote by
\Hom_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H},\matheur{H}^{\prime})
the space of the morphisms from
\matheur{H}
to
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
, and
\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H})
the endomorphism ring of
\matheur{H}
.

Remark 7.1.6
.

Let
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
be a (mixed)
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure.
A
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure of
\matheur{H}
is a triple
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
, where

H^{\prime}
is a
\matheur{k}
-vector suspace of
H
,

W_{\mu}H^{\prime}=H^{\prime}\cap W_{\mu}H
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
, and

\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{q}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H^{\prime}
.
In particular, if
\matheur{H}
is pure of weight
\mu
, then so is
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
.

Example 7.1.7
.

One natural example of a pure
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure comes from pure uniformizable dual
t
-motives.
Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive of weight
\wt(\matheur{M})
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
. Recall in Section
3.1
that

H{M}=\Hom_{\matheur{A}}(H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\matheur{k})
.
Set

\mathfrak{q}{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\big(\sigma\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\big)\quad\text{ and }\quad W_{\mu}H{M}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{ if $\mu<-\wt(\matheur{M})$,}\\
H{M},&\text{ if $\nu\geq-\wt(\matheur{M})$.}\end{cases}

By
[
HJ20
, Theorem 2.4.32 and 2.3.34 (a)]
,
the triple
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(H{M},W_{\bullet}H{M},\mathfrak{q}{M})
is a pure Hodge–Pink structure of weight
\mu{M}
, where

\mu{M}=-w(\matheur{M})=\frac{\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\sigma]}\matheur{M}}{\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\matheur{M}}.

Moreover, given two pure uniformizable dual
t
-motives
\matheur{M}
and
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, every morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
(over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
) induces a morphism

f_{\mathrm{HP}}:\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\rightarrow\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}),

and
(f\mapsto f_{\mathrm{HP}})
gives a
\matheur{k}
-linear (resp.
\matheur{k}
-algebra) isomorphism (resp. when
\matheur{M}=\matheur{M}^{\prime}
) (see
[
HJ20
, Theorem 2.4.32 and 2.3.34 (b)]
)

\matheur{k}\otimes{A}\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M},\matheur{M}^{\prime})\cong\Hom_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime}),\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})).

The following fact comes from the function field analogue of the Hodge conjecture for dual
t
-motives (see
[
HJ20
, Theorem 2.4.32 and 2.3.34 (c)]
):

Theorem 7.1.8
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Given a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
, there exists a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
together with an essentially surjective morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
such that the corresponding morphism
f_{\mathrm{HP}}:\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\hookrightarrow\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
gives an isomorphism
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\cong\matheur{H}^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.1.9
.

It is worth pointing out that
the “effectiveness” of
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
(i.e.
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is indeed a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\sigma]
-module) comes from the “duality” in
[
HJ20
, Proposition 2.4.3]
, and the essential surjectivity of the morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
implies that
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is finitely generated over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\sigma]
.
Thus
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is indeed a dual
t
-motive in our setting.

7.2.
CM Hodge–Pink structures and CM dual
t
-motives

Let
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
be a pure
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure of weight
w(\matheur{H})
.
Put
\mathfrak{p}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H
, and identify

\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{(t-\theta)\mathfrak{p}}\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{v_{\theta},\matheur{k}}{\otimes}H=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}.

The
Hodge–Pink filtration
of
H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
is
F^{\bullet}H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(F^{i}H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
, where
F^{i}H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
is the subspace of
H_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
corresponding to

\frac{\mathfrak{p}\cap(t-\theta)^{i}\mathfrak{q}}{(t-\theta)\mathfrak{p}\cap(t-\theta)^{i}\mathfrak{q}}\quad\cong\quad\frac{\mathfrak{p}\cap(t-\theta)^{i}\mathfrak{q}+(t-\theta)\mathfrak{p}}{(t-\theta)\mathfrak{p}}\quad\subset\quad\frac{\mathfrak{p}}{(t-\theta)\mathfrak{p}}.

Let
r=\dim_{\matheur{k}}H
.
Take an integer
n
sufficiently large so that
(t-\theta)^{n}\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{q}
.
There exist
r
integers
w_{1},...,w_{r}
satisfying that

\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{(t-\theta)^{n}\mathfrak{p}}\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\frac{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]}{(t-\theta)^{n+w_{i}}},

which is independent of the choice of
n
.
We call
w_{1},...,w_{r}
the
Hodge–Pink weights of
\matheur{H}
.
In particular, the local semistability of
\matheur{H}
implies that

r\cdot w(\matheur{H})=w_{1}+\cdots+w_{r}.

Suppose further that
\matheur{H}
has
full-CM
by a CM field
\matheur{K}
, i.e.
\dim{k}H=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
and there exists a
\matheur{k}
-algebra homomorphism

(7.2.1)

\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}).

Then
\mathfrak{p}
becomes a free module of rank one over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}
.
Recall that
O{K}
denotes the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
, and here we put
O_{\mathbf{K}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\mathbb{F}_{q}}O{K}
.
Observe that

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{A}}O{K}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\xi},

where for each
\xi\in J{K}
,
\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}
is the completion of
O_{\mathbf{K}}
with respect to the maximal ideal
\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
and
\hat{\mathbf{K}}_{\xi}
is the field of fraction of
\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}
.
As
\mathfrak{q}
is also a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}
-module and there exists a sufficiently large integer
n
so that

(t-\theta)^{n}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{q}\subset(t-\theta)^{-n}\cdot\mathfrak{p}\quad\subset\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H,

we obtain that
\mathfrak{q}
is actually a free
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}
-module of rank one, and there exist unique integers
w_{\xi}
for
\xi\in J{K}
satisfying that

\frac{\mathfrak{q}}{(t-\theta)^{n}\mathfrak{p}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\frac{\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{n+w_{\xi}}\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\frac{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]}{(t-\theta)^{n+w_{\xi}}},

where the last isomorphism is due to that

(t-\theta)O_{\mathbf{K}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}
(i.e.
(t-\theta)
splits completely in
O_{\mathbf{K}}
).
Thus the numbers
w_{\xi}
for
\xi\in J{K}
coincide with the Hodge–Pink weights of
\matheur{H}
.

Definition 7.2.2
.

Let
\matheur{H}
be a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure with full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
.
The
Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}
is

\Phi_{\matheur{H}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}w_{\xi}\xi\quad\in I{K}.

In fact, we have the following:

Lemma 7.2.3
.

Let
\matheur{H}
be a pure
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure of weight
w(\matheur{H})
which has full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
.
Then
\Phi{H}
lies in
I{K}^{0}
with
\wt(\Phi{H})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H})
,
where
\matheur{K}^{+}
is the maximal totally real subfield of
\matheur{K}
.

Proof.

Write
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
, and denote by
w(\matheur{H})
be the weight of
\matheur{H}
.
Recall that we let
\matheur{H}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(H_{\infty},W_{\bullet}H_{\infty},\mathfrak{q})
where
H_{\infty}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}H
and
W_{\mu}H_{\infty}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}W_{\mu}H
, which is a
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-Hodge–Pink structure.
Then we have an embedding

\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}_{\infty}),

which makes
H_{\infty}
,
W_{\mu}H_{\infty}
for every
\mu\in\mathbb{Q}
, and
\mathfrak{q}
become
\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}\matheur{K}
-modules.
On the other hand, as
\matheur{K}
is a CM field over
\matheur{k}
, we have that

\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}\cong\prod_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\matheur{K}_{\infty,\xi^{+}},

where for each
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
,
\matheur{K}_{\infty,\xi^{+}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{k}_{\infty}\underset{\tilde{\nu}_{\xi^{+}},\matheur{K}^{+}}{\otimes}\matheur{K}
is the compositum of
\matheur{K}
and
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
over
\matheur{K}^{+}
with respect to the embedding

\tilde{\nu}_{\xi^{+}}:\matheur{K}^{+}\hookrightarrow\matheur{k}_{\infty}\quad\text{ so that }\quad v_{\theta}\circ\tilde{\nu}_{\xi^{+}}=\nu_{\xi^{+}}\in\Emb(\matheur{K}^{+},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}).

In particular, one has that

[\matheur{K}_{\infty,\xi^{+}}:\matheur{k}_{\infty}]=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]
for every
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
.

For each
\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}
, we take
e_{\xi^{+}}
to be the idempotent of
\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes{k}\matheur{K}
corresponding to
\matheur{K}_{\infty,\xi^{+}}
.
Then
e_{\xi^{+}}\matheur{H}_{\infty}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=(e_{\xi^{+}}H_{\infty},W_{\bullet}(e_{\xi^{+}}H_{\infty}),e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{q})
is a pure
\matheur{k}_{\infty}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure of
\matheur{H}_{\infty}
, and

\matheur{H}_{\infty}=\bigoplus_{\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}e_{\xi^{+}}\matheur{H}_{\infty}.

Moreover, comparing with the decomposition

\displaystyle\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}

\displaystyle\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K}\cong\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}(\matheur{k}_{\infty}\otimes_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{K})

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}\matheur{K}_{\infty,\xi^{+}}\cong\prod_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\underset{\tilde{\nu}_{\xi^{+}},\matheur{K}^{+}}{\otimes}\matheur{K}

\displaystyle\cong\prod_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\underset{\tilde{\nu}_{\xi^{+}},O_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}{\otimes}O_{\matheur{K}}\cong\prod_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}^{+}}}\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}\right),

we get

e_{\xi^{+}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J_{\matheur{K}}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}\hat{e}_{\xi},\quad\text{ where $\hat{e}_{\xi}$ is the
idempotent of $\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}$ corresponding to $\hat{O}_{\mathbf{K},\xi}$.}

Consequently, taking sufficiently large integer
n
so that
(t-\theta)^{n}\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathfrak{q}
, we obtain that

\frac{e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{q}}{(t-\theta)^{n}e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{p}}\cong\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}\frac{\hat{O}_{\matheur{K},\xi}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{n+w_{\xi}}\hat{O}_{\matheur{K},\xi}}.

The local semistable condition of
\matheur{H}
(at
\infty
) ensures that

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}w_{\xi}

\displaystyle=\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{q}}{e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{q}\cap e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{p}}\right)-\dim_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\left(\frac{e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{p}}{e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{q}\cap e_{\xi^{+}}\mathfrak{p}}\right)

(7.2.4)

\displaystyle\leq\dim_{\matheur{k}_{\infty}}(e_{\xi^{+}}H_{\infty})\cdot w(\matheur{H})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H}).

Since

\sum_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}}^{+}}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\in J{K}\\
\pi_{\mathbf{X}/\mathbf{X}^{+}}(\xi)=\xi^{+}\end{subarray}}w_{\xi}\right)=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}w_{\xi}=\dim_{\matheur{k}}(H)\cdot w(\matheur{H})=\sum_{\xi^{+}\in J_{\matheur{K}}^{+}}\bigg([\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H})\bigg),

the inequality (
7.2
) is actually an equality, whence
\Phi_{\matheur{H}}\in I{K}^{0}
with
\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H})
as desired.
∎

Remark 7.2.5
.

Let
\matheur{H}=(H,W_{\bullet}H,\mathfrak{q})
be a pure
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure of weight
w(\matheur{H})
which has full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
.
Suppose
\matheur{H}
is
effective
, i.e.

\mathfrak{p}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H\subset\mathfrak{q}.

Then
w_{\xi}\geq 0
for every
\xi\in J{K}
, for which
\Phi{H}
becomes a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
with

\wt(\Phi{H})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H}).

Proposition 7.2.6
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive of weight
w(\matheur{M})
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Given a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
, suppose that
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
has full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
.
Then
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
is effective and we may take
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
in Theorem
7.1.8
to be a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
, where
\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})=-[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{M})
.

Proof.

Recall that
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})=(H^{\matheur{M}},W_{\bullet}H^{\matheur{M}},\mathfrak{q}{M})
is a pure
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink structure of weight
-w(\matheur{M})
, and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{q}{M}=\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}(\sigma\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!])

\displaystyle\supset\Hom_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!])=\Hom_{\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}}(\mathbb{M}^{\dagger},\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!])

\displaystyle=\Hom_{\matheur{A}}(H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!])=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H^{\matheur{M}}=\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}\mathfrak{p}^{\matheur{M}}.

Here
\mathbb{T}^{\dagger}
and
\mathbb{M}^{\dagger}
are introduced in Section
2.2.3
.
Then
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
, where

H^{\prime}
is a
\matheur{k}
-subspace of
H{M}
,
W_{\mu}H^{\prime}=W_{\mu}H^{\matheur{M}}\cap H^{\prime}
, and

\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{q}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H^{\prime}\supset\mathfrak{p}{M}\cap\mathbb{C}_{\infty}(\!(t-\theta)\!)\otimes{k}H^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes{k}H^{\prime}=\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}.

Thus
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
is pure of weight
-w(\matheur{M})
and effective.
By Theorem
7.1.8
, there exists a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
together with an essentially surjective morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
such that
f_{\mathrm{HP}}:\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\cong\matheur{H}^{\prime}
.
As
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
has full-CM by
\matheur{K}
, one has that
\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})=\dim_{\matheur{k}}(\matheur{H}^{\prime})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
, and

\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}^{\prime})\cong\matheur{k}\otimes_{\matheur{A}}\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M}^{\prime}).

In particular, the intersection of
\matheur{K}
and
\End_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t,\sigma]}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
via the above embedding is an
\matheur{A}
-order
\mathcal{O}
in
\matheur{K}
.
Let
O{K}
be the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{K}
.
Replacing
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
by
O{K}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}
if necessary, we may assume that
\mathcal{O}=O{K}
.

From the isomorphism
f_{\mathrm{HP}}:\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\cong\matheur{H}^{\prime}
, we have that

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[\![t-\theta]\!]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}[t]}\left(\frac{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}{\sigma\matheur{M}^{\prime}}\right)\cong\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}}{\mathfrak{p}^{\matheur{M}^{\prime}}}\cong\frac{\mathfrak{q}^{\prime}}{\mathfrak{p}^{\prime}}\cong\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\frac{\hat{O}_{\matheur{K},\xi}}{\mathfrak{P}_{\xi}^{w_{\xi}^{\prime}}\hat{O}_{\matheur{K},\xi}},

where
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\xi\in J{K}}w_{\xi}^{\prime}\xi
is the Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathfrak{I}_{\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\prod_{\xi\in J{K}}\mathfrak{P}^{w_{\xi}^{\prime}}\subset O_{\mathbf{K}}
to get
\sigma\matheur{M}^{\prime}=\mathfrak{I}_{\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}\matheur{M}^{\prime}
.
By Lemma
7.2.3
and Remark
7.2.5
, we know that
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is a generalized CM type of
\matheur{K}
.
Hence by Definition
2.4.1
,
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is a CM dual
t
-motive with CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})
over
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
,
and

\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{H}^{\prime})=-[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]\cdot w(\matheur{M}).

∎

Remark 7.2.7
.

Keep the notations in Proposition
7.2.6
.
By Theorem
2.4.3
(3) we may choose
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
to be defined over
\bar{k}
.
Suppose further that
\matheur{M}
is also defined over
\bar{k}
, then by Lemma
2.2.6
the essentially surjective morphism
\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is over
\bar{k}
as well.

Definition 7.2.8
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive over
\bar{k}
, and
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink substructure of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
with full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
over
\matheur{k}
.
A dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
given in Proposition
7.2.6
is called a
CM dual
t
-motive associated to
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
.
By Theorem
2.4.3
(3), such
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is unique up to isogeny.

7.3.
The Deligne–Gross conjecture

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive defined over
\bar{k}
.
Given a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink substructure
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
, via the period isomorphism
H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\cong H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
(see (
3.1.2
)), one may identify
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H^{\prime}\subset H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
as a subspace of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
.
Suppose
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
has full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{k}^{\mathrm{sep}}
, i.e.
\dim{k}H^{\prime}=[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]
and
\matheur{K}
embeds into the endomorphism algebra
\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}^{\prime})
over
\matheur{k}
.
Note that
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
becomes a
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{v_{\theta},\matheur{k}}{\otimes}\matheur{K}
-module, and

\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\underset{v_{\theta},\matheur{k}}{\otimes}\matheur{K}\cong\prod_{\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})}\mathbb{C}_{\infty}.

For each embedding
\nu\in\Emb(\matheur{K},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
,
there exists a nonzero
\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cap H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
, which is unique up to
\mathbb{C}_{\infty}^{\times}
-multiples, satisfying that

\alpha(\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})=\nu(\alpha)\cdot\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}},\quad\forall\alpha\in\matheur{K},

The main result in this section is to derive an explicit description of the periods

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}},\quad\forall 0\neq\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M}),

when
\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is algebraic (i.e.
\omega_{\nu}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})\cap H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
), in terms of two-variable gamma values when the CM field
\matheur{K}
is contained in a cyclotomic function field.

Suppose
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
where
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Let
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be a CM dual
t
-motive associated to
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
.
Then
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
has generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})
, where
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in I_{\matheur{K}}^{0}
is the Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
.
By Proposition
4.2.4
(3), there exists a function
\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}:(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A)\times(\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}
such that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)\cdot\operatorname{St}(x,y).

By Theorem
5.4.5
, we prove the following analogue of Deligne–Gross period conjecture:

Theorem 7.3.1
.

Let
\matheur{M}
be a pure uniformizable dual
t
-motive defined over
\bar{k}
.
Given a
\matheur{k}
-Hodge–Pink sub-structure
\matheur{H}^{\prime}=(H^{\prime},W_{\bullet}H^{\prime},\mathfrak{q}^{\prime})
of
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
, suppose
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
has full-CM by a CM field
\matheur{K}
, where
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
For each
\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
, put
\nu_{\varrho}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\nu_{1}\circ\varrho\big|{K}:\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}_{\infty}
.
Identifying
H^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\mathbb{C}_{\infty}\otimes_{\matheur{k}}H^{\prime}\subset H^{\matheur{M}}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
with a subspace of
H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})
via the period isomorphism in (
3.1.2
), let
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\mathbb{C}_{\infty})\cap\matheur{H}^{\prime}_{\mathbb{C}_{\infty}}
be a nonzero differential satisfying

\alpha(\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})=\nu_{\varrho}(\alpha)\cdot\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}},\quad\forall\alpha\in\matheur{K}.

Take

\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}:(\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A)\times(\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z})\rightarrow\mathbb{Q}

such that

\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)\cdot\operatorname{St}(x,y)\quad\in\mathscr{S}(\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}),

where
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is the Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
, see Definition
7.2.2
.
Suppose that
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is algebraic, i.e.
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M},\bar{k})
.
For
\gamma\in\matheur{H}_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
with
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\neq 0
,
we then have that

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)^{\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(\varrho^{-1}\star x,\varrho^{-1}\star y))}.

Proof.

Let
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
be a CM dual
t
-motive associated to
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
, and
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
the essentially surjective homomorphism over
\bar{k}
such that
f_{\mathrm{HP}}:\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})\cong\matheur{H}^{\prime}\subset\matheur{H}(\matheur{M})
as in Proposition
7.2.6
.
Let
\xi_{\varrho}\in J{K}
be the point so that
\nu_{\xi_{\varrho}}=\nu_{\varrho}
.
Let
\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{\varrho}}\in H_{\mathrm{dR}}(\matheur{M}^{\prime},\bar{k})
be the differential associated with
\xi_{\varrho}
.
Up to a
\bar{k}^{\times}
-multiple, we then have
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}=f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{\varrho}}
.
Note that
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
has generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})
.
Hence for each
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
such that
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is nonzero, we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\sim\int_{\gamma}f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{\varrho}}\sim\int_{f_{*}\gamma}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{\varrho}}

\displaystyle\sim p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi_{\varrho},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})\sim p_{\matheur{K}}(\xi_{1},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}^{\varrho^{-1}})\sim\wp^{\operatorname{cyc}}_{\nu_{1}}\big(\varrho\cdot\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}\big)\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}(\varrho\star x,\varrho\star y)\big)^{\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)}

\displaystyle\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\wp_{\nu_{1}}^{\operatorname{cyc}}\big(\operatorname{St}(x,y)\big)^{\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(\varrho^{-1}\star x,\varrho^{-1}\star y)}\sim\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(x,y)^{\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(\varrho^{-1}\star x,\varrho^{-1}\star y)},

where the last equivalence is from Theorem
5.4.5
(and Remark
5.4.6
).
∎

Remark 7.3.2
.

As
\matheur{M}^{\prime}
is defined over
\bar{k}
and
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
is isomorphic to
\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}^{\prime})
via the essentially surjective morphism
f:\matheur{M}\rightarrow\matheur{M}^{\prime}
over
\bar{k}
, we are always able to choose the differential
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
to be algebraic for every
\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
by taking
\omega_{\varrho}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}=f^{*}\omega_{\matheur{M}^{\prime},\xi_{\varrho}}
as in the proof above.

Remark 7.3.3
.

Keeping the notation in Theorem
7.3.1
,
write the Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{\prime}
as
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot\xi_{\varrho}\in I{k}^{0}
.
Although the choice of the function
\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
might not be unique, we can follow Theorem
6.3.7
and take
\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
to be the following explicit one: for
x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A
and
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}
, write
x=\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)}\bmod A
where
a\in\matheur{A}
and
\mathfrak{c}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
with
\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}
and
\text{gcd}(a,\mathfrak{c})=1
, put

\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\begin{cases}\displaystyle\frac{(1-q)\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}+\frac{m_{\varrho}n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]},&\text{ if $\mathfrak{c}=1$ and $y=\displaystyle\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}\bmod\mathbb{Z}$ for $0\leq i<\ell$,}\\
\displaystyle\frac{m_{\varrho}n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]},&\text{ if $\mathfrak{c}\neq 1$ and $y=\displaystyle\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}}\bmod\mathbb{Z}$ for $0\leq i<\ell$,}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Here
n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)
is defined in (
6.3.4
).
As in (
6.3.8
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\frac{1}{\mathfrak{n}(\theta)}A/A\\
y\in\frac{1}{q^{\ell}-1}\mathbb{Z}/\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\varepsilon^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}(x,y)\operatorname{St}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\matheur{G}{K}}+\sum_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\left(\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}m_{\varrho}\cdot n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho,a,i)\right)\cdot\operatorname{St}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})

\displaystyle=\varphi_{\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}}.

Hence given
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
, we obtain that

\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho_{0}}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{\prime}})}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]}}\cdot\prod_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0}^{-1},a,i)}\right)^{\frac{m_{\varrho}}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}

whenever
\gamma\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}(\matheur{M})
such that
\int_{\gamma}\omega_{\varrho_{0}}^{\matheur{H}^{\prime}}
is nonzero.

Example 7.3.4
.

Let
E_{\rho}
be an abelian
t
-module over
\bar{k}
which is pure and uniformizable (i.e. the Hartl–Juschka dual
t
-motive
\matheur{M}(\rho)
is pure and uniformizable).
Put
\matheur{H}(E_{\rho})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\matheur{H}(\matheur{M}(\rho))
, and for each
N\in\mathbb{N}
we define the
N
-th Hodge–Pink structure of
E_{\rho}
by

\matheur{H}^{N}(E_{\rho})\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigwedge^{N}_{\matheur{k}}\matheur{H}(E_{\rho})=\matheur{H}\Big(\bigwedge^{N}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\Big).

Let
d
be the dimension of
E_{\rho}
and
r
be the rank of
E_{\rho}
.
Suppose further that
E_{\rho}
has “complex multiplication” by
O{K}
, where
\matheur{K}
is a CM field over
\matheur{k}
with
[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]=n
and
O{K}
is the integral closure of
\matheur{A}
in
\matheur{k}
.
This means that there exists an
\matheur{A}
-algebra homomorphism from
O{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\text{ab.~$t$-mod.}}(E_{\rho})
,
which corresponds to a
\matheur{k}
-algebra embedding
\matheur{K}\hookrightarrow\End_{\mathrm{HP}}(\matheur{H}(E_{\rho}))
.
In particular, one has that
n\mid r
.
Put
d_{0}\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=r/n
.
We may identify

\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)\mathrel{\vbox{\hbox{\scriptsize.}\hbox{\scriptsize.}}}=\bigwedge^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}\matheur{H}(E_{\rho})=\matheur{H}\Big(\bigwedge^{d_{0}}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}(\rho)\Big)\hookrightarrow\matheur{H}\Big(\bigwedge^{d_{0}}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\Big)=\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}(E_{\rho}).

In particular, the Hodge–Pink structure
\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)
is pure of weight
d_{0}\cdot\frac{d}{r}=\frac{d}{n}
and has full-CM by
\matheur{K}
.
This is equivalent to saying that
\bigwedge^{d_{0}}_{O_{\mathbf{K}}}\matheur{M}(\rho)
is a CM dual
t
-motive with generalized CM type
(\matheur{K},\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)})
, where
\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}
is the Hodge–Pink type of
\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)
with

\wt(\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)})=\frac{d}{n}\cdot[\matheur{K}:\matheur{K}^{+}]=\frac{d}{[\matheur{K}^{+}:\matheur{k}]}.

Now, suppose
\matheur{K}\subset\matheur{K}_{\mathfrak{n},\ell}
for
\mathfrak{n}\in\matheur{A}_{+}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.
Write

\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}=\sum_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}_{\matheur{K}}}m_{\varrho}\xi_{\varrho}\quad\in I{K}^{0}.

Then by Remark
7.3.3
we have that for
\varrho_{0}\in\matheur{G}{K}
,

\displaystyle\int_{\gamma_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\gamma_{d_{0}}}\omega_{\varrho_{0}}^{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}

\displaystyle\sim\tilde{\pi}^{\frac{d}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}\cdot\prod_{\varrho\in\matheur{G}{K}}\left(\prod_{\mathfrak{c}\mid\mathfrak{n}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\in(\matheur{A}/\mathfrak{c})^{\times}\\
i\in\mathbb{Z}/\ell\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Gamma}(\frac{a(\theta)}{\mathfrak{c}(\theta)},\frac{q^{i}}{1-q^{\ell}})^{n_{\mathfrak{c}}(\varrho\varrho_{0}^{-1},a,i)}\right)^{\frac{m_{\varrho}}{[\matheur{K}:\matheur{k}]}}

for

\gamma_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\gamma_{d_{0}}\in H_{\mathrm{Betti}}\big(\bigwedge^{d_{0}}_{\bar{k}[t]}\matheur{M}(\rho)\big)
so that the period integral on the left hand side is nonzero.
In particular, when
\matheur{K}
is imaginary and quadratic, we may write
\matheur{G}{K}=\{\varrho_{1}=\mathrm{id}{K},\varrho_{2}\}
, and

\Phi_{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}=m_{1}\xi_{\varrho_{1}}+m_{2}\xi_{\varrho_{2}}\quad\text{ with }\quad m_{1}+m_{2}=d\quad(\text{as $\matheur{K}^{+}=\matheur{k}$}).

Then we get the following analogue of the Gross formula in
[
Gr78
, (4),(5) in p. 194]
:

\int_{\gamma_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\gamma_{d_{0}}}\omega_{\varrho_{1}}^{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}\sim(\varpi{K}^{+})^{m_{1}}\cdot(\varpi_{\matheur{K}}^{-})^{m_{2}}\sim(\varpi{K}^{+})^{m_{1}}\cdot\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}}{\varpi{K}^{+}}\right)^{m_{2}},

and

\int_{\gamma_{1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\gamma_{d_{0}}}\omega_{\varrho_{2}}^{\matheur{H}^{d_{0}}_{\matheur{K}}(\rho)}\sim(\varpi{K}^{+})^{m_{2}}\cdot(\varpi_{\matheur{K}}^{-})^{m_{1}}\sim(\varpi{K}^{+})^{m_{2}}\cdot\left(\frac{\tilde{\pi}}{\varpi{K}^{+}}\right)^{m_{1}},

where
\varpi{K}^{\pm}
are given in (
6.3.12
).
